**Just closed:** M51 — Live Re-Theme for the Declarative Surface. User-directed: "Scope M51," following M50's own writeup renumbering this as the next roadmap item once the theme backend (color + shape/elevation) was complete across the whole MD3 catalog. Scoped via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`). **Real investigation before designing confirmed this milestone is a genuinely small, well-bounded addition, not a new subsystem:** `crates/engine-spec/src/reconcile.rs`'s `Reconciler` already retains the full parsed `WidgetSpec` (`self.spec`) and every widget `id`'s `NodeId` (`self.ids`) for the *lifetime* of a `View`, purely to support hot-reload; `crates/engine-spec/src/build.rs`'s `patch_node` already does exactly "recompute this node's `PaintProperties`/`layout_style` from its spec + the active theme layers, overwrite in place, leave `id`/`parent`/`children`/`access`/`interaction` untouched" -- the *entire* mechanism a live re-theme needs for one node, already built, already tested, already the real engine behind every existing hot-reload. `reconcile_node` only *skips* `patch_node` when the spec is unchanged (a real fast path for the common hot-reload case, where most nodes usually didn't change) -- live re-theme is the opposite case: the spec is always unchanged, but the *theme* changed, so `patch_node` needs to run unconditionally for every node instead. **Explicitly investigated and deliberately not attempted, named not silent:** live re-theme for `Window`'s own imperative catalog -- `Window.add_button`/etc. retain no spec at all once built, so there is nothing analogous to unconditionally re-run; a real fix would need a new per-node "how was this constructed" theme-derivation tracking mechanism, a genuinely separate, large piece of work -- the same "live token-linkage" concept flagged as the highest-risk/highest-value piece of the whole customization request since the very first investigation in this session, long before M49 existed. `Window.set_theme`'s own existing narrow 4-field color re-tint (`Tree::set_all_interaction_tints`/`set_all_component_tints`) is unchanged by this milestone.

### What shipped, single phase
1. `resolve_theme_layers` (`crates/engine-py/src/view.rs`) -- `View::new`'s own inline ~35-line theme-resolution block (parses `default_theme`/`custom_theme` YAML or the shipped default, converts `styles:` to a `Stylesheet`, resolves seed precedence, applies `colors:` overrides) factored into a shared private helper once a genuine second real call site (`View.set_theme`, below) needed the identical logic -- the same "two real call sites justify factoring out" precedent `resolve_button_colors`/`fill_scrollbar_thumb` already established in earlier milestones. `View::new` itself is behavior-identical, confirmed by the full pre-existing `test_theme.py` suite re-passing unmodified.
2. `Reconciler::retheme` (`crates/engine-spec/src/reconcile.rs`) -- `pub fn retheme(&self, tree, default_theme, custom_theme, sheet, scheme, base_dir) -> Result<(), SpecError>`, walking `self.spec` via a new private `retheme_node` recursive helper (mirroring `record_ids`'s own recursive-walk shape) and calling `patch_node` *unconditionally* for every node via `self.ids[spec.id]` -- no `node_props_equal` check at all, since the spec is guaranteed unchanged (a theme change, not a content change). Zero changes needed to `patch_node`/`build_tree` themselves. `&self`, not `&mut self` -- reads `self.spec`/`self.ids`, only mutates the passed-in `Tree`. 2 new unit tests, GIL-free: a `corner_radius` change from a new theme layer is applied to every matching node; a node with its own inline style is untouched (inline still wins over the new theme, exactly like at construction time).
3. `View.set_theme` (`crates/engine-py/src/view.rs`) -- `#[pyo3(signature = (default_theme=None, custom_theme=None, theme_seed=None, dark=false))]`, calling `resolve_theme_layers` then `self.reconciler.retheme(...)`, then updating `self.default_theme`/`self.custom_theme`/`self.scheme` so a *later* `poll_reload()` continues resolving against the newly-installed theme rather than silently reverting to whatever `View::new` originally used. **Real, deliberate convention, matching `Window.set_theme`'s own already-shipped precedent, not invented fresh here:** each call is a complete, fresh theme selection -- omitting `default_theme`/`custom_theme` resets to the shipped default / no custom override, not "keep whatever the previous call used." A caller who only wants to change the seed re-passes the same theme path(s) it already has in hand.

**Verification:** 7 new pytest tests (`tests/test_theme.py`): `set_theme(custom_theme=...)` changes an already-built node's `corner_radius` live, read back via the exact same `Node` object the view returned before the call (proving in-place patching, not a rebuild); a widget's own inline style still wins over the new theme; omitting `default_theme`/`custom_theme` on a later call resets to the shipped default rather than being a silent no-op; `set_theme(theme_seed=...)` changes a declarative `background: primary` token without raising (the same honest "no Python-facing color getter" limit every other color test in this suite already states); node ids survive a retheme (both a pre-held `Node` and a fresh lookup read the same live value); a `poll_reload()` called *after* `set_theme()` continues resolving against the newly-installed theme, not the original one; and a **real, verified-not-assumed finding**, caught by actually running the test rather than by inspection: a `{{ }}`-bound property does *not* survive a `retheme()` call -- `patch_node` only recomputes the static style cascade (the identical, pre-existing behavior any content-only `poll_reload` already has today, not a new limitation this milestone introduces), so a bound field reverts to its spec's own static value, proven here as "reverts sanely to a real value," not "crashes" or "goes stale." `python/tre/_core.pyi` updated (`View.set_theme` stub, matching `Window.set_theme`'s own doc-comment conventions). Extended `examples/theme_customization.py` + a new `theme_customization_retheme.yaml` fixture: after the window is already showing, `view.set_theme(custom_theme=...)` swaps in a second custom theme live, and `theme_checkbox` (no stylesheet rule or inline override of its own) picks up the new `corner_radius: 18` on the exact same, already-built node, while `stylesheet_checkbox`/`inline_checkbox` are unaffected -- the app's own stylesheet and each widget's own inline style still win, exactly like at construction time. Full chain green: `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`/`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo test --workspace --release` (`engine-spec` 65, up from 63, +2; every other suite unchanged -- no `engine-core`/`engine-render`/`engine-md3` logic touched at all); `maturin develop --release`; `pytest tests/` (709 passed, up from 702, +7, 1 skipped unchanged); all 84 examples (zero new files, one extended, zero failures); `demo/showcase.py` (all 5 phases, exit 0).

**M51 -- Live Re-Theme for the Declarative Surface -- is now fully complete, single phase.** Completes the "theme as a YAML file" roadmap for the declarative `View` surface end to end: construction-time resolution (M49/M50) plus genuine live re-resolution (M51), all through the same `default_theme`/`custom_theme`/`theme_seed` parameters. Live re-theme for `Window`'s own imperative catalog remains explicitly out of scope, named below -- the "live token-linkage" mechanism it would need is real, separate, large follow-up work, not yet scoped.

**Explicitly out of scope for this milestone, named not silent:** live re-theme for `Window`'s imperative MD3 catalog (`add_button`/etc.) -- would need a new per-node "how was this constructed" theme-derivation tracking mechanism, confirmed genuinely necessary and genuinely large by this milestone's own investigation; `Window.set_theme`'s own existing narrow 4-field color re-tint is unchanged. Re-applying `{{ }}` bindings after a `retheme()` call -- `patch_node` recomputes only the static cascade, identical to any content-only `poll_reload` today; verified, not silently assumed safe, by a dedicated test.

**Just closed:** M50 — Theme-Driven Shape & Elevation for the Imperative MD3 Catalog. User-directed: "Scope the corner-radius/elevation to use the new theme pattern. I would like the theme backend complete before re-theming everything else" -- explicitly reprioritizing this ahead of M51/live re-theme, which M49 had originally scoped as the next roadmap item. Scoped via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`), grounded in a dedicated Explore agent's exhaustive component-by-component audit of all 56 `add_*` factories (`window_factory.rs`, 7,581 lines) before any design was locked in: **28 of 56 have a real, themeable corner radius; 14 of those also have a real, themeable elevation; the other 28 have neither** (custom-painted `NodeKind`s with no `Rect` shape at all, or deliberately flat/square MD3 rows). Only `add_fab` (size) and `add_toolbar` (docked/floating) genuinely pick between multiple corner-radius values by variant; everywhere else a fixed value applies regardless. **What shipped, five phases:** (1) `ThemeSpec` gained a `components:` section (`ComponentOverride { corner_radius, elevation }`, `engine-spec/src/theme.rs`) -- a deliberately separate namespace from `styles:` (which only ever reaches the 7 declarative kinds), keyed by `"<component>"` (applies to every variant) or `"<component>.<variant>"` (overrides just one); `ThemeState` gained matching storage and a real 2-tier, **per-field** lookup (`shape`/`elevation`, `window.rs`) -- a variant-specific entry that sets only `elevation` must not shadow the bare key's own `corner_radius`, a real bug caught and fixed by a dedicated unit test before any factory used it. (2) Wired the 8 Buttons & FAB factories -- **a real, load-bearing bug caught and fixed before it shipped, not by inspection alone:** `add_split_button`/`add_button_group`'s own hover/press shape-morph code used to recompute `height / 2.0` as a fresh, independent literal for their `interactive_shape`/`press_interactive_shape` pairs, completely bypassing whatever `add_button` itself had just resolved for `paint.corner_radius` -- a themed button's own painted *shape* would have silently disagreed with its own *corner_radius* field. Fixed by resolving the theme override once and reusing the identical value for both, not recomputing it a second, independent way. (3) Wired the 10 Containers & Surfaces factories. (4) Wired the final 10 Navigation/data-entry/misc factories -- across phases 2-4, every factory that only *coincidentally* shares a Rust `const` with a different, unrelated factory (`add_search_view` reusing `DIALOG_CORNER_RADIUS`, `add_time_input_field`/`add_period_selector`/`add_spin_box` reusing `CHIP_CORNER_RADIUS`, `add_graph_node` reusing `CARD_CORNER_RADIUS`, `add_navigation_drawer` reusing `SIDE_SHEET_CORNER_RADIUS`) still gets its own distinct theme key -- decoupling "shares a number today" from "should always share an override" -- while sub-elements that are *structurally* the same real component (`add_top_app_bar`/`add_spin_box`'s own icon buttons) deliberately reuse `"icon_button"` rather than inventing a redundant key. (5) Populated the shipped `default_theme.yaml`'s new `components:` section with every safe, fixed default value, copied byte-for-byte from `window_factory.rs`'s own real `const`s (verified by direct read, not recalled) -- **a real correctness constraint identified and honored, not glossed over:** components whose real default is a *formula over a caller-supplied dimension* (`add_button`/`add_icon_button`/`add_segmented_button`/`add_toolbar`/`add_button_group`'s own tightened shape) were deliberately given **no** shipped entry at all, since baking in one fixed number would silently override their real "scales with height/size" behavior for every call that doesn't happen to match whatever number was chosen -- a real behavior change, not a neutral default; omitting them is what correctly preserves the existing formula. **A real completeness gap found and closed while populating the shipped file, not originally scoped:** `Window.set_theme` had no way to auto-load *any* default theme at all (only `custom_theme`) -- meaning the newly-populated `components:` section would have been dead data for the entire imperative catalog, silently unreachable. Fixed by giving `Window.set_theme` its own `default_theme` parameter, mirroring `View.__new__`'s identical convention (omit for the shipped default; a real path replaces it entirely) -- deliberately scoped to `components:` only, never `colors:`/`seed:`, since `Window`'s color/seed story is already fully served by the required `seed` argument plus `custom_theme`'s own override, and letting a second theme file quietly compete with a required argument would be a real, confusing ambiguity. **Verification:** 5 new `engine-py` unit tests (`window.rs`, GIL-free `ThemeState` logic) proving the 2-tier per-field lookup directly, including the shadowing bug above; 40 new pytest tests (`tests/test_theme.py`) across all 28 wired factories plus the new `Window.set_theme(default_theme=)` -- variant-specific overrides, bare-key-applies-to-every-variant, cross-factory key isolation (an "icon_button" override must never leak into plain "button" and vice versa), unthemed defaults preserved exactly, and the shipped-default/custom-theme merge (custom wins on overlapping keys). Two components (`add_segmented_button`, `add_dialog`'s own panel) have no Python-facing readback for their real override at all (`corner_radii_override`, or a child node never returned to Python) -- proven by *not raising* instead, the same honest limit this suite already established for comparable cases in M48/M49. Extended `examples/theme_customization.py` + its own custom-theme YAML fixture with a real `components: {button.filled: {corner_radius: 4, elevation: 2}}` entry, asserted directly on a real `add_button` node through the identical `custom_theme` parameter M49 already built -- no new Python API needed for the override itself, only for the new `default_theme` completeness fix. `python/tre/_core.pyi` updated (`Window.set_theme`'s widened signature and docstring, `View.__init__`'s docstring noting `components:` is silently unused there). Full chain green at every phase boundary: `cargo check`/`clippy -D warnings`/`fmt` clean, `cargo test --workspace --release` (`engine-py` 20, up from 11, +9: 5 `ThemeState` lookup tests this milestone plus 4 already-existing from earlier work; every other Rust suite unchanged -- no `engine-core`/`engine-render` logic touched), `maturin develop --release`, `pytest tests/` (702 passed, up from 663, +39, 1 skipped unchanged), all 84 examples (zero new files, one extended), showcase demo -- re-run in full after every phase, including after populating the shipped defaults, to confirm zero behavior change for every pre-existing example/test that never sets a `components:` override (the real, load-bearing claim the whole milestone's own safety rested on). See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M49 — Theme as a YAML File: Color Overrides + Per-Kind Default Styles. User-directed: "Yes scope M49," following M48's own writeup naming M49 as the next roadmap piece. Scoped via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`). **Real investigation that materially reshaped the scope, via a dedicated Explore agent:** the open question was whether "theme YAML" could reach the *real* MD3 component catalog (`Window.add_button`/`add_fab`/etc., `window_factory.rs`, confirmed 7,581 lines, 56 `add_*` factories) or only the narrow declarative `kind:` surface (7 node kinds). Corner-radius/elevation turned out to be a dead end for this milestone -- every one is a Rust `const` or a required constructor argument, never theme-driven, for any of the 56 factories; migrating them all to a theme-driven shape/elevation scale is real, explicitly out-of-scope follow-up. **Color was not a dead end -- it already flowed through one shared choke point, for both paths:** `window_factory.rs`'s `resolve_*_colors` helpers all resolve roles via `ThemeState::role` -> `ColorScheme::role` (`window.rs`/`color.rs`), and the declarative path (`engine_spec::build::resolve_color`) resolves MD3 tokens against a `ColorScheme` too -- the *same* underlying `role()` lookup. A single new override layer, applied once wherever a `ColorScheme` is selected, reaches the entire real component catalog for free, not just the 7 declarative kinds -- a materially better outcome than originally assumed. **What shipped, four phases:** (1) `ColorScheme::set_role`/`apply_overrides` (`engine-md3/src/color.rs`) -- a mirrored setter of the existing `role()` getter plus a real override-applying method, parsing hex/CSS strings via the same parser used elsewhere; wired into `Window.set_theme`'s new `custom_theme` param and `View::new`'s new `default_theme`/`custom_theme` params, both applying overrides to whichever scheme gets selected -- every existing `resolve_*_colors` call site needed zero changes. (2) `StyleSpec` gained `elevation: Option<f32>` (a real parity gap -- `PaintProperties.elevation` existed since day one but was never exposed to YAML); new `engine_md3::shape` module centralizing MD3's real published shape/elevation scale as real, tested constants for the first time, replacing ~40 scattered doc-comment-only mentions across `window_factory.rs` -- data only this milestone, not yet consumed by those call sites. (3) The real cascade mechanism: `cascade.rs`'s `resolve_style` split into `resolve_style_within_sheet` (unchanged external behavior) plus a new `resolve_style_layered(spec, default_theme, custom_theme, sheet)` that resolves *each* layer through its own independent cascade before merging the three layer results in source-priority order, inline last -- the real, deliberate design matching the user's own stated mental model exactly (each tier entirely supersedes the tier below it, regardless of any tier's own internal selector specificity), *not* the same thing as concatenating all three sheets' rules into one combined list (which would let a default theme's `id:` rule beat an app's own `baseline` stylesheet rule, backwards from what was described). `build_tree`/`patch_node`/`load_styled_view`/`Reconciler::load`/`Reconciler::reconcile` all threaded the two new optional layers through, mirroring exactly how `sheet` was already threaded. (4) `View.__new__` gained `default_theme`/`custom_theme` (YAML file paths); omitting `default_theme` loads the engine's own shipped default (`crates/engine-py/assets/default_theme.yaml`, embedded at compile time via `include_str!`, giving real per-kind corner-radius defaults for the 7 declarative kinds) -- the user's own stated "a yaml style file loaded as the default." Seed precedence: an explicit `theme_seed` argument always wins; otherwise `custom_theme`'s own `seed:`; otherwise `default_theme`'s. `Window.set_theme` gained `custom_theme` with the opposite-direction precedence for its own *required* `seed` argument (the theme file's seed wins when present, since there's no way to omit a required argument to signal "defer to the file" the way `View`'s optional `theme_seed` can) -- explained directly in the doc comment rather than left as an unexplained asymmetry. **Verification:** 15 new pytest tests (`tests/test_theme.py`) -- the shipped default applying a real corner_radius, a custom `default_theme` path replacing it entirely, custom-theme styles superseding the default, a widget-wide stylesheet superseding both theme layers, all four tiers together resolving in the user's own stated order, color overrides reaching a declarative token without raising, unknown-role/invalid-color errors naming the offending value, both seed-precedence directions, `Window.set_theme` reaching a real `add_button`, and `poll_reload` re-resolving against the *same* theme after a hot-reload (not silently falling back to the shipped default -- the real reason `Reconciler`/`View` thread the layers through explicitly, not just at construction time) -- plus 5 new `engine-md3` unit tests (`set_role`/`apply_overrides`: known-role override, unknown-role rejection, unparseable-color rejection, other-roles-untouched) and 6 new `cascade.rs` unit tests (`resolve_style_layered`: layer-supersedes-specificity, custom-beats-default, inline-still-wins, unset-field-falls-through-to-default, no-layers-falls-back-to-inline) plus 3 new `theme.rs` parse tests and 2 new `shape.rs` scale-ordering tests. New live example `examples/theme_customization.py` + 3 YAML fixtures: proves all four real tiers on three checkboxes, plus a custom theme's color override reaching both a declarative `background: primary` `Rect` and a real imperative `add_button`, through a genuine 60-frame `App.run()`. `python/tre/_core.pyi` updated (`View.__new__`, `Window.set_theme`). Full chain green: `cargo check`/`clippy -D warnings`/`fmt` clean, `cargo test --workspace --release` (`engine-md3` 19 up from 14 +5, `engine-spec` 60 up from 52 +8, every other suite unchanged), `maturin develop --release`, `pytest tests/` (663 passed, up from 648, +15, 1 skipped unchanged), all 84 examples (+1), showcase demo. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M48 — General Live Property Exposure: Layout Mutation + Border. User-directed: "I want all properties usable and exposed for a users use. Customization is a key function of any GUI framework. Even changing the MD3 theme should be customizable by a user as the MD3 specification is just the default setting." A direct factual audit ("Do we have bindable properties for all renderable nodes for yaml and python?") first surfaced the real gaps this milestone closes; scoped via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`), which also surfaced a much larger follow-on roadmap (M49 theme-as-YAML cascade, M50 live re-theme), deliberately deferred and only sketched at the mechanism level -- this milestone is the first, concrete, ready-to-implement piece. **Real gaps confirmed by direct investigation, not assumed:** no live resize/reposition API existed anywhere -- `width`/`height`/`padding`/`gap` were fixed at construction; the one real primitive that could serve this, `Tree::set_layout_style` (already general -- any `NodeId`, arbitrary `taffy::Style`), had exactly one narrow Python-facing caller (`Window.resize_terminal`, size-only). `border_color`/`border_width` (real `PaintProperties` fields since M30 Phase 1) were unreachable from `animate()`, YAML bindings, `StyleSpec`, and every widget constructor. **What shipped:** a new `Node.set_layout(width=None, height=None, padding=None, gap=None)` generalizing `resize_terminal`'s own exact real pattern (clone the current `layout_style`, patch only the fields passed, push back through `Tree::set_layout_style`) -- immediate, not eased, since layout fields aren't `Animated<T>`; two new `animate()`/`get()` arms for `border_color`/`border_width`, mirroring `background`/`corner_radius` exactly; `StyleSpec` widened with `border_width`/`border_color` (parsed the same hex/CSS/MD3-token way `background` already is), wired into `node_kind_and_paint` via a small internal split (`node_kind_and_base_paint` keeps every existing match arm byte-for-byte unchanged, with border resolved once afterward -- the same reason `corner_radius`/`opacity` are computed once outside the match rather than duplicated per arm) and into `cascade.rs`'s `merge()` so border is a real, cascadable stylesheet property, not just a static leaf value; `apply_binding_value` widened so `{{ }}` bindings reach both border (via the existing `animate()` forwarding, plus widening the `Str`-as-color guard to also match `border_color`) and the four new layout fields (routed to `Node.set_layout` directly, since they can never reach `animate()`'s own dispatch at all -- a new, small pre-`animate()` branch, the same shape `checked`/`text` already establish for their own non-numeric reasons); `Window.add_rect` (the one genuinely generic imperative shape factory -- confirmed via grep that no `add_container` exists at all) gained optional `border_color`/`border_width` construction kwargs. **Verification:** 19 new pytest tests (`tests/test_live_style.py`) covering `set_layout` (individually and combined), border via `animate()`/`get()`/construction/static YAML/bindings, and the two non-numeric-binding/invalid-color error paths -- two real test-authoring mistakes caught by actually running them, not shipped: an initial test asserted `get()` right after `animate(..., duration_ms=0)` on a plain `Window`-created node, which the existing, documented `animate()` contract ("only registers, snaps on the next tick") never guarantees without a running render loop -- fixed to match every sibling `animate()` test's own established "must not raise" convention instead of a false readback assertion; and the layout-binding type-mismatch test expected `TypeError` where the real code (matching its own `checked`/`text` sibling branches) raises `ValueError` -- fixed to match. New live example `examples/live_style.py` + `.yaml`: a real bound border (two `Signal`s) cycling through three border/color states via a dispatched click, combined with a real, live `Node.set_layout` resize on the same node, through a genuine 60-frame `App.run()`. `python/tre/_core.pyi` updated (`Node.set_layout` stub, `add_rect`'s widened signature). Full chain green: `cargo check`/`clippy -D warnings`/`fmt` clean, `cargo test --workspace --release` (all pre-existing suites unchanged, zero new Rust-level `#[test]`s needed -- no new engine-core logic, `Tree::set_layout_style` already has its own Rust coverage from M33), `maturin develop --release`, `pytest tests/` (648 passed, up from 629, +19, 1 skipped unchanged), all 83 examples (+1), showcase demo. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M47 — `VirtualList` Scrollbar Thumb. User-directed: after a full "Known gaps" audit (prompted by "M30, 35, and 36... identify any gaps that need to be looked at") found one real, still-open gap -- `VirtualList` never had a real scrollbar/visual affordance (named in M37's own trailer note, confirmed via direct source read that M38 Phase 6's own scrollbar-thumb paint code was gated to `ScrollView` only) -- the user asked to scope and build it. **Real investigation, not assumed:** read `ScrollView`'s own identical M38 Phase 6 capability directly as the concrete precedent to replicate -- `ScrollViewState::thumb_geometry`, `Tree::grabs_scroll_view_thumb`/`update_scroll_view_thumb_drag`, `engine-render::paint_scroll_view_thumb`, all sharing the real `SCROLLBAR_THICKNESS`/`SCROLLBAR_MARGIN`/`SCROLLBAR_MIN_LENGTH`/`SCROLLBAR_GRAB_SLOP` tokens already `pub` in `engine-core`. **Real, load-bearing difference from a copy-paste, found before writing code:** `VirtualList` has no single measured child to read a "content extent" from (windowed materialization, `Fixed` or `Variable`) -- reuses its own already-real `VirtualListState::total_extent()` (the same primitive `Tree::scroll_virtual_list_by`'s own clamping already uses, so wheel-scroll and thumb-drag can never disagree about it) instead of a caller-supplied parameter; and `VirtualList` is vertical-only today (`scroll_virtual_list_by`'s own signature has no horizontal variant), so the new thumb code is a real, narrower single-axis version, not a second horizontal-capable copy. See this file's own Milestone 47 section below for the full phase/step writeup.

The "Fixed gaps" convention itself -- a collapsible archive a closed gap gets *moved* into, instead of struck through in place forever -- was added to the reusable `build-tracker` skill (`generate_tracker_artifact.py`'s own parser/renderer, `BUILD_TRACKER_TEMPLATE.md`, `SKILL.md`'s format docs and maintenance workflow), not just this project's own copy, per the user's own explicit "add this to the template" request.

**Just closed:** M46 — Reentrant-Notification Guard. User-directed: M45's own `BUILD_TRACKER.md` entry named a real hazard and deliberately left it unfixed (a `Signal.get()` read of a *different* Signal before writing to it, while an outer recording scope is open, misattributes that Signal as a dependency too; combined with the outer scope's own evaluation writing back to it, this produced unbounded reentrant recursion with no error). The user asked to scope and fix it. **Real investigation before designing:** re-traced the exact original failure (kept from M45's own debugging) and confirmed the dangerous *consequence* has a narrow, well-defined shape regardless of cause -- a single Signal/Computed's own `_notify()` calling itself several frames down, never a genuinely unbounded chain of distinct objects. **Real scope decision, not silently assumed:** closing the *cause* (over-broad dependency attribution itself) would need every plain `Signal.get()` call to open its own isolated recording frame -- an invasive change to the hot path of every Signal read, for a case `untrack()` (M45) already targets as an escape hatch. This milestone fixes the *consequence* instead: a per-object reentrancy guard on `_notify()` that raises one clear, actionable error the moment the pattern occurs, for any cause, not just the originally-observed one. **A real gap found while designing the fix:** `batch()`'s own flush loop doesn't call `_notify()` at all (a deliberate M45 fix for cross-signal callback dedup) -- a guard placed only inside the ordinary immediate-notify path would never trigger during a batch flush, leaving the identical hazard reachable there uncaught. **A real, small refactor to close that gap cleanly:** `Signal`/`Computed` already had byte-for-byte identical `_subscribe`/`_unsubscribe` and near-identical `_notify` bodies (both independently fixed for the same M45 live-iteration bug) -- factored into a new shared `_Notifiable` base carrying the subscriber list, the dedup-and-snapshot `_notify(already_invoked=None)`, and the new `_notifying` reentrancy flag, so `batch()`'s flush now threads its own shared dedup set straight into the same guarded method instead of duplicating the loop a third time. **Real design question resolved:** does `Computed`/`Effect`'s own re-tracking need a *second*, matching guard on `_recompute`/`_run`? No -- both already unsubscribe from old dependencies *before* running their own `fn` and only resubscribe *after*, so a write inside `fn` can't directly re-invoke that same method through a dependency's notify (it isn't subscribed at that moment); the one guard on `_notify` was the whole gap. **Verification:** 6 new pytest tests (`tests/test_reactivity.py`): the isolated reentrancy guard itself (a bare Signal whose subscriber writes back to it) raises a clear `RuntimeError` instead of recursing; the guard resets after a caught exception (a later, unrelated write on the same Signal still works); a legitimate non-cyclic chain (Computed-of-Computed-of-Signal, no path back) does not trip it; `batch()`'s own flush path hits the identical guard, not just the immediate path; the original, real YAML-binding-based hazard now raises the same clear error instead of recursing; and the error message's own suggested remedy (`untrack()` around the offending read, not the write) is verified end to end to actually fix the hazard, not just asserted. Full pre-existing `test_reactivity.py` suite (all 17 M45 tests) re-run to confirm the `_Notifiable` refactor is a true behavior-preserving no-op. No Rust changes -- entirely within `python/tre/__init__.py`. `pytest tests/` (629 passed, up from 623, +6, 1 skipped unchanged), all 82 examples, showcase demo, all clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M45 — Richer Reactivity. User-directed: M44's own scoping named item 2 ("richer reactivity") and deliberately deferred it; the user later said simply "Start it." Scoped via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`) before implementing. **Real, load-bearing finding that corrected the plan's own earlier scoping note:** `begin_recording`/`end_recording` (`view.rs`) were NOT already exposed to Python (contrary to what M44's own scoping text assumed) -- plain private `fn`s, called only internally by `attach_bindings_and_handlers`. Widened to `#[pyfunction]`s (`_begin_recording`/`_end_recording`), the same mechanism `{{ }}` bindings already use, so `Computed`/`Effect`/`untrack` (pure Python, `python/tre/__init__.py`) can open/close a tracked evaluation scope identically. **Real prerequisite correctness fix, found by tracing an actual reachable scenario, not a hypothetical:** `RECORDING` was a single flat `Option<Vec<...>>`, not a stack -- the binding grammar already permits a zero-arg method call with real side effects, so a binding whose evaluation triggers a `Signal.set()` that synchronously fires a `Computed`'s own *nested* recording scope would silently wipe the outer binding's own already-recorded dependencies, permanently breaking its reactivity with no error. Fixed by turning `RECORDING` into a real `Vec<Vec<Py<PyAny>>>` stack (`_record_read` only ever touches the top frame); confirmed via a dedicated pytest regression test. **A real design simplification found during the same investigation:** `untrack(fn)` needs no third primitive -- calling `_begin_recording()`/`_end_recording()` around `fn()` and discarding the result already hides its reads from the frame beneath, for free. `Computed`/`Effect` duck-type against `Signal`'s own `_subscribe`/`_unsubscribe`/`.get()` shape (confirmed: `View._attach`'s real subscribe call site never type-checks its target), so a `{{ }}` binding can point straight at a `Computed` with zero Rust changes -- proven end to end. `batch()` uses a plain depth counter (nested calls flush only at the outermost exit) and a module-global pending set (this engine's whole render/dispatch loop is single-threaded, confirmed by reading `app.rs`, so `threading.local()` would be unjustified complexity); a `Computed`'s own downstream notification routes through the identical `_schedule_notify` mechanism, so it participates in batching automatically with zero `Computed`-specific batching code. **Two further real bugs caught by actually running the new example, not by inspection, on top of the ones anticipated in scoping:** (1) `batch()`'s first working version deduplicated by *Signal*, then called each pending Signal's own `._notify()` -- which still re-invoked a callback subscribed to *multiple* pending Signals once per Signal (a `Computed` depending on two Signals both written in one batch recomputed twice, not once); fixed by deduplicating at the *callback* level instead (a plain `set()` of callbacks, since bound methods compare/hash by `(__self__, __func__)` identity even though each access creates a distinct wrapper object -- confirmed directly before relying on it). (2) `Signal._notify`/`Computed._notify` iterated `self._subscribers` *live* -- a `Computed`-of-`Computed` chain means the first subscriber invoked can itself unsubscribe-then-resubscribe from that very list as part of its own recompute, silently skipping the next real subscriber and re-invoking the mutating one a second time; fixed by snapshotting the list (`list(self._subscribers)`) before iterating, in both `_notify` implementations and `batch()`'s own flush loop. **A related, real hazard found and deliberately routed around while writing the regression test for the `RECORDING` stack fix itself:** a `Signal.get()` override that reads a *different* Signal's current value (via `.get()`) before writing to it, while an outer recording scope is open, gets that different Signal misattributed as a dependency of the outer scope too (recording is ambient -- it doesn't know how deep the call stack is) -- combined with the outer binding's own evaluation writing to that same Signal, this is a genuinely separate, pathological self-referential-write hazard, not the nested-recording bug this milestone set out to fix; the regression test's own `trigger_b` deliberately avoids it (sets a value it already has in hand, never reading the target Signal first) to stay isolated to the one real thing it verifies. **Verification:** 4 pytest tests were not needed at the Rust level (the `RECORDING` stack is inherently pyo3/GIL-bound, matching this crate's own established split) -- instead, 17 new pytest tests (`tests/test_reactivity.py`): `Computed` derivation/recomputation/skip-if-unchanged/chaining/binding-composition; `Effect` construction-run/rerun/dispose/branch-dependent-tracking; `batch()` collapsing/nested-flush-timing/exception-safety/exactly-once-recompute; `untrack()` hiding a read/returning its result; and the nested-recording regression test itself. New live example `examples/reactivity.py` + `.yaml`: a `Computed`-of-`Computed` price/quantity/total/label chain, a real `Effect` log, `batch()` collapsing two related writes into exactly one recompute per click, through a genuine 30-frame `App.run()`. `python/tre/_core.pyi` updated (`_begin_recording`/`_end_recording` stubs). Full chain green: `cargo check`/`clippy -D warnings`/`fmt`, `cargo test --workspace --release` (`engine-py` 15 unchanged -- no new Rust-level `#[test]`s needed), `maturin develop --release`, `pytest tests/` (623 passed, up from 606, +17, 1 skipped unchanged), all 82 examples (+1), showcase demo. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M44 — Widening Live-Bindable Properties. User-directed: after M43 closed, asked "what do you recommend we look at next?", then "scope both 1 and 2, let's start with 1" -- scoped via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`) covering both widening live-bindable properties (this milestone) and richer reactivity (`Computed`/`Effect`/`batch()`/`untrack()`, named and deferred, not built). **Real bug found by direct investigation, not assumed:** `apply_binding_value` (`view.rs`), the function every `{{ }}` binding's resolved value passes through, dispatched purely on the resolved `engine_spec::Value`'s own *runtime type* -- `Bool` unconditionally routed to `set_checked`, `Str` unconditionally to `set_text`, regardless of what `property` the binding actually declared. A `background:` binding resolving to a hex-string `Signal` was silently misrouted into `set_text` (which raised on a non-text widget like `Rect`, just via the wrong error path); any binding resolving to a non-primitive Python value (`Value::Handle`, e.g. an `(r,g,b,a)` tuple) was rejected outright even though `Node.animate` already accepts that exact shape imperatively (confirmed by direct read: `animate()` already supports `background`/`transform`/`shape` as composite values, and every numeric property beyond opacity/corner_radius -- `rotation`/`check_progress`/`thumb_position`/`select_progress`/`toggle_progress`/`elevation` -- was *already* reachable via bindings before this milestone, just never stated or tested as such). **The fix:** dispatch is now property-name-first. `checked`/`text` stay the two non-`animate()` special cases, now gated by `property` (a binding declared on `checked` that resolves to the wrong shape is a real, specific type-mismatch error instead of silently accepting whatever value happened to be a `Bool`). Every other property forwards to `animate()`, which already knows how to validate/extract whatever Python shape it needs; a `Str` resolved for `property == "background"` is parsed as a color via a new, pure, GIL-free `parse_background_color` helper (`peniko::color::parse_color`, the identical real parser `engine_spec::build::resolve_color` already uses for *static* YAML colors -- MD3 theme-role token strings are explicitly out of scope, since `apply_binding_value`'s own `throwaway_node` has no access to the view's real, live `ColorScheme`); a `Value::Handle` is recovered via `PyViewModelResolver::to_pyobject` (widened from private to `pub(crate)`, the same precedent `collect_bindings`/`collect_handlers` set for M43) and forwarded to `animate()` verbatim -- a side effect of this being property-name-agnostic is that `transform`/`shape` bindings are now reachable too, not just `background`. **Verification:** 4 new Rust unit tests (`parse_background_color`'s own exact-value hex/hex+alpha/CSS-named/error-message coverage -- pure, GIL-free logic, no interpreter needed) + 6 new pytest tests (`tests/test_view_binding.py`: hex-string and RGBA-tuple `background` bindings apply and re-evaluate without raising; an invalid color string raises with the bad value named; a `Bool` bound to `background` is rejected directly instead of silently misrouting through `set_checked`; `checked`/`text` give a clear, specific type-mismatch error for a wrongly-shaped binding). **Real, honest limitation, not worked around:** `tre` has no Python-facing getter for a node's currently-applied `background` color at all (`Node.get` only returns `f64`) -- the positive-path pytest coverage can only prove a binding *applies without raising* (plus that the node/Tree stays healthy afterward, via a co-bound `opacity` read), not read the resulting color back; the actual color math has its own exact-value Rust unit test instead. New live example `examples/bindable_background.py` + `.yaml`: two swatches, one bound to a hex-string `Signal`, one to an RGBA-tuple `Signal`, both changing color through a real dispatched click, rendered in a genuine 60-frame window. Full chain green: `cargo check`/`clippy -D warnings`/`fmt`, `cargo test --workspace --release` (`engine-py` 15, up from 11, +4), `maturin develop --release`, `pytest tests/` (606 passed, up from 600, +6, 1 skipped unchanged), all 81 examples (+1), showcase demo. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M43 — Embeddable Components: Multi-Instance Views with Independent ViewModels, **both phases**. User-directed: "I want to be able to embed a view into another view and have the embedded view have a separate viewModel. The essence of MVVM and single page applications," with multiple simultaneous instances required -- approved via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`) after weighing two real architectural options directly with the user. Phase 1 added a new `Component` pyclass + `View.instantiate(path, into)`, sharing the same live `Tree` as whatever they're instantiated into -- a real investigation found `engine-spec`/`engine-core` needed zero changes at all (`build_tree`/`Reconciler::load`/`Tree::add_child`/`Tree::remove` were already exactly the right, already-`pub` primitives), and a real design correction during implementation removed `Component`'s planned `click`/`hover`/`right_click` methods entirely (dispatch on an embedded node already works correctly through the owning `View`/`Window`'s existing methods, confirmed by reading `Tree::absolute_position`'s own parent-chain walk). Phase 2 added `Component.remove()` with automatic `Signal` unsubscription (a new `Signal._unsubscribe`), fixing a real panic risk a removed-but-still-subscribed component would otherwise hit. **A second real, significant bug was caught and fixed by actually running the live example, not by inspection:** `View.click`/`hover`/`right_click`/`_attach` had always taken `&mut self`, holding pyo3's own exclusive per-object borrow for their whole duration -- a handler dispatched from inside `click()` that called `view.instantiate(...)` (the real "Add" button pattern a dynamic list needs) panicked with "Already mutably borrowed"; fixed by widening all four to `&self`, matching `Window`'s own equivalent methods. 11 real pytest tests across both phases, a real live example with working "Add"/"Remove" buttons through a genuine `App.run()`. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md` for the full writeup.

**Just closed:** M43 Phase 1 — real component instantiation with an independent `ViewModel`. User-directed: "I want to be able to embed a view into another view and have the embedded view have a separate viewModel. The essence of MVVM and single page applications," with multiple simultaneous instances required. New `Component` pyclass + `View.instantiate(path, into)` share the same live `Tree` as whatever they're instantiated into -- confirmed via direct investigation that `engine-spec`/`engine-core` needed zero changes (`build_tree`/`Reconciler::load`/`Tree::add_child`/`Tree::remove` were all already public and exactly the right primitives). `View::_attach`'s own body was factored into a shared `attach_bindings_and_handlers` helper, reused by the new `Component::_attach` -- unchanged behavior, verified by the full pre-existing `_attach`-heavy pytest suite passing unmodified. Real design correction found during implementation: `Component` has no `click`/`hover`/`right_click` of its own (would silently distort its real, parent-constrained layout) -- dispatch on an embedded node goes through the owning `View`/`Window`'s existing methods instead, already fully correct with zero new dispatch code. 7 new pytest tests, a new live 3-instance example. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M42 — Live `View`: Wiring the Declarative Layer into a Real Window, **both phases**. User-directed: "I want Tesserae UI Framework to be a project built on top of TRE with its own git and GitHub and project" -- approved via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`) naming this real TRE-side gap as Part 1, ahead of bootstrapping the separate `tesserae` repo itself (Part 2, not yet started). Phase 1 gave `View` persistent `theme`/`completions`/`width`/`height` fields and a new `PyWindow::from_view(view, width, height, title)` sharing a View's own Tree/root/handlers directly into a real `Window` -- a real investigation finding (`App::run`'s own setup step already builds `WindowSetup` generically from any `PyWindow`) meant zero changes were needed to `app.rs`/`WindowRuntime` for this half. Phase 2 added `Window.show_view(view)`, switching which `View` an already-live window shows without closing/reopening it -- the user's own explicit plan-review feedback ("this allows for switching of current views without needing to bootstrap each view/viewModel"). A real, load-bearing correctness finding not covered by the original plan text (`NodeId` collisions across independent `Tree`s) meant the swapped bundle is `tree`+`root`+`handlers`+`context_menus` together, not just `(Tree, NodeId)`; two real bugs (a reentrant-borrow panic when a handler calls `show_view` on itself, and a CPython cyclic-GC double-counting bug caught by an existing regression test) were found and fixed before shipping, both via this project's own "verify, don't assume" discipline applied to its own brand-new code. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md` for the full writeup; `BUILD_TRACKER.md`'s own M42 section above has the complete real investigation/implementation/bug-fix chain.

**Just closed:** M42 Phase 1 — `PyWindow::from_view`, wiring `View` into a real, live window for the first time. User-directed: "I want Tesserae UI Framework to be a project built on top of TRE with its own git and GitHub and project" -- approved via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`) naming this real TRE-side gap as Part 1, with the user's own explicit follow-up feedback on that plan ("`app.py` should be the entry point... this allows for switching of current views without needing to bootstrap each view/viewModel") widening Phase 2's own scope, still open. `View` gained persistent `theme`/`completions`/`width`/`height` fields (replacing fresh-per-call throwaway instances) and a new `available_space()` helper switching its layout from `AvailableSpace::MaxContent` to `Definite` once shown live; `PyWindow::from_view(view, width, height, title)` shares `view`'s own `Tree`/root/handlers/theme/completions directly into a new `Window`. **Real, scope-narrowing finding:** direct read of `App::run`'s own setup step confirmed `WindowSetup` is already built generically from any `PyWindow`'s fields, so this needed zero changes to `app.rs`/`WindowRuntime` -- simpler than this milestone's own original plan-time guess. 3 new Rust unit tests (`view.rs`, no GIL needed -- `View::new`'s body touches no `Python<'_>`), 5 new pytest tests, 1 new live, non-headless example (`examples/live_view.py`, a real `Signal`-bound counter through a genuine 30-frame `App.run()`). Full chain green: `cargo test --workspace --release` (+3), `pytest tests/` (586 passed, +5, 1 skipped unchanged), all 78 examples (+1), showcase demo. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M41 — Hardening IV: Documentation Drift & Dead Code, **both phases**. User-directed: after M40 closed, asked which direction "let's move on" should take, chose "Continue hardening TRE v2." A dedicated Explore agent inventoried every real, stated v1 limit still open across the whole codebase; almost all of it turned out to be a deliberate, already-argued scope choice, not worth reopening. Two genuine, cheap doc-hygiene bugs were fixed instead: `add_code_editor`'s own doc comment still claimed no syntax highlighting/gutter/Tab-indentation, though all three were built in full at M31 and never corrected; four "Known gaps" bullets left un-struck-through after later milestones closed them. The confirmed-dead `AppHandler` trait (`engine-core/src/input.rs`, sketched at M4 Phase 1, never `impl`'d anywhere) was removed outright, with every doc comment that cited it as real corrected to name the actual mechanism that shipped (`engine-py::dispatch.rs`'s `HandlerMap`/`call_handler`). Separately, `.github/workflows/wheels.yml` (the real cross-platform packaging CI, M21 Phase 2) was re-triggered against current `main` for the first time since the `v0.2.0` tag, 22 milestones ago -- all 12 jobs (Linux/macOS/Windows × Python 3.9-3.13, plus `sdist`) passed, confirming the full dependency set added since (images, icons, the whole MD3 catalog, Terminal, several hardening passes) still builds cleanly cross-platform. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M40 — Smooth Window Resize, **both phases**. User-directed: after being asked "anything else you would recommend we add/fix before we start the Tesserae UI Framework project?", scoped and built the real fix for a resize gap M33 never touched (correctness) and this milestone's own investigation named (smoothness) -- "TRE v1 and pyCopper had some issues with window trailing behind cursor and stuttering. I believe v1 ended up implementing google chrome's method." A real, throwaway empirical probe against this session's own real KWin/Wayland compositor found the originally-scoped design (porting both sibling projects' own coarse-bucketed-swapchain technique) would have shipped a real, visibly broken crop, not smoothing -- this compositor crops an oversized `wl_surface` buffer to the window's own declared geometry by default, it does not scale it, without `wp_viewporter` (the exact fork this milestone had already, separately, deferred). A second real probe measured this machine's own actual `surface.configure()` cost at ~600µs-1ms/call (AMD Radeon 890M, RADV/Vulkan) -- modest enough that no bucketing is needed at all. **Real, corrected fix:** `GpuState::resize` now runs at most once per real frame (the existing `RedrawRequested` cadence), always at the window's true current size, instead of inline on every raw `Resized` event -- collapsing an entire live-drag burst into one real reconfigure per frame, with zero compositor-scaling assumption and no fork. Full verification chain clean, unchanged test/pytest/example counts (a pure internal render-loop refactor). **Real, honestly-stated limit:** whether this actually *feels* smoother during a real live drag can't be verified from here at all (no window-manipulation tool available to simulate one, and "feels smooth" is inherently a hands-on-the-mouse judgment) -- left for the user to try directly. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M39 Phase 5 — real, measured-first `Tree::tick_all` profiling, **closing M39 entirely (all 5 phases)**. User-directed: after being asked "are there any gaps or fixes left?", the user picked five of six real found gaps, in this explicit order: Code Editor horizontal scroll; Loading Indicator + Time Picker Dial; a shape-morphed border's own inset gap; Terminal cell text attributes; `Tree::tick_all`'s own naive whole-tree walk — full investigation/scoping/completion writeups for all five phases live in this file's own Milestone 39 section below; this entry records only the final, milestone-closing phase. A real, throwaway scratch benchmark (mirroring M34 Phase 1's own identical "scratch, not committed" methodology) measured `tick_all` at ~32µs/call for a realistic 2000-node idle tree, barely moved by adding live animations on 1% of nodes (confirming `Animated::tick`'s own per-field early return already makes the animation work itself cheap — the real cost is pure per-node walk overhead), and ~1.09ms/call at a 10x, genuinely unrealistic 20,000-node scale (confirms linear scaling, no hidden quadratic blowup, still only ~6.5% of a 16.67ms frame budget even there). **Real, honest conclusion: the numbers do not justify building the active-set optimization** — under 0.3% of frame budget at this catalog's own realistic node counts, an even smaller real cost than M34 Phase 1's own "modest, not dramatic" tessellation-caching win; a new `active: HashSet<NodeId>` mechanism would trade real, ongoing maintenance risk (a new invariant every future animation-starting call site must remember to uphold) for an imperceptible per-frame saving. Per this whole project's own "measured, not assumed" discipline, a benchmark validly concluding "no action needed" is this phase's own real, complete deliverable, not a placeholder for future work. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M38 Phase 7 — real vertical scroll+clip for Code Editor with caret-follow, **closing M38 entirely (all 7 phases)**. User-directed: "Let's knock out the known gaps" — the real investigation, scoping, and full completion writeup for all seven phases live in this file's own Milestone 38 section below; this entry records only the final, milestone-closing phase. Paused via `AskUserQuestion` since Code Editor (a plain `TextField`, fixed box, no scroll/clip at all) had no real, unambiguous path forward: composing `ScrollView` around it (the milestone's own original scoping note's preferred direction) would need real `taffy` measure-function integration, confirmed via direct grep to have zero precedent anywhere in this codebase. User chose a dedicated `TextFieldState.scroll_offset` mechanism instead, self-contained, mirroring `VirtualList`/`Carousel`'s own established "per-`NodeKind` scroll, not `ScrollView` reuse" pattern. New `Tree::scroll_text_field_caret_into_view`, called from every real cursor-moving dispatch chokepoint, uses a real, cited line-height approximation (VS Code's own default `fontSize * 1.35`, since `engine-core` has no font-shaping access to measure one exactly, §4) — a real, honest heuristic imprecision, not a correctness bug: the same `scroll_offset` value drives both the real clip and the real glyph shift at paint time, so the rendered result stays internally consistent regardless of how precisely the heuristic guessed. Real clip + scroll wired into `engine-render`'s own `NodeKind::TextField` paint arm; along the way, direct source read found `TextField`'s own box fill was *still* an uncached fresh `RoundedRect::to_path` every frame despite M38 Phase 1's own completion note claiming otherwise (a real, honest correction of a prior write-up, caught by checking actual current source rather than trusting it) — fixed to route through `GeometryCache` like every sibling `NodeKind`. `TextFieldState` lost its `Clone`/`Debug`/`PartialEq` derive once it gained a real `Animated<f64>` field (the same real constraint `ScrollViewState`/`Splitter`/`Icon` already have), which broke one real downstream `.clone()` in `engine-py::app.rs`'s own click-to-position helper — fixed by holding the borrow for the whole helper instead of escaping it via a clone. Full verification: `cargo test --workspace --release` clean (`engine-core` 196, up from 193, +3; `engine-render`'s own `text_field_paint` suite 15, up from 13, +2 real pixel-level tests); `pytest tests/` 563 passed/1 skipped, up from 562, +1; all 75 examples and the showcase demo clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M6 Phase 4 — `Tree::absolute_position` transform-awareness (§8), **closing M6 entirely (all 4 phases)**. The post-M5 code review finding: `absolute_position` never composed `PaintProperties.transform`, so `open_overlay`'s anchor placement, `splitter_geometry`'s drag math, and every `engine-py` synthetic-point entry point silently computed the wrong canvas position for a node inside a panned/zoomed `Container`. Every real caller enumerated via grep first (two in `engine-core`, six in `engine-py`) — a small, fully enumerated set where *every* caller wants the transform-aware answer, unlike `add_child`'s ~80 or `hit_test`'s hot per-frame path, so this rewrites `absolute_position` in place rather than adding a parallel checked method the way M6 Phase 1/M5 Phase 2 did. Composes the identical `parent * translate(layout.location) * own_transform` product `paint_node`/`hit_test_at` already compose, walking the chain root-to-node (the order an `Affine` composes correctly in) rather than the old bottom-up accumulation. New `engine-core` test proves a node under a real ancestor transform reports its real position; the full pre-existing suite (every overlay/splitter-drag/docking pixel test) passed unmodified, confirming the no-op-for-identity-transform claim. New pytest test proves `Window.click(node)` still finds a node after `Node.animate("transform", ...)` has moved it — would have failed before this fix. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 51, up from 50)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; full pytest suite (78 passed, up from 77, 1 skipped) and all ten examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M7 Phase 1 — real MD3 motion tokens (§7.5). `MotionCurve` had exactly one real variant, `Linear`, since M3 step 2. Verified the exact curve values against a real, authoritative source rather than recalling them — two secondary sources disagreed on "Emphasized" (one flattened it to `Standard`'s own value, a common CSS approximation), resolved by cross-checking Android's own `MotionTokens.kt` (generated directly from the official Material Design spec): `Emphasized` is a genuine two-segment compound curve joined at `(0.166666, 0.4)`, not a single 4-parameter curve. New `CubicSegment` type evaluates any cubic Bézier and solves "given x, find the eased y" via bisection on `X(t)` — the same problem every browser engine solves for CSS `cubic-bezier()`. Four new tests, including a round-trip consistency check (the same rigor `kurbo::Affine::inverse()`'s own tests use) and a real, externally-verified landmark check on `Emphasized`'s own split point — all passed on the first run. No consumers wired to a non-`Linear` curve yet (this phase is the data table itself); Phase 3 (ripple/hover/focus-ring) and Phase 5 (container transform) are what use one for real. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 55, up from 51)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; full pytest suite (78 passed, 1 skipped) unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M7 Phase 2 — elevation → real shadow rendering (§7.2). Confirmed `engine-render` cannot depend on `engine-md3` (§4's own dependency diagram has no such edge) — the shadow-geometry math lives directly in `engine-render`, matching ripple/hover's own established precedent (M4 Phase 5). Rejected a first web source's elevation values as "alpha-stage, may change" and fetched the real, authoritative source instead: Material Web's own production `_elevation.scss`, which encodes the exact per-level shadow values as a continuous piecewise-linear formula — hand-verified against all 6 documented integer levels, both shadow layers, before trusting it. The CSS blur-radius → Gaussian std-dev conversion (`std_dev = blur/2`) verified against the real W3C spec text. MD3's `shadow` color role confirmed constant black regardless of theme, so it didn't need to wait for Phase 3's dynamic-color wiring. New `elevation_shadow.rs`: elevation 0 paints nothing (full pre-existing suite passed unmodified, confirming the no-op claim); a real elevation paints real shadow pixels below the node, not just its own fill — both passed on the first run. New `examples/elevation.py`. Full `cargo test --workspace`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; full pytest suite (78 passed, 1 skipped) and all eleven examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M7 Phase 3 — dynamic color, wired for real (§7.1, completing §7.3). `InteractionState` gains a real `tint: Color` field (default real black, byte-for-byte the old hardcoded value); `engine-render`'s ripple/hover paint arm reads it instead of `Color::from_rgba8(0, 0, 0, 255)`. New `engine-py::window::ThemeState`/`SharedTheme`, shared with every `Node` a `Window` hands out the same way `handlers`/`context_menus` already are (`View`'s own 3 construction sites get a fresh, private, always-black instance instead — out of this phase's stated "Window/App-level" scope, so `View`'s behavior is unchanged). `Window.set_theme(seed, dark=False)` builds a real `DynamicTheme::from_seed` and immediately pushes the resolved "on-surface" color into every already-opted-in node via new `Tree::set_all_interaction_tints`; `Node.enable_interaction()` applies the same color immediately for a node opting in afterward. Real live OS theme switching wired end to end: new `InputEvent::ThemeChanged { dark }` (a true no-op in `Tree::dispatch`, mirroring `Scroll`'s own precedent), a new `engine-platform::translate_theme` mapping the real, verified `winit::window::Theme::{Light,Dark}` (confirmed unsupported on this dev machine's own X11/Wayland session per `winit`'s own docs — a real platform limitation, not a bug), and a new `ThemeChanged` arm in `engine-py::App::run`'s `on_input` closure that re-resolves and re-pushes the tint. New pixel test proves a real, non-black tint paints a real hue (not just a darker gray); new `examples/theme.py`. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 58 up from 55, `engine-render` +1, `engine-platform` +1)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; full pytest suite (78 passed, 1 skipped, unaffected) and all twelve examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M7 Phase 4 — shape morphing, wired for real (§7.4). Real finding: `engine_md3::ShapeKey` (M3 step 10) had no actual MD3-specific content at all — only `Interpolate`/`peniko::kurbo`, no `ColorScheme`/`DynamicTheme`. `ARCHITECTURE.md` §2's own struct sketch and `engine-core::node.rs`'s own doc comment both already named `PaintProperties.shape: Animated<ShapeKey>` as the intended landing spot — impossible while `ShapeKey` lived in `engine-md3` (§4's dependency rule). Resolved the identical way M7 Phase 1 resolved `MotionCurve`: moved `shape_morph.rs` into `engine-core` verbatim (confirmed via grep — zero outside references, no re-export needed), correcting `engine-md3`'s own now-stale `Cargo.toml` description in the process. New `ShapeKey::empty()`/`is_empty()` is the "no morph set" sentinel; `paint_node`'s `Rect`/`Splitter` fill now draws the morphed silhouette instead of the plain rounded rect when active, a true no-op otherwise. New `engine-py::Node.animate("shape", [(x,y), ...])` arm builds a closed `BezPath` and converts via `ShapeKey::from_path`, mirroring `"transform"`'s own real, narrower-shape pattern. New pixel test proves the default is unmodified and a real triangle morph paints the real silhouette (a bounding-box corner outside the triangle stays background); new `examples/shape_morph.py`. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 65 up from 58, `engine-md3` down to 4 having lost the relocated suite, `engine-render` +2)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; full pytest suite (78 passed, 1 skipped, unaffected) and all thirteen examples confirmed clean. New `engine-md3::container_transform` module implements the real 4-step "begin" choreography: captures the trigger's real bounds/appearance, captures the destination's own real targets, initializes the destination to the trigger's captured from-state via a computed non-uniform scale+translate `Affine` (verified safe against `Interpolate for Affine`'s own "any zero-shear diagonal affine stays diagonal at every `t`" contract), drives one synchronized `animate_to` set (transform/corner_radius/background/elevation), and cross-fades each container's own direct children with an immediate-vs-staggered start (`Animated::animate_to`'s own future-`start: Instant` support, already real, confirmed by reading `animation.rs` directly). A separate, explicit `teardown` detaches the trigger via the already-real `Tree::detach` — `CompletionHandle`'s own queue-drain mechanism §7.6's text names is confirmed still unwired anywhere in this codebase, a real, stated gap, not silently worked around. New `engine-py::Window.begin_container_transform`/`end_container_transform`. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-md3` 7, up from 4)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; full pytest suite (78 passed, 1 skipped) and all fourteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M7 — MD3 Visual Fidelity is now fully complete.** All 5 phases (real MD3 motion tokens, elevation → real shadow rendering, dynamic color wired for real, shape morphing wired for real, container transform) closed the milestone's own original investigation: every §7 subsystem that was "built but disconnected" is now genuinely reachable from a real Python app, through the real render pipeline.

**Just closed:** M8 Phase 1 — engine-level paint culling (§11.8). `build_tree_scene` computes a real, canvas-space "currently visible" `Rect` once (the whole viewport) and threads it into `paint_node`; `paint_node` computes each node's own real composed absolute bounding box (all four local corners transformed, not just two, so this stays correct under any future rotation-capable `Affine`) right after `composed`/`w`/`h` are known, and skips all Vello scene-encoding — shadow, fill, interaction, and every recursive call into children — the instant that box doesn't `overlap` (`peniko::kurbo::Rect::overlaps`, verified directly in kurbo's own source) the visible rect. `paint_node` is private with exactly two call sites (itself, `build_tree_scene`), so this needed no external caller updates. New `paint_culling.rs` proves the real, distinguishing behavior this adds (not just "off-screen content doesn't rasterize," which would be true anyway): a parent positioned 10,000px off-screen, whose own child has a `transform` that would otherwise bring it back onto visible canvas, never paints that child at all — its whole subtree is skipped before the child's own transform is ever composed. A second test proves a node only partially overlapping the viewport still paints its real, visible portion — a bounding-box-overlap decision, not an all-or-nothing "must be fully inside" test. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-render` +2)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean — every prior test passed unmodified, confirming the no-op claim for on-screen content. This phase touches no Python-facing API; `pytest` (78 passed, 1 skipped) and all fourteen examples confirmed clean, a pure regression check. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M8 Phase 2 — `VirtualList` real scroll offset & clipping (§11.7). Real finding: `Tree::tick_all` only ever ticks `node.paint`/`node.interaction` — kind-specific `Animated<T>` fields (`SplitterState.position` is the existing precedent) are driven by their own dedicated mechanism, not the central tick, so new `VirtualListState.scroll_offset: Animated<f64>` follows the identical shape: a plain value set directly, not eased by default. Real finding: `vello_hybrid::Scene::push_layer`'s own `clip_path` is baked into absolute/device-space strips at the moment it's called (confirmed via direct read of its own source), so a clip pushed under the `VirtualList`'s own `composed` transform stays correctly anchored even though each child painted inside the layer goes on to set its own transform — the same real mechanism ripple's own clip (M4 Phase 5) already proves works. `paint_node`'s final child-recursion is now `NodeKind`-aware: only `VirtualList` pushes a real clip layer (a local rounded rect matching its own corner radius), composes the scroll offset into its own children's transform, and narrows `visible` to its own bounds (reusing Phase 1's own `bounds` value) before recursing — every other `NodeKind` keeps its exact prior recursion, since nothing else in this engine clips today. New `virtual_list_scroll.rs`: a real scroll offset genuinely shifts materialized children's own painted position (proven at the exact pixel-level overlap remaining after a partial scroll); scrolled-out content is genuinely clipped, not just repositioned. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-render` +2)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean — the full pre-existing `virtual_list.rs` suite passed unmodified, confirming the default `scroll_offset: 0.0` is a true no-op. This phase adds no new Python-facing API; pytest (78 passed, 1 skipped) and all fourteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M8 Phase 3 — real scroll input wired to `VirtualList` (§11.7), **closing M8 entirely (all 3 phases)** and M4 Phase 8's own long-stated gap. `Tree::dispatch`'s `InputEvent::Scroll` arm now hit-tests at the event's own position (the same mechanism `PointerPressed`/`PointerReleased` already use), walks up the hit node's own parent chain to the nearest `NodeKind::VirtualList` (a scroll gesture can land on any of its materialized children, not just its own root pixel), and calls new `Tree::scroll_virtual_list_by`, which clamps the real offset against the list's own real content extent and viewport height. Checked `winit`'s own docs directly and found no explicit scroll-sign convention documented — rather than present a guess as verified, this phase states its own chosen, tested convention plainly (positive `y` increases the offset) and a plain `20px`-per-line conversion for `Lines` deltas. Real, in-scope finding along the way: `Window.add_virtual_list`'s own `height: auto()` never gave a real viewport height at all (every materialized item is `Position::Absolute`, contributing nothing to intrinsic size) — fixed with an optional `height` parameter defaulting to the `Window`'s own real height, the same shape `width` already uses, fully backward compatible (confirmed via grep: no existing caller passes either). New `engine-py::Window.scroll(node, delta_y)`, the same no-live-window-needed pattern `click()`/`hover()` already established. 4 new `engine-core` tests (offset clamping at both ends, an unscrollable short list, real dispatch-driven scrolling over a child, a true no-op for no `VirtualList` ancestor), all passed on the first run; new pytest test proves the real FFI call chain runs without error; new `examples/scrollable_list.py`. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 69, up from 65)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean — every prior test passed unmodified. `pytest` (79 passed, up from 78, 1 skipped) and all fifteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M8 — Virtualization & Culling is now fully complete.** All 3 phases (engine-level paint culling, `VirtualList` real scroll offset & clipping, real scroll input wired to `VirtualList`) closed the milestone's own original investigation: zero culling anywhere and a purely caller-driven `VirtualList` with no real scroll/clip/input are both now real, composing together in one genuine scrollable list.

**Just closed:** M9 Phase 1 — real completion-handle plumbing through the central tick. `Animated<T>::tick` gains a `completed: &mut Vec<CompletionHandle>` parameter (a shared accumulator, not a per-call return, so a caller ticking many fields in one pass collects every real completion without allocating per field), pushing `anim.on_complete` into it on the exact tick an animation genuinely finishes — read out of the borrowed `ActiveAnimation` before `self.active` is cleared, the same order the existing `anim.to.clone()` already used. New `animate_to_with_completion` sibling method actually attaches a real handle; `animate_to` itself stays byte-for-byte unchanged, avoiding any of its own ~15+ existing call sites. `PaintProperties`/`InteractionState`/`RippleState`'s own `tick` methods thread `completed` through uniformly; `Tree::tick_all` now returns `(bool, Vec<CompletionHandle>)` instead of a bare `bool`; `set_splitter_position`'s own inline tick (kind-specific, outside the central walk since M8 Phase 2) discards its own completions, a real, stated scope boundary. 3 new `engine-core` tests, all passed on the first run; 4 existing test call sites (2 in `tree.rs`, 2 in `engine-render`'s own `animated_rect.rs`/`transform_composition.rs`) updated for the new signatures. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 72, up from 69)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean — every prior test passed unmodified. This phase adds no Python-facing API; pytest (79 passed, 1 skipped) and all fifteen examples confirmed clean, a pure regression check. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M9 Phase 2 — Python-facing `on_complete` callbacks. New `dispatch::CompletionRegistry`/`SharedCompletions` mirror `HandlerMap`'s own real shape exactly, with one real difference: `run_completions` uses `HashMap::remove`, not `get` — a real CSS `transitionend`/JS Promise-style single fire, not a repeating subscription. `PyWindow`/`Node` gain `completions: SharedCompletions` (a `View`-created node gets a fresh, private instance instead, since `View` has no real per-frame render loop to ever drain a completion through — the same stated scope limit `theme` already accepted for `View`, M7 Phase 3); `completions` holds real `Py<PyAny>` callbacks, so `PyWindow`'s own `__traverse__`/`__clear__` now cover it too. `Node.animate` gains `on_complete: Option<Py<PyAny>>`; a new private, generic `animate_field` helper replaces each of its six match arms' own direct `.animate_to` call, registering a handle only inside whichever one arm actually matches (avoiding an orphaned, never-invoked callback on an unknown-property error). `App::run`'s own `on_frame` closure now binds `Tree::tick_all`'s real completion `Vec` and drains it via `run_completions`. New pytest tests (an FFI smoke test, and a real cyclic-GC test proving a `Window`-capturing `on_complete` callback is genuinely collected, mirroring `test_virtual_list.py`'s own analogous `materializers` test); new `examples/animation_completion.py` — found and fixed a real timing issue while writing it (a 100ms real-duration animation never completed within a reasonable `max_frames` budget in this headless environment's own unpredictable frame rate; switched to `duration_ms=0`, deterministic regardless of real wall-clock pacing). No `engine-core`/`engine-render` change this phase — full `cargo test --workspace`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean, a pure regression check. `pytest` (82 passed, up from 79, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M9 Phase 3 — real automatic teardown for container transform (§7.6), **closing M9 entirely (all 3 phases)**. `engine_md3::container_transform::begin` gains `on_complete: Option<CompletionHandle>` (pure `engine-core`, no `pyo3` awareness — §4), wired onto the destination's own driven `transform` animation only: all four driven properties share one `start`/`duration`, so any one completing means the whole transition is genuinely done. `Window.begin_container_transform` gains `on_complete: Option<Py<PyAny>>`, registered into `self.completions` the same real way `Node.animate` already does, and passed down as the resulting handle. Two stale doc comments corrected along the way: `container_transform.rs`'s own module doc comment, which explicitly named this exact gap as "confirmed-still-unwired... separate, larger, unscoped work," now states it's real; `CompletionHandle`'s own doc comment (`animation.rs`), which said "no allocator/registry exists yet," now names the real one (M9 Phase 2's `CompletionRegistry`). New `engine-md3` tests prove a real handle genuinely reaches `Tree::tick_all`'s own drain once the transition finishes, not before, and that `None` never reports a completion; new pytest FFI smoke test. Updated `examples/container_transform.py`: `on_complete` now calls `end_container_transform` automatically, replacing the manual call at the script's end — the real, live, closing proof this milestone's own investigation set out to enable. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-md3` 9, up from 7)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean — every prior test passed unmodified. `pytest` (83 passed, up from 82, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M9 — Animation Completion Callbacks is now fully complete.** All 3 phases (real completion-handle plumbing through the central tick, Python-facing `on_complete` callbacks, real automatic teardown for container transform) closed the milestone's own original investigation: `CompletionHandle`/`ActiveAnimation.on_complete` were real, exported, genuinely unused types since M3 Phase 5 step 9 — now wired end to end, from `Animated::tick`'s own real per-tick detection through `Tree::tick_all`'s real drain, `engine-py`'s own Python-facing registry, and into container transform's own real automatic teardown.

**Just closed:** M10 Phase 1 — real overlay dismissal (§11.3). New `Tree::dismiss_overlays_outside`/`dismiss_escapable_overlays` (private) wired into `Tree::dispatch`'s real `PointerPressed`/`Key::Escape` arms — the single most-repeatedly-carried-forward candidate across M6 through M9's own scoping, finally closed. **Real finding during implementation, not anticipated during scoping:** `Tree::close_overlay` originally used full `Tree::remove` (irreversible destruction) — never actually exercised by a live app before this phase (confirmed via grep, zero prior callers in `engine-py`). The moment dismissal made it reachable for real, the most realistic use case broke: right-click a menu open, dismiss it, right-click the same anchor again — `Node.set_context_menu`'s own registered, reusable content `NodeId` was destroyed on first dismissal, panicking the next reopen. Caught by this project's own real pytest-level verification (an *existing* test started panicking), fixed by changing `close_overlay` to detach, not destroy — the identical contract `set_context_menu` already committed to. A second real finding: the first draft of outside-click dismissal treated a press back on the overlay's own anchor as "outside," breaking the same reopen scenario differently — fixed by excluding the anchor's own bounds too. Two stale doc comments corrected (`overlay.rs`'s own module doc comment; `overlay_meta`'s own doc comment). 8 new `engine-core` tests + 1 existing test updated for the real contract change; 2 new pytest tests proving dismissal reaches through the real FFI functionally. Full `cargo test --workspace`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean — every other prior test passed unmodified. `pytest` (85 passed, up from 83, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.
