## Milestone 4 — Real Input Dispatch (§4, §7.3, §9, §10, §11.10)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 9 phases done (2026-09-16).** Not sequenced anywhere in `ARCHITECTURE.md` — picked up the single most consistently recurring finding from every M3 interaction-dependent step (7, 9, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15, each checked directly): real pointer/keyboard `InputEvent`/`AppHandler` dispatch and hit-testing didn't exist. Phase 1 (3 steps) built it end to end for pointer/keyboard events reaching `Tree::dispatch`; Phase 2 closed the two real gaps Phase 1 explicitly left open (assistive-technology action dispatch, a Python-facing keyboard test entry point); Phase 3 closed the last repeatedly-named gap (real splitter-drag input); Phase 4 wired `View`'s declarative `on_click` handlers to that same real dispatch; Phase 5 made ripple/hover actually visible through the real render pipeline plus a real Python-facing hover opt-in; Phase 6 introduced a real `EventKind` vocabulary and wired `HoverEnter`/`HoverExit` through it; Phase 7 wired a real right-click to §11.3's overlay mechanism for context menus; Phase 8 added real scroll-wheel input plumbing; Phase 9 wired real docking drag-to-rearrange, needing zero new `engine-core` code. Every phase was verified directly against the current codebase (grep/direct reads, not assumption) before being scoped, and every phase's real scope was found to be at least as large as — often larger than — its own one-line tracker summary, confirmed by investigating before implementing rather than assuming the summary was complete.

### Phase 1 — Core Dispatch Mechanism ✅
- Step 1: `InputEvent`/`AppHandler`/hit-testing core, `engine-core` only (no `winit`, no `pyo3` yet) — ✅ (new `input.rs`: `InputEvent`/`PointerButton`/`Key`/`AppHandler`/`DispatchOutcome`, deliberately narrow `Key` — `Tab`/`Enter`/`Space`/`Escape` only, matching §10's own stated minimal keyboard model. `Tree` gains `hit_test` (§11.10, reverse paint-order rect containment — explicitly not transform-aware yet, `PaintProperties.transform` doesn't exist; no `NodeKind::Canvas` custom hit-test either, `Canvas` doesn't exist), `update_hover` (§7.3's own "falls out of hit-testing" text, opt-in-only per Design Principle 6, MD3 values stay caller-supplied so `engine-core` stays MD3-agnostic), `move_focus` (§10 Tab/Shift-Tab in tree order, "interactive" = non-empty `access.actions`, the real existing signal M3 step 7's button already sets), and `dispatch` (the one top-level entry point, producing `DispatchOutcome::Activated` as the sole meaning-dependent outcome for `AppHandler` to interpret later). Found a real architecture/API drift: §10's own text says Enter/Space dispatches `accesskit::Action::Default`, which doesn't exist in the pinned `accesskit = "0.25.0"` — the real equivalent is `Action::Click`, already what M3 step 7's own button uses. 6 new tests, each isolating one claim. `engine-core` now at 32 unit tests. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)
- Step 2: Real `winit` event translation (`engine-platform`) — ✅ (`WindowEvent::CursorMoved`/`MouseInput`/`KeyboardInput`/`ModifiersChanged` translate into real `InputEvent`s via a new `on_input` closure on `run_windowed_multi`, matching `on_frame`/`build_access_update`'s own shape; `run_windowed`'s two existing callers pass a no-op, unchanged. Found and fixed a real doc-comment error from step 1: `winit::event::MouseButton` has six real variants, not the three `PointerButton` claimed unverified. `winit` has no public API to inject a synthetic `WindowEvent` into a live loop — checked directly — so the real proof is 4 new unit tests against `translate_pointer_button`/`translate_key` directly, no live `EventLoop` needed. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)
- Step 3: `engine-py` implements the meaning-dependent half for real — ✅ (`Node.set_on_click(callback)` exists: `click_handlers: Rc<RefCell<HashMap<NodeId, Py<PyAny>>>>` shared between a `Window` and every `Node` it hands out, the same sharing shape `tree` already uses -- avoids giving `Node` a `PyWindow` back-reference. Visited by real `__traverse__`/`__clear__`, reusing M3 step 15 Stage C's materializer precedent exactly. Also adds `Action::Click` to `access.actions`, so a node with a click handler becomes Tab-reachable for free (§10). New `Window.click(node)` is a direct, programmatic click entry point proving the whole path with no live window needed. An uncaught handler exception is caught via `PyErr::print`, matching §9's "caught, logged, non-fatal" policy with a real traceback. 6 new pytest tests, including a real GC-cycle-collection proof for `click_handlers`. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Assistive-Technology Action Dispatch (§10) ✅
- Step 1: `Tree` gains direct activation/focus primitives (`engine-core`) — ✅ (`Tree::activate(node)` — the direct, non-`InputEvent` counterpart to a mouse click's `Activated` outcome, deliberately symmetric with the mouse path: doesn't check `access.actions` first. `Tree::set_focus_to(node, ...)` — the direct-target counterpart to `move_focus`'s tab-order computation, what `accesskit::Action::Focus` actually needs; factored the shared `focus_ring`-transition logic out of `move_focus` into a private `transition_focus` helper, reused by both. Both build on a new, public `from_access_id` (the reverse of the existing `to_access_id`), verified directly against `slotmap = "1.1.1"`'s own documented `KeyData::as_ffi`/`from_ffi` reversible round-trip guarantee — a stale accessibility-client-supplied id fails this `Tree`'s own generation check safely, the same generational-safety property §5 already relies on elsewhere. 5 new tests, each isolating one claim. `engine-core` now at 37 unit tests.)
- Step 2: Real `accesskit_winit` wiring (`engine-platform`) — ✅ (`run_windowed_multi` gained a sixth parameter, `on_access_action: FnMut(WindowId, accesskit::ActionRequest)`, firing on a real `accesskit_winit::WindowEvent::ActionRequested` — closing the exact gap step 7's own comment named since early in this project. Handed up raw/untranslated, unlike `on_input`: `engine-platform` doesn't know `engine_core::NodeId` exists, so the `from_access_id` conversion is the caller's job. `AccessibilityDeactivated` needed no new handling -- every `TreeUpdate` build already gates on activation state via the existing `update_if_active`. No live AT-SPI/UIA/NSAccessibility client is available in this environment to drive a genuine end-to-end proof — stated explicitly, not silently skipped; the real, honest proof is at the `engine-core` logic level, already fully unit-tested in step 1.)
- Step 3: `engine-py` wires the new callback + a keyboard test entry point — ✅ (`App::run()`'s new `on_access_action` closure converts the target id and matches `Action::Click`/`Action::Focus` onto `tree.activate`/`tree.set_focus_to`, feeding the outcome through the *same* `dispatch::run_activation` a mouse click and `Window.click()` already share -- one mechanism, three real entry points. New `Window.press_key(key, shift=False)` closes Phase 1's other stated gap: translates a small string vocabulary (`"tab"`/`"enter"`/`"space"`/`"escape"`) into `engine_core::Key` and dispatches through the identical path, making Tab-then-Enter/Space activation directly testable from Python for the first time. 6 new pytest tests, all passed on the first run. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 3 — Real Pointer-Drag Dispatch (§11.5, §11.4) ✅
- Step 1: Wire real mouse-drag to `Tree::set_splitter_position` (`engine-core`) — ✅ (`Tree` gains a `dragging: Option<NodeId>` field; the flanking-siblings geometry `set_splitter_position` already computed inline was factored into a shared `splitter_geometry` helper. New `update_drag(point, now)` converts a pointer coordinate into a 0.0..=1.0 fraction (relative to the left sibling's own absolute start and the flanking siblings' combined, drag-constant extent) and calls the *existing* `set_splitter_position` with it — §11.5's own "on drag... used both standalone... and by docking — one mechanism, two call sites" text, made real rather than reimplemented. `dispatch`'s `PointerPressed`/`PointerMoved`/`PointerReleased` arms now start (primary-button press on a splitter, reusing the existing hit-test call), live-update (every move while dragging, alongside the existing hover update), and end (primary-button release, unconditionally — a real mouse-up always ends a drag wherever it lands, giving free pointer-capture-like behavior since `update_drag` never depends on hit-testing) a real splitter drag. Because docking's own zone-resize already reuses `set_splitter_position` verbatim (M3 step 15 Stage B), dragging a dock-zone boundary is real now too, with zero docking-specific code touched. 4 new `engine-core` unit tests (`engine-core` now at 41) + a new `engine-render` pixel-readback test (`splitter_drag_dispatch.rs`) driving the whole press/move/release sequence through real `Tree::dispatch` rather than a direct call, all passed on the first run. No `engine-platform`/`engine-py` changes were needed — `Tree::dispatch` is the exact function `App::run()`'s existing real `winit` wiring already calls, so the mechanism reached the full stack for free. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)
- Step 2: `Window.add_splitter` (`engine-py`) — ✅ (mirrors `add_rect`'s own exact parameter shape plus a new `initial_position` keyword (0.0..=1.0, defaults to an even split); inserts a real `NodeKind::Splitter` and appends it to the window's own root row — no new mechanism needed, that root is already the flex parent `Tree::splitter_geometry` expects. `add_rect` (left), `add_splitter`, `add_rect` (right) is now a complete, real, mouse-draggable resizable-pane layout from Python. 5 new pytest tests + a new real example script (`examples/resizable_panes.py`) that opens a live window with a working layout and exits cleanly after real frames -- its own docstring states plainly that proving an actual live mouse drag needs a human running it interactively, since nothing here can automate a real pointer. All passed on the first run. See `LOG.md`.)

### Phase 4 — Wire `View`'s Declarative Handlers to Real Dispatch (§16.2) ✅
- Step: **Scope narrowed during implementation** — investigated whether `View`'s tree should embed into a `PyWindow` to reach `App::run()`'s event loop; found `View` has no width/height/render-loop concept at all and giving it one is real, separate, much larger work than the actual confirmed bug (a validated handler never registered, not "`View` can't run live") — deliberately not built here — ✅ (`View::_attach` now registers each validated `on_click` handler into the same `click_handlers`/`Tree::dispatch` mechanism `Node.set_on_click` already uses, via a temporary `Node` sharing `View`'s own `tree`/`click_handlers` — the same reuse pattern `apply_binding_value` already established for `animate`. `Node::set_on_click` widened from module-private to `pub(crate)`, matching `animate`'s own visibility, for its second in-crate caller. Other declared event names still validate-only, matching §16.2's own "generalizing to whatever named events a `NodeKind` exposes" — not manufactured ahead of Phase 6's `EventKind` work. New `View.click(node)` mirrors `Window.click` exactly (real dispatch, no live window needed) using `AvailableSpace::MaxContent` layout sizing since `View` has no window size of its own — every existing `view.yaml` already declares explicit `style.width`/`style.height` on its root widget, so this sizes correctly. Handlers are called with zero arguments, matching the established `Node.set_on_click`/`run_activation` convention rather than manufacturing an `Event` object early. 4 new pytest tests in `test_view_handlers.py` — a real click actually invokes a wired handler, repeated clicks each invoke it again, a non-`on_click` handler still validates but a click doesn't call it, an uncaught handler exception is caught/logged/non-fatal matching `Window.click`'s own §9 policy — all passed on the first run; full suite (41 passed, 1 skipped) and all three examples unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 5 — Real Ripple/Hover, End to End (§7.3) ✅
- Step: **Scope widened during implementation** — investigating this phase's own tracker scope before writing code found `paint_node` (`engine-render`'s real per-node walk) never read `node.interaction` at all, and that ripple's own "needs an opt-in" premise was half wrong (`Tree::dispatch`'s `PointerPressed` already lazily creates `InteractionState` on any hit node, unlike `update_hover`, which correctly never does) — ✅ (`paint_node` now paints a flat neutral-tint hover overlay and each active ripple, clipped via `push_layer` to its own growing circle and bounded to the node's own rect, for any node with `Some(interaction)`, painted after the node's own fill and before its children (state layers sit under content, matching real MD3). A fixed black tint, not per-scheme MD3 tokens — dynamic color isn't wired into `paint_node` for any property yet, a separate, larger, pre-existing gap. New `Node.enable_interaction()` — a thin `Tree::interaction_mut` call, kept deliberately separate from `set_on_click` since a purely-hoverable, non-clickable node is a real, independent case §7.3 itself describes. 2 new `engine-render` pixel-readback tests (`ripple_hover_dispatch.rs`) prove the real, complete path through `build_tree_scene` — a real dispatched press ripples with zero opt-in, a real dispatched hover shows nothing until opted in and then shows a uniform tint; 3 new pytest tests cover the FFI wiring (the definitive pixel proof has no Python-level equivalent, matching the `test_splitter.py`/`splitter_drag_dispatch.rs` split); new `examples/ripple_button.py`. All passed on the first run (after one real bug in the test itself — `update_hover`'s own "repeated call, same result, is a no-op" contract required moving off-node between two dispatches to get a genuine hover transition); full suite (44 passed, 1 skipped) and all four examples unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 6 — `EventKind` + Real `HoverEnter`/`HoverExit` (§7.3, §16.2) ✅
- Step: New `EventKind { Click, HoverEnter, HoverExit }` and `DispatchOutcome::HoverChanged { old, new }` in `engine-core`; `Tree::dispatch`'s `PointerMoved` arm reports a real transition instead of always `None` — ✅ (confirmed `update_hover`'s transition-tracking already ran unconditionally, independent of any `enable_interaction()` opt-in, matching §7.3's own "fires... independent of whether the default visual handles it" text exactly — no new gating logic needed, just surfacing what was already computed and discarded. 1 new `engine-core` unit test mirroring the existing `dispatch_activates_only_...` shape.)
- Step: Generalize handler storage from `click_handlers: HashMap<NodeId, Py<PyAny>>` to `handlers: HashMap<(NodeId, EventKind), Py<PyAny>>` (the Rule of Three, once `Click`/`HoverEnter`/`HoverExit` all needed the same shape) — ✅ (renamed across `Node`/`PyWindow`/`View`/`WindowSetup`/`WindowRuntime`; `dispatch::run_activation` generalized into `run_dispatch_outcome`; aliased as `dispatch::HandlerMap` once clippy's own `type_complexity` lint flagged the raw tuple-keyed type. New `Node.set_on_hover_enter`/`set_on_hover_exit`, `Window.hover(node)`/`View.hover(node)` mirroring `.click()`'s own no-live-window-needed pattern, `View::_attach`'s `on_click`-only special case generalized into a small match. Caught and fixed one real bug mid-rename: a blind `sed` pass collided with `View`'s own pre-existing, unrelated `handlers: Vec<...>` field (the parsed YAML declarations) — the compiler caught it immediately as a duplicate struct field; fixed by renaming that field to `declared_handlers`. 5 new pytest tests in `test_hover_events.py`, all passed on the first run; full suite (49 passed, 1 skipped) and all four examples unaffected by the internal rename. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

- Step: **Scope widened during implementation** — found no Python-facing overlay API existed at all (`open_overlay`/`close_overlay`/`OverlayMeta` were doc-comment-only in `engine-py`), and `open_overlay` requires unattached content (confirmed via `add_child`'s real no-dedup implementation) — ✅ (`Node.set_context_menu(content)` first `Tree::detach`s content if attached (docking's own existing mechanism, reused verbatim), then records `(anchor, content)` in a new plain `Rc<RefCell<HashMap<NodeId, NodeId>>>` shared field — no `Py<PyAny>` involved, so no GC-traversal obligation unlike `handlers`. Deliberately did not wire dismissal (`dismiss_on_outside_click`/`dismiss_on_escape`) — real, separate, still-open gap named since M3 step 13, not silently dropped. 4 new pytest tests (the real, functional proof: right-clicking the anchor, then clicking the now-live menu item confirms its own handler fires) + `examples/context_menu.py`, all passed on the first run; full suite (53 passed, 1 skipped) and all five examples unaffected. - Step: New `InputEvent::Scroll { delta, position }` + `winit::WindowEvent::MouseWheel` translation in `engine-platform`, mirroring Phase 1 step 2's pointer/keyboard translation pattern — ✅ (verified `winit = "0.30.13"`'s real `MouseScrollDelta` directly in its vendored source first: exactly two variants, `LineDelta`/`PixelDelta`, genuinely different units — new `engine_core::ScrollDelta` preserves that real distinction, the same lesson `PointerButton`'s own past correction already taught this codebase, rather than collapsing both into one plain tuple. `position` reuses `last_cursor_position` the same way `MouseInput` already does, since `winit`'s own `MouseWheel` carries no position either. New `engine-platform::translate_scroll_delta` mirrors `translate_pointer_button`/`translate_key`'s own pure-function shape exactly. `Tree::dispatch` gained the required match arm as a true, deliberate no-op — `DispatchOutcome::None` unconditionally, matching this step's own explicit "plumbing only" scope; wiring scroll to `VirtualList`'s window movement needs the still-open real scrollable-viewport gap (clipping + scroll offset, §11.8/§11.9) named below under Milestone 5, not manufactured ahead of that need. 2 new unit tests (one per crate), both passed on the first run; full `cargo test --workspace` green, and — since this phase touched no `engine-py` code — the full pytest suite and all five examples confirmed unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 9 — Docking Drag-to-Rearrange (§11.4) ✅
- Step: **Scope narrowed during implementation** — no Python-facing docking API existed at all (confirmed via grep), and reading `apply_active_tab` closely first found it never checks whether a panel is still attached elsewhere before reattaching it (the same "`add_child` has no dedup" class of bug M4 Phase 7 found for `open_overlay`) — ✅ (new `engine-py::dock` module: `DockState` shared as `Rc<RefCell<...>>` on `PyWindow`, needing *zero* new `engine-core` code — `Tree::hit_test`/`Tree::apply_active_tab`/`Node::parent` are exactly what a purely meaning-dependent "which node is a handle/panel/zone" orchestration needs (Design Principle 6). New `Window.add_dock_zone`/`dock_panel`/`set_active_tab`/`set_dock_handle`, and `start_panel_drag`/`drop_panel_at` mirroring `.click()`/`.hover()`/`.right_click()`'s own no-live-window-needed pattern; real `winit` wiring inspects the raw `InputEvent` directly (not `DispatchOutcome`, which has no business knowing about docking). Deliberately shipped with no drop-zone highlight overlay — `open_overlay`'s own placement is hardcoded to a dropdown-menu position and can't cover a target zone's bounds; extending it would be disproportionate to this phase's actual core claim, named as a real, stated gap. **A second real bug caught only by running the live example, not pytest:** `examples/docking.py` panicked with a genuine `accesskit` "duplicate child" validation failure on first run — the exact bug class above, just applied to `dock_panel`'s own initial setup, which no pytest test exercises since none of them render a real frame or call `build_access_update`. Fixed by detaching the panel from its existing parent first. 7 new pytest tests + a new example, all green after the fix; full suite (60 passed, 1 skipped) and all six examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 5 — Transform Composition & Custom Drawing (§11.9, §11.10, §11.11)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 4 phases done (2026-09-16).** Deliberately kept separate from M4 rather than folded in as more phases: these are rendering/scene-graph capability gaps exposed *by* hit-testing, not input-dispatch work themselves. Confirmed directly against the current codebase before scoping: `PaintProperties` (`crates/engine-core/src/node.rs`) had no `transform` field at all before Phase 1, and the real `NodeKind` enum had exactly five variants (`Rect`/`Container`/`Text`/`Splitter`/`VirtualList`) before Phase 3 — no `Canvas`. `Tree::hit_test`'s own doc comment said "not transform-aware yet" from M4 Phase 1 step 1 through M5 Phase 1 — fixed in Phase 2. Phase 4 validated all three pieces (plus `VirtualList`) compose in one real tree, not separately, closing the milestone.

### Phase 1 — Transform Composition (§11.9) ✅
- Step 1: Add `transform: Animated<kurbo::Affine>` to `PaintProperties`, composed down the tree during the paint walk (parent's effective transform × its own), exactly like nested `<g transform>` in SVG — ✅ (`PaintProperties.transform: Animated<peniko::kurbo::Affine>`, default `Affine::IDENTITY`; `Interpolate for Affine` is a plain componentwise coefficient lerp — `kurbo`'s own real rotation-aware `Affine::svd()` exists but is `pub(crate)`, not exported, confirmed directly in its vendored source, and §11.9's own text only ever asks for pan/zoom, never rotation. `engine-render::paint_node` refactored from an accumulated `(offset_x, offset_y)` absolute-coordinate scheme to a composed-`Affine` local-coordinate one, using the real `vello_hybrid::Scene::set_transform` (confirmed both `fill_path`/`glyph_run` respect it) — zero per-`NodeKind` special-casing. A real bug found and fixed by reading code, not by a failing test: `InteractionState::ripples`' `origin` is captured in absolute canvas space, which the new local-space model would have silently misplaced — fixed via `composed.inverse()`. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)
- Step 2: `engine-render` pixel-readback proof that a `Container`'s animated transform (pan offset × zoom scale) propagates to descendants for free, with zero per-descendant special-casing — ✅ (new `transform_composition.rs`: a `Container`'s transform animated toward a combined pan+zoom target propagates to an untouched `Rect` child, proven genuinely mid-flight at t=0.5, not just at either endpoint. Full pre-existing pixel-readback suite passed unmodified — the "pure no-op for identity-transform content" claim reasoned through algebraically first, then confirmed in practice. Full `cargo test --workspace` (44)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; full pytest suite (60 passed, 1 skipped) and all six examples unaffected, as expected — no `engine-py` changes this phase. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Transform-Aware Hit-Testing (completing §11.10) ✅
- Step 1: `hit_test` transforms the pointer position into each candidate's local space via the inverse of its composed transform (Phase 1) before containment testing against `Node::computed_layout()` — ✅ (`Tree::hit_test` now delegates to a private `hit_test_at` composing the identical `parent * translate(layout.location) * own_transform` product `paint_node` composes during paint, kept in sync via cross-referencing doc comments since `engine-core` can't depend on `engine-render` to share the arithmetic directly. Public signature unchanged, so every existing caller — `update_hover`, `dispatch`'s press/release arms, `engine-py::dock`'s drag-drop, the Python click/hover/right-click entry points — gets transform-awareness for free. A real test-design gap (not an implementation bug) found while writing the new tests: `Container` nodes are themselves valid hit-test targets by their own full bounds, and a node's own transform moves itself too, not just descendants, so a point missing a moved child falls through to a still-valid ancestor instead of `None` — fixed by correcting the tests' expected values. Two new `engine-core` tests (translate-only, combined scale+translate) prove hit-testing agrees with where paint actually draws. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; pytest suite and all six examples unaffected — no `engine-py` changes needed. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 3 — `NodeKind::Canvas` + Custom Hit-Testing (§11.10, §11.11) ✅
- Step 1: `NodeKind::Canvas` with an app-supplied draw callback — a genuinely new FFI callback shape, mirroring §11.7's own "materialize item N" ad hoc callback precedent (§8) rather than the event-driven `on_click`/`on_complete` shape — ✅ (confirmed neither `engine-core` nor `engine-render` can call into Python — the real crate DAG, §4 — so the callback is invoked exactly once by a new, explicit `Window.redraw_canvas` entry point, mirroring `Window.set_virtual_list_window`'s own "store now, invoke later" shape; its result — plain `CanvasState { commands: Vec<DrawCommand>, hit_test: Option<CustomHitTest> }`, no `Py<PyAny>` — is what `paint_node`/`hit_test_at` actually read. New `engine-py::CanvasContext` pyclass (`fill_rect`/`fill_circle`/`stroke_path`). `paint_node` gained a `Canvas` arm using real `vello_hybrid::Scene::stroke_path`/`set_stroke`. New `engine-render/tests/canvas_paint.rs` proves commands paint at their correct, transform-composed position with zero special-casing. A real naming bug (`color_` vs. `color` in pyo3 kwargs) caught by running the new pytest tests, fixed. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)
- Step 2: Optional custom hit-test callback that overrides the default rect test for that node only, per §11.10's own text — ✅ (`CustomHitTest::Circle`/`Path`, the latter using `kurbo = "0.13.1"`'s own real `ParamCurveNearest` trait for exact distance-to-path math — confirmed directly in its vendored source, not hand-rolled; already generalizes to true bezier curves even though `set_hit_test_path` only builds straight polylines from Python this phase. Two new `engine-core` tests prove the override is real — a point inside the node's own bounding box but outside the custom shape genuinely misses, not just a narrower rect. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 4 — Node Graphs & Charts Groundwork (§11.11) ✅
- Step 1: No new framework mechanism per §11.11's own text — this phase is validation only: a real example (a small node graph or chart) proving `Canvas` + transform composition + custom hit-testing + virtualization/culling actually compose as designed, not separately-built follow-up machinery — ✅ (confirmed §11.8 culling was never actually built, zero grep hits — an honest, pre-existing gap outside M5's own scoped sections, not something to fake here; §11.11's own text already permits decimation/visibility as an application concern. New `engine-render/tests/graph_composition.rs`: one shared animated-transform `Container` holding a `Rect` node, a `Canvas` edge, and a `VirtualList` item together — all three paint and hit-test correctly at their transformed positions, and the edge's custom hit-test still genuinely excludes an off-path point, proven in a combined scene for the first time, not just in isolation; passed on the first run. A real, load-bearing Python-API gap found by reading `engine-py/src/node.rs` directly before writing the live example, not by trial and error: no `transform` property on `Node.animate()`, no `Position::Absolute` exposed by `add_rect`/`add_canvas` — a Python graph with independently-positioned nodes/edges isn't buildable today. Scope narrowed, not silently worked around: new `examples/node_graph.py` draws the whole graph as `DrawCommand`s inside one `Canvas` instead, with a `CustomHitTest::Circle` on one node matching §11.10's own example verbatim. No production code changes this phase. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 6 — Imperative API Completeness

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 4 phases done (2026-09-16).** Not a rendering/input-dispatch capability gap like M4/M5 — this milestone closes real, concrete holes in `engine-py`'s own imperative (`Window.add_rect`-style, not YAML/`engine-spec`) API surface, each confirmed by direct investigation across M4/M5 rather than assumed: `Node` has no `add_child` (only ever built at the `engine-core` level, with **no cycle detection at all** — confirmed by reading `Tree::add_child` directly, `taffy`'s own rejection aside, a Python caller could attach an ancestor as its own descendant and corrupt the tree); `Node.animate()`'s real property list (`node.rs`) is `opacity`/`corner_radius`/`elevation`/`background` only, no `transform`; neither `Window.add_rect` nor `add_canvas` expose `taffy::Position::Absolute`, so every Python-created node is flex-row/block-flowed only, never freely positioned (the concrete blocker M5 Phase 4 hit while trying to write a positioned node-graph example); and `Tree::absolute_position` still doesn't know `PaintProperties.transform` exists (M5's own post-milestone code review), so overlay anchor placement, splitter-drag geometry, and every `Window`/`View` synthetic-point entry point (`.click()`/`.hover()`/`.right_click()`) silently compute the wrong canvas position for a node inside a panned/zoomed `Container`. None of these are new mechanisms — each is completing/exposing something that already exists one layer down.

### Phase 1 — `Node.add_child` (real cycle rejection) ✅
- Step 1: `engine-core`: a real ancestor-chain check before `Tree::add_child` attaches, returning a rejectable error instead of blindly corrupting the tree — the first real use of the `EngineError::CycleRejected` shape §8 sketched from the start but nothing has ever needed until now — ✅ (new `Tree::try_add_child`, walking up from `parent` via `Node::parent` links, the same walk `absolute_position` already uses; `add_child` and its ~80 existing call sites untouched. Also detaches an already-attached child first via `Tree::detach`, the same "`add_child` has no dedup" fix M4 Phase 7/9 each already made once for their own caller. Three new `engine-core` tests, all passed on the first run. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)
- Step 2: `engine-py`: `Node.add_child(child)`, raising a clear Python exception on a rejected cycle (not a panic) — ✅ (new `EngineError::CycleRejected`/`ForeignNode`; `Node.add_child` checks `Rc::ptr_eq(&self.tree, &child.tree)` first — a real, found-not-assumed risk: `NodeId` is only unique within its own `Tree`, so a cross-`Window` call could alias an unrelated node and hand a foreign id to `taffy`. 5 new pytest tests, all passed on the first run; full suite (73 passed, 1 skipped) and all eight examples unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — `transform` exposure from Python ✅

## Milestone 7 — MD3 Visual Fidelity

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 5 phases done (2026-09-16).** The user chose this direction explicitly over the other real, investigated M6-era candidate (`VirtualList` real scrolling + engine-level culling), which remains a real, un-scoped future option, not abandoned. §7's own MD3 subsystem is, section by section, *built but disconnected* from what a real app actually sees — confirmed by direct investigation, not assumed: `elevation` is a real, animatable, Python-settable `PaintProperties` field (§7.2) that `paint_node` never reads — no node has ever actually painted a shadow, despite `build_shadow_scene` (M3 step 8) already proving `vello_hybrid::Scene::fill_blurred_rounded_rect` works. `DynamicTheme`/`ColorScheme` (§7.1, all 49 real MD3 roles, verified against `material-colors`' own 129-test reference suite, M3 step 11) is wired into the `engine-spec`/YAML view path (`engine-spec::build`/`reconcile`) but not the imperative `Window.add_rect`-style API at all, and ripple/hover's own state-layer tint is hardcoded black, not a real "on-surface" scheme role (confirmed via direct read of `paint_node`'s own doc comment, M4 Phase 5). `ShapeKey` (§7.4 shape morphing) exists as pure, unit-tested `Interpolate` math (M3 step 10) with zero `PaintProperties`/`NodeKind` integration — `node.rs`'s own doc comment has named this omission since M3 step 3. `MotionCurve` has exactly one real variant, `Linear` — confirmed via grep, no MD3 named curve (Standard, Emphasized, ...) and no `engine_md3::motion` module exist anywhere, despite several earlier phases' own doc comments already describing the intended `engine_md3::motion::STANDARD` shape as if real. Live theme switching (§7.1's own "in scope" text) was deliberately deferred in M3 step 11 because `AppHandler`/real input dispatch didn't exist yet — confirmed stale: M4 built real dispatch entirely, so this blocker no longer applies. Container transform (§7.6) is the one true exception — confirmed via grep, zero implementation, not even a spike; ARCHITECTURE.md's own text already scopes its real, concrete choreography and names the one genuinely new primitive it needs (`Node::computed_layout()`, also confirmed not to exist yet).

### Phase 1 — Real MD3 Motion Tokens (§7.5) ✅
- Step 1: `MotionCurve` gains the real MD3 named easing curves (Standard, Emphasized, ...) as cubic-bezier variants, evaluated via `kurbo`'s own curve math — durations/curves as a static data table in `engine-md3` (`engine_md3::motion`), not a new tweening dependency, per §7.5's own text. Foundational for every later phase in this milestone: ripple/hover/focus-ring's own real MD3 values (currently `Linear`, M4 Phase 5/6's own stated scope) and container transform's "Emphasized" choreography (§7.6) both need this to exist first — ✅ (values verified against Android's own `MotionTokens.kt`, not recalled — `Emphasized` is a real two-segment compound curve, not a single cubic-bezier, contrary to a common CSS approximation one secondary source used. New `CubicSegment` + bisection-based `solve_y_for_x`; landed directly on `MotionCurve` in `engine-core`, matching that enum's own pre-existing doc comment intent, not a separate `engine-md3` module — `engine-core` stays free of any new dependency. Four new tests (boundary, monotonicity, round-trip, and a real landmark check on `Emphasized`'s own documented split point), all passed on the first run. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

- Step 1: Wire `node.paint.elevation.current` into `paint_node`'s real per-node walk, using the exact `Scene::fill_blurred_rounded_rect` technique `build_shadow_scene` (M3 step 8) already proved works against this pinned `vello_hybrid` version — mapping MD3's own elevation levels to real blur/offset values, not an arbitrary guess — ✅ (real values from Material Web's own production `_elevation.scss`, not guessed — a two-layer key+ambient shadow, continuous piecewise-linear formula supporting fractional elevation, `std_dev = blur/2` per the real W3C spec, real constant-black shadow color. Lives directly in `engine-render`, confirmed correct per §4's own dependency diagram (no `engine-render → engine-md3` edge exists). 2 new pixel tests + new `examples/elevation.py`, all passed on the first run; full pre-existing suite unmodified. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 3 — Dynamic Color, Wired for Real (§7.1, completing §7.3) ✅
- Step 1: A `Window`/`App`-level theme concept in `engine-py`, generated via the real, already-proven `DynamicTheme::from_seed` (M3 step 11) — closing the gap where the imperative API has no way to reference an MD3 scheme role at all, only raw `(r, g, b, a)` tuples — ✅ (new `PyWindow.set_theme(seed, dark=False)`, storing a shared `ThemeState { theme: Option<DynamicTheme>, dark: bool }` — full `DynamicTheme` kept, not just the resolved color, so a later live switch can re-resolve without the seed. New `Tree::set_all_interaction_tints` pushes a freshly resolved color into every already-opted-in node; `Node.enable_interaction()` applies it immediately for a node opting in after the theme was set. `View`'s own 3 construction sites get a fresh, private, always-`None` `ThemeState` instead — out of this phase's stated "Window/App-level" scope, `View`'s own behavior is unchanged.)
- Step 3: Real live theme switching — `winit`'s `ThemeChanged` forwarded through the real `InputEvent`/dispatch path M4 built (the exact blocker `engine-md3::color`'s own doc comment named as the reason this was deferred in M3 step 11, confirmed stale by this phase's own investigation) — ✅ (new `InputEvent::ThemeChanged { dark: bool }`, a true no-op in `Tree::dispatch` mirroring `Scroll`'s own M4 Phase 8 precedent; new `engine-platform::translate_theme` free function maps the real, verified `winit::window::Theme::{Light,Dark}` — confirmed via its own source doc comment to be unsupported on iOS/Android/X11/Wayland/Orbital, a real platform limitation on this dev machine's own Linux session, not a bug in the wiring. `engine-py::App::run`'s `WindowSetup`/`WindowRuntime` gain a shared `theme` handle; the `on_input` closure gains a `ThemeChanged` arm updating the active mode and re-pushing the resolved tint via `set_all_interaction_tints`.)

New pixel test (`ripple_hover_dispatch.rs`) proves a real, non-black tint paints a real hue (red-channel-dominant), not just a darker gray, the way a hardcoded black tint could only ever produce; `engine-core` gains 3 new tests (`InteractionState` default-black no-op, `set_all_interaction_tints` touches only opted-in nodes, `ThemeChanged` dispatches to a true no-op); `engine-platform` gains 1 (`translate_theme` maps both real `winit` variants). New `examples/theme.py`. ## Milestone 8 — Virtualization & Culling (§11.7, §11.8)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 3 phases done (2026-09-17).** The user chose this direction explicitly over two other real, investigated candidates (Interaction & Overlay Completeness — `dismiss_on_outside_click`/`dismiss_on_escape`, real `OverlayMeta` data since M3 step 13, never acted on by any dispatch; no drop-zone highlight overlay for docking; Canvas Authoring Completeness — `stroke_path`/`set_hit_test_path` only build straight polylines even though the underlying hit-test math already supports true bezier curves). This is the single most-repeated "up next" candidate across M4 through M7's own gap notes, confirmed real by direct investigation, not assumed: `Tree::set_virtual_list_window` (`engine-core/src/tree.rs`) requires the *caller* to supply an explicit `visible: Range<usize>` — there is no real scroll offset, no clipping, and no engine-computed "what's visible" anywhere; `ItemExtent` has exactly one real variant, `Fixed` (confirmed via direct read of `node.rs`), matching its own doc comment's stated "no consumer needs the callback variant yet." Paint culling (§11.8) has genuinely zero implementation — `grep -rn "cull"` across every crate returns zero hits. `InputEvent::Scroll`/`ScrollDelta` are real and reach `Tree::dispatch` for real, from a genuine `winit::WindowEvent::MouseWheel` (M4 Phase 8) — but `Tree::dispatch`'s own handling of it is a confirmed, deliberate no-op (`InputEvent::Scroll { .. } => DispatchOutcome::None`), never wired to `VirtualList` or anything else, exactly as M4 Phase 8's own gap note has stated since it landed. `ARCHITECTURE.md` §11.8's own text already specifies the exact culling mechanism this milestone builds: "The paint pass (§6) skips Vello scene-encoding entirely for any subtree whose computed layout bounds... transformed into viewport space... don't intersect the current visible/clip region" — directly reusing `Tree::layout`/`Tree::absolute_position`, both real and already-used-externally since M6 Phase 4 (and reconfirmed reusable by M7 Phase 5's own investigation, which found no new "computed layout" primitive was even needed there). `paint_node`'s own child-recursion (`engine-render/src/lib.rs`) currently applies zero clipping anywhere — every child paints unconditionally at whatever position its own layout dictates; `vello_hybrid::Scene::push_layer`'s own real `clip_path` support (already used for ripple, M4 Phase 5) is the mechanism `VirtualList`'s own real clipping needs.

### Phase 1 — Engine-Level Paint Culling (§11.8) ✅
- Step 1: `build_tree_scene`/`paint_node` gain a real "current visible rect" (canvas-space, computed once from the render target's own width/height at the top, narrowed by any clip a node introduces on the way down) — a node whose own composed absolute bounds don't intersect it skips Vello scene-encoding for its *entire* subtree, not just its own fill, matching §11.8's own text exactly — ✅ (`paint_node` gains a `visible: Rect` parameter, checked via `peniko::kurbo::Rect::overlaps` right after `composed`/`w`/`h` are computed — a whole-subtree early return, before any shadow/fill/interaction painting and before any recursion into children. `build_tree_scene` computes the initial full-viewport rect once; the recursive call threads it through unchanged for now (Phase 2 is the first real narrowing consumer). 2 new pixel tests in `paint_culling.rs`, both passed on the first run — one proves the real distinguishing behavior (an off-screen parent's own child, whose `transform` would otherwise bring it back on-screen, never paints, since the whole subtree is skipped before the child's own transform is ever composed); one proves a partially-overlapping node still paints its visible portion. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean — every prior test unmodified; no Python-facing API touched, pytest (78 passed, 1 skipped) and all fourteen examples unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — `VirtualList` Real Scroll Offset & Clipping (§11.7) ✅
- Step 1: `VirtualListState` gains a real `Animated<f64>` scroll offset, composed into its materialized children's effective position the same way `PaintProperties.transform` already composes into any node's own paint — ✅ (real finding: `Tree::tick_all` never ticks kind-specific `Animated<T>` fields — `SplitterState.position`'s own precedent confirmed this — so `scroll_offset` follows the identical "set directly, not eased by default" shape. Vertical only, a real, stated v1 scope limit.)
- Step 2: `paint_node`'s `VirtualList` arm clips its own materialized children to its own bounds via `Scene::push_layer`'s real `clip_path` (the same mechanism ripple's own clip already proves works, M4 Phase 5) — content scrolled outside the list's own box is genuinely not drawn, not just positioned off to the side — ✅ (real finding: `push_layer`'s own `clip_path` bakes into absolute strips at call time, confirmed via direct read of `vello_hybrid`'s own source, so it stays correctly anchored regardless of each child's own later transform. `paint_node`'s final child-recursion is now `NodeKind`-aware — only `VirtualList` pushes the clip, composes the scroll offset, and narrows `visible` to its own bounds (reusing Phase 1's own `bounds` value, so an off-screen-within-the-clip child is engine-culled too); every other kind keeps its exact prior recursion. 2 new pixel tests in `virtual_list_scroll.rs`, both passed on the first run; the full pre-existing `virtual_list.rs` suite passed unmodified, confirming the default-offset no-op claim. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; no Python-facing API touched, pytest (78 passed, 1 skipped) and all fourteen examples unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 3 — Real Scroll Input Wired to `VirtualList` (§11.7, closing M4 Phase 8's own gap) ✅
- Step 1: `Tree::dispatch`'s `InputEvent::Scroll` arm, when it hits a real `NodeKind::VirtualList`, updates that list's own real scroll offset (via `animate_to` or a direct set, matching whichever gives the most natural real-mouse-wheel feel) instead of remaining a no-op — closing the milestone: real scroll input, a real scroll offset, real clipping, and real culling all composing together in one genuine scrollable list — ✅ (direct set, matching `scroll_offset`'s own Phase 2 "not eased by default" shape. New `Tree::scroll_virtual_list_by` (clamped, dispatch-reusable, the same "direct method `dispatch` reuses" shape `set_splitter_position` uses); `dispatch`'s own `Scroll` arm hit-tests and walks up to the nearest `VirtualList` ancestor, scroll-bubbling from any of its materialized children. Real, in-scope fix found along the way: `add_virtual_list`'s own `height: auto()` never gave a real viewport at all — now a real, optional `height` parameter, backward compatible. New `engine-py::Window.scroll`, mirroring `click()`/`hover()`. 4 new `engine-core` tests + 1 new pytest test, all passed on the first run; new `examples/scrollable_list.py`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; pytest (79 passed, 1 skipped) and all fifteen examples unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 9 — Animation Completion Callbacks

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 3 phases done (2026-09-17).** The user chose this direction explicitly over two other real, investigated candidates (Interaction & Overlay Completeness — `dismiss_on_outside_click`/`dismiss_on_escape`, real `OverlayMeta` data since M3 step 13, re-confirmed today still never acted on by any dispatch; `Node.set_context_menu`/`dock::set_dock_handle` re-confirmed today to still have no `Rc::ptr_eq` same-tree check at all (only `add_child`/M7 Phase 5's `begin_container_transform`/`end_container_transform` have one); no drop-zone highlight overlay for docking; Canvas Authoring Completeness — `stroke_path`/`set_hit_test_path` still confirmed to only build straight polylines, `Window.redraw_canvas` still explicitly app-triggered only). Confirmed real by direct investigation: `engine_core::animation::CompletionHandle(pub u64)` and `ActiveAnimation.on_complete: Option<CompletionHandle>` are real, exported types, but genuinely unused anywhere — every real `animate_to` call site (`engine-py::Node.animate`'s six properties, `engine_md3::container_transform::begin`'s four driven properties) sets `on_complete: None`, and no drain/queue/consumption mechanism exists anywhere (confirmed via grep). `engine-core::interaction`'s own module doc comment already named this exact queue as the real mechanism a Python-visible completion callback would eventually need, deliberately deferred at M3 Phase 5 step 9 pending a real caller. M7 Phase 5's own `container_transform.rs` explicitly re-confirmed and re-deferred the identical gap: `teardown` is a plain, manually-invoked function specifically because this queue didn't exist yet. `Animated::animate_to`'s own real signature (`to, duration, curve, now`) has no `on_complete` parameter at all today — adding one is additive (a new sibling method, not a breaking change to the ~15+ existing call sites across `engine-core`/`engine-md3`/`engine-py`, confirmed via grep). `Tree::tick_all`'s own real signature returns a bare `bool` today; real callers that bind it (`crates/engine-core/src/tree.rs`'s own tests) will need updating once it returns real completion data too — real callers that discard it (`engine-py::app.rs`'s own per-frame render loop, `view.rs`) don't break on a return-type change, but `app.rs`'s own loop is exactly where completions need to start actually being drained and dispatched to stored Python callbacks, mirroring `run_dispatch_outcome`'s own "look up and call a registered callback" shape.

### Phase 1 — Real Completion-Handle Plumbing Through the Central Tick ✅
- Step 1: `Animated<T>::tick` surfaces a just-completed `on_complete` handle instead of silently dropping it; `PaintProperties`/`InteractionState`/`RippleState`'s own `tick` methods thread it through to `Tree::tick_all`, which returns the real set of handles that finished this tick (not just a bare `bool`) — every existing call site that binds the old return value updated to match, every call site that discards it untouched — ✅ (`completed: &mut Vec<CompletionHandle>` is a shared accumulator threaded through the whole walk, not a per-field return — `anim.on_complete` read out before `self.active` is cleared, the same order the existing `anim.to.clone()` already used. 2 `tree.rs` test call sites + 2 downstream `engine-render` test call sites updated for the new signature.)
- Step 2: A new `Animated<T>::animate_to_with_completion` (or equivalent) that actually attaches a real `CompletionHandle` to the `ActiveAnimation` it starts — `animate_to` itself stays unchanged (`on_complete: None`, byte-for-byte current behavior, zero of its ~15+ existing call sites touched) — ✅ (identical body to `animate_to` except `on_complete: Some(...)`. 3 new tests, all passed on the first run: a real handle reports exactly on the completing tick, never early, never twice; plain `animate_to` never reports a completion; `InteractionState::tick` genuinely threads a real completion up from `hover_opacity`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; no Python-facing API touched, pytest (79 passed, 1 skipped) and all fifteen examples unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Python-Facing `on_complete` Callbacks ✅
- Step 1: `Node.animate(property, to, duration_ms, on_complete=None)` — when given, mints a fresh `CompletionHandle` (a monotonic counter, shared the same `Rc<RefCell<...>>` way `handlers`/`context_menus`/`theme` already are), stores the real Python callback keyed by it, and calls the new `animate_to_with_completion` instead of `animate_to` — ✅ (new `dispatch::CompletionRegistry`/`SharedCompletions`; a new private, generic `animate_field` helper replaces each of `animate`'s six match arms' own direct `.animate_to` call, registering a handle only inside whichever one arm actually matches — avoids leaking an orphaned callback on an unknown-property error. `completions` holds real `Py<PyAny>` values, so `PyWindow`'s own `__traverse__`/`__clear__` now cover it too, same as `handlers`. `View`-created nodes get a fresh, private instance, matching `theme`'s own precedent.)
- Step 2: `App::run`'s own per-frame loop drains `Tree::tick_all`'s new completions and invokes each matching stored Python callback exactly once (one-shot, matching a real CSS `transitionend`/JS Promise-style single fire, not a repeating subscription), the same "look up and call a registered callback" shape `run_dispatch_outcome` already uses — ✅ (new `dispatch::run_completions`, `HashMap::remove` not `get`. New pytest FFI smoke test + a real cyclic-GC test (mirroring `test_virtual_list.py`'s own analogous `materializers` test); new `examples/animation_completion.py` — found and fixed a real timing issue while writing it (a 100ms real-duration animation never completed within a reasonable `max_frames` budget in this headless environment's own unpredictable frame rate; `duration_ms=0` is deterministic regardless of real wall-clock pacing). No `engine-core`/`engine-render` change this phase; full suite/clippy/fmt clean, pytest (82 passed, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 3 — Real Automatic Teardown for Container Transform (§7.6, closing its own stated gap) ✅
- Step 1: `Window.begin_container_transform` gains an optional `on_complete` parameter, wired onto the destination's own driven `transform` animation (all four driven properties share one `start`/`duration`, so any one of them completing means the transition is genuinely done) via the new completion mechanism — a real app can now pass a callback that calls `end_container_transform` and have teardown fire automatically, closing the exact gap `container_transform.rs`'s own doc comment named as confirmed-still-unwired — ✅ (`engine_md3::container_transform::begin` gains `on_complete: Option<CompletionHandle>` — pure `engine-core`, no `pyo3` awareness, only the `transform` animation needs it. `Window.begin_container_transform` registers a real Python callback the same way `Node.animate` already does. Two stale doc comments corrected (`container_transform.rs`'s own module doc comment; `CompletionHandle`'s own doc comment in `animation.rs`). 2 new `engine-md3` tests + 1 new pytest test, all passed on the first run; `examples/container_transform.py` updated to use real automatic teardown instead of a manual call. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; pytest (83 passed, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 10 — Interaction & Overlay Completeness

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 3 phases done (2026-09-17).** The user chose this direction explicitly over two other real, investigated candidates (Canvas Authoring Completeness — `stroke_path`/`set_hit_test_path` still confirmed to only build straight polylines (`polyline()`), even though the underlying hit-test math already supports true bezier curves, `Window.redraw_canvas` still explicitly app-triggered only; Variable-Height `VirtualList` Items — `ItemExtent` still has exactly one real variant, `Fixed`, re-confirmed today). This is the single most-repeatedly-carried-forward candidate across M6 through M9's own scoping, confirmed real by direct investigation, not assumed: `OverlayMeta.dismiss_on_outside_click`/`dismiss_on_escape` (`crates/engine-core/src/overlay.rs`) are real data, stored correctly since M3 step 13, but genuinely never read by any dispatch mechanism (confirmed via grep — `overlay_meta` has exactly the one real reader, `dispatch::open_context_menu`'s own re-open guard, never a dismissal check). `overlay.rs`'s own module doc comment is stale, still claiming "real pointer/keyboard `InputEvent`/`AppHandler` dispatch and hit-testing... don't exist anywhere in this codebase yet" — true at M3 step 13, false since M4 built all of it. `Node.set_context_menu` (`node.rs`) and `dock::set_dock_handle` still have no `Rc::ptr_eq` same-tree check at all (confirmed via grep — only `Node.add_child`, M6 Phase 1, and `Window.begin_container_transform`/`end_container_transform`, M7 Phase 5, have one) — a real, small correctness gap M6 Phase 1's own investigation first surfaced and explicitly deferred. No drop-zone highlight overlay exists for docking — `Tree::open_overlay`'s own real positioning is anchor-relative only (placed below a single anchor node's bounds), not "cover an arbitrary target rect," which is what highlighting a drop zone during a drag actually needs — confirmed via direct read of `open_overlay`'s own implementation, not assumed from the Known Gaps note alone.

### Phase 1 — Real Overlay Dismissal (§11.3) ✅
- Step 1: `Tree::dispatch`'s real `PointerPressed`/`PointerReleased` and `KeyPressed` arms close any currently-open overlay whose own `OverlayMeta` opts into `dismiss_on_outside_click`/`dismiss_on_escape`, reusing the already-real `Tree::hit_test`/`Tree::close_overlay` — a real press outside every open overlay's own subtree, or a real `Key::Escape`, closes it; `overlay.rs`'s own stale module doc comment corrected to reflect that real dispatch has existed since M4 — ✅ (wired to `PointerPressed` only, not `PointerReleased`, matching real desktop convention; consumes the dismissing press rather than also clicking through. Two real findings while verifying end to end: `close_overlay` originally destroyed its own content node — never exercised by a live app before this phase — breaking the realistic "dismiss then reopen the same anchor" case, fixed by detaching instead, matching `set_context_menu`'s own existing contract; outside-click dismissal's first draft also wrongly treated a press back on the overlay's own anchor as "outside," fixed by excluding it too. 8 new `engine-core` tests + 1 existing test updated for the real contract change; 2 new pytest tests. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; pytest (85 passed, up from 83, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Same-Tree Safety Checks for `set_context_menu`/`set_dock_handle` (§8) ✅
- Step 1: `Node.set_context_menu` and `dock::set_dock_handle` gain the same `Rc::ptr_eq` same-tree guard `Node.add_child`/`Window.begin_container_transform` already use, rejecting a `Node`/handle from a foreign `Window`'s own `Tree` with a real `EngineError` instead of silently storing a foreign `NodeId` that could alias an unrelated real node the next time it's read back — ✅ (`Node.set_context_menu` checked directly; `dock::set_dock_handle` itself is a free function with no `Node` context of its own, so the check lives at its one real caller, `PyWindow.set_dock_handle`, instead — both widened from `()` to `PyResult<()>`, additive, confirmed via grep no Rust-level caller exists for either. 2 new pytest tests, both passed on the first run; existing tests unmodified. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (no `engine-core` touched); pytest (87 passed, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 3 — Drop-Zone Highlight Overlay for Docking (§11.4) ✅
- Step 1: A real way to position an overlay to cover an arbitrary target rect (a dock zone's own real computed bounds), not just anchor-relative-below — the concrete primitive gap `open_overlay`'s own real implementation confirms is missing — ✅ (new `Tree::position_overlay_over(content, rect)` in `engine-core`, next to `open_overlay`: sets `Position::Absolute` with `inset` from the rect's own origin and an explicit `style.size` matching its width/height, independent of any anchor; deliberately doesn't attach the node or touch `self.overlays` — a rect-covering highlight has no anchor and no outside-click/Escape dismissal, so attach/detach lifecycle is the caller's own. New `engine-core` unit test proves it covers an arbitrary rect and resizes to it, independent of any anchor.)
- Step 2: Wires that primitive into the real drag-in-progress path (`dock::start_drag`/the real `PointerMoved`-during-drag dispatch) so the zone currently under the pointer shows a real, live highlight, closing the milestone — ✅ (new `DockState.highlight: Option<NodeId>`; `set_drop_zone_highlight` registers it, detaching it from root first — the same `set_context_menu`-style "starts hidden" contract, since `add_rect` already attached it to root immediately; new `dock::drag_over` is the real `PointerMoved`-during-drag step this module previously had no need for, reusing `enclosing_zone` unchanged to find the enclosing zone (if any), showing the highlight over it or hiding it if none; `end_drag_at` gains one new unconditional step, before its own existing early returns, that always hides the highlight when a drag ends, for any reason. New `PyWindow.set_drop_zone_highlight` (the same `Rc::ptr_eq` guard M10 Phase 2 added to `set_dock_handle`) and `drag_panel_over`, the same synthetic-dispatch pattern `start_panel_drag`/`drop_panel_at` already establish. **Real finding, not anticipated in `PLAN.md`:** the first pytest draft reused `(200.0, 50.0)` from an existing test as "inside the Right zone" — direct debugging showed that point actually sits exactly on the zone's own flex-shrunk right edge, not inside it, revealing that *pre-existing* test's own "functional proof" doesn't actually discriminate a real move from a same-zone no-op; left as-is, out of this phase's own scope. New tests use `(160.0, 50.0)`, confirmed to land genuinely inside. `examples/docking.py` registers a real highlight; its own doc comment, which named this exact gap, is corrected. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 81, up from 80); pytest (93 passed, up from 87, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 11 — Canvas Authoring Completeness

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** The user chose this direction explicitly over two other real, investigated candidates (Variable-Height `VirtualList` Items — `ItemExtent` still has exactly one real variant, `Fixed`, re-confirmed today via direct read of `node.rs`; `AppShell`/Single-Page Navigation, §11.2 — a newly-surfaced gap, never previously named in any prior milestone's own scoping: ARCHITECTURE.md §11.2 fully specifies an `AppShell` struct with named menu-bar/toolbar/dock/status-bar/content regions, confirmed via grep to have zero implementation anywhere in this codebase, not even a stub). This is the single most-repeatedly-passed-over candidate across M8 and M10's own scoping, confirmed real by direct investigation, not assumed: `stroke_path`/`set_hit_test_path` (`crates/engine-py/src/canvas.rs`) both build their `kurbo::BezPath` through a private `polyline()` helper using only `move_to`/`line_to` — no `quad_to`/`curve_to` exposed to Python at all, confirmed via direct read. The underlying hit-test machinery already generalizes to true curves: `CustomHitTest::Path` stores a full `BezPath`, and `tree.rs` already imports `kurbo::ParamCurveNearest` for its distance-to-path math, which works identically for line/quad/cubic segments — only the *authoring* surface is polyline-only, not the math underneath it. `Window.redraw_canvas` also remains a purely app-triggered `PyWindow` method — confirmed via grep, zero automatic per-frame invocation anywhere in `App::run`'s own frame loop — but whether that's a real gap or a deliberate, correct choice for this framework's retained-mode `Tree` model (an immediate-mode "redraw everything every frame" policy would waste real work for canvas content that hasn't changed) is itself an open question this milestone's own Phase 2 investigates rather than presupposes.

### Phase 1 — True Bezier Curve Authoring (§11.10, §11.11) ✅
- Step 1: `stroke_path`/`set_hit_test_path` gain a real way to author curved segments (quadratic/cubic bezier), not just straight polylines — exposing the same curve vocabulary `kurbo::BezPath` already supports internally and `CustomHitTest::Path`'s own distance math already handles, closing the concrete authoring gap `polyline()`'s own doc comment names (§11.10's own stated "a bezier curve within N pixels of the point" example currently only ever gets a straight-line approximation) — ✅ (`polyline` renamed to `build_path`, widened from `Vec<(f64, f64)>` to `Vec<Vec<f64>>` — 2 numbers is a line-to (fully backward-compatible with every existing call site), 4 is `quad_to`, 6 is `curve_to`, any other length a real `PyValueError`; `stroke_path`/`set_hit_test_path` keep their exact signatures, both still calling the one shared helper. Rejected a custom `FromPyObject` tagged-point enum — pyo3 0.29's own more complex trait shape, real avoidable complexity — in favor of plain arity-dispatch. New `engine-core` unit test proves the resulting hit-test is genuinely curve-aware: a real curve's own true midpoint, 50px from the naive straight chord between its endpoints, hits; a point 2px from that chord but ~48px from the real curve misses — the adversarial pair a chord-only distance would get wrong. New pytest coverage proves the FFI surface accepts real quadratic/cubic segments and rejects invalid point lengths. No `engine-core` production change, only the new test. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 82, up from 81); pytest (96 passed, up from 93, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean, including the three pre-existing `stroke_path` call sites unmodified. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Canvas Redraw Triggering: Investigate and Resolve (§11.10, §11.11) ✅
- Step 1: Determine whether `redraw_canvas`'s explicit, app-triggered-only design is a real gap or a deliberate, correct choice for this framework's retained-mode `Tree` model, and either wire real automatic redraw for whatever specific case genuinely needs it, or formally close the question with a stated, justified "no change needed" — closing the milestone either way — ✅ (confirmed structural, not an oversight: `canvas_draws` never appears in `app.rs`'s own real winit frame loop — not part of `WindowSetup`/`WindowRuntime`, the two structs carrying everything the per-frame closures can see, which only call `Tree::tick_all` and drain completions. `WindowSetup`'s own doc comment already states every Python-callback field threaded into the frame loop is invoked only on a real, specific event, never unconditional per-frame work — adding `canvas_draws` would be the first blanket per-frame Python invocation in this codebase. `ARCHITECTURE.md` names no automatic-redraw requirement. Real cost analysis: unconditional per-frame redraw would impose a real Python call, a fresh `CanvasContext`, and a full `Vec<DrawCommand>` rebuild every frame for every registered canvas regardless of whether content changed — exactly the per-frame overhead this codebase consistently designs against elsewhere. **Resolution: no change needed** — the current app-triggered model is deliberate and correct for a retained-mode `Tree`, mirroring M7 Phase 5 Step 1's own "already existed, nothing new needed" resolution shape. `BUILD_TRACKER.md`'s own M5 carried-forward gap note corrected to record this. No production code change; full suite/clippy/fmt/pytest/all examples re-confirmed clean as a formality. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 12 — Variable-Height VirtualList Items

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** The user chose this direction explicitly over two other real, investigated candidates (Form Components & Two-Way Data Binding, §16.7 — a newly-surfaced, substantial gap: ARCHITECTURE.md §16.7 fully specifies two-way binding sugar (a `TextField`'s text auto-writing back to its bound `Signal`), entirely unimplemented, confirmed via grep zero hits for "two_way"/"TwoWay" anywhere in `engine-spec`/`engine-py`; can't be built yet regardless, since the `NodeKind`s it needs (`TextField`/`Slider`/`Checkbox`) don't exist at all (confirmed via grep) and `EventKind` (`input.rs`) still has only `Click`/`HoverEnter`/`HoverExit`, no `Change` variant — real, but substantially larger in scope than the other two candidates, new MD3 components rather than wiring existing data; `AppShell`/Single-Page Navigation, §11.2 — confirmed via grep to still have zero implementation anywhere, despite ARCHITECTURE.md's own detailed struct sketch). This is the single most-repeatedly-passed-over candidate of all three real M12 candidates investigated, confirmed real by direct investigation, not assumed: `ItemExtent` (`crates/engine-core/src/node.rs`) still has exactly one real variant, `Fixed(f64)`, re-confirmed today via direct read — its own doc comment already states the architectural intent verbatim, unchanged since M8: "fixed, or a size-hint callback for variable-height items... no consumer needs the callback-based variant yet." Two real call sites in `tree.rs` still assume uniform spacing, confirmed via direct read: `set_virtual_list_window`'s own materialization math positions every item at `idx as f64 * item_extent` (line 816), and the M8 Phase 3 scroll-clamping math computes total scrollable content extent as `item_extent.value() * item_count` (line 850) — both are real, concrete primitive gaps a variable per-item height breaks, not just a config-surface limitation. `engine-py::PyWindow.add_virtual_list`/`set_virtual_list_window` (`window.rs`) both only expose `item_extent: f64`, and `set_virtual_list_window`'s own `let ItemExtent::Fixed(v) = state.item_extent;` (line 838) is an irrefutable pattern only because `ItemExtent` has exactly one variant today — the exact spot a second variant would force a real match arm.

### Phase 1 — Real Variable-Height Extent Support in `engine-core` (§11.7) ✅
- Step 1: `ItemExtent` gains a real way to represent variable, per-item extents (not a callback stored inside `engine-core` itself — §4's own pyo3-agnostic boundary rules that out, the same real reason `materialize` lives in `engine-py`, not here); `VirtualListState`'s own materialization math (`idx * item_extent`) and the M8 Phase 3 scroll-clamping math (`item_extent * item_count`) both generalize to real per-item cumulative sums over whatever extents are actually known, closing the concrete primitive gap `ItemExtent`'s own doc comment has named since M8 — ✅ (`ItemExtent::Variable` — a fieldless marker; the real per-item data lives on new `VirtualListState.resolved_offsets: BTreeMap<usize, f64>`, storing already-*cumulative* offsets, not raw heights, so `engine-core` never sums anything itself, only looks a resolved value up — key `item_count` holds the real total extent. New `VirtualListState::offset_of`/`total_extent` are the single shared source of truth both `tree.rs` call sites now use, replacing the old uniform formulas; `ItemExtent::value()` removed, its two callers rewritten. New `Tree::set_virtual_list_resolved_offsets` is Phase 2's own entry point into this storage. New tests: `Fixed` behavior unchanged; a real, non-uniform resolved-offset set proves real per-item positioning and total-extent clamping for `Variable`; a `#[should_panic]` test proves an unresolved index panics clearly. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 86, up from 82); pytest (96 passed, 1 skipped, unchanged) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean — a pure regression check, this phase touches no Python-facing behavior. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Python-Facing Size-Hint Callback (§11.7) ✅
- Step 1: `Window.add_virtual_list`/`set_virtual_list_window` gain a real way to supply per-item heights from Python — a size-hint callback, mirroring `materialize`'s own per-index "resolve once, cache" callback shape (`materializers: HashMap<NodeId, Py<PyAny>>`) rather than inventing a second storage mechanism — wiring Phase 1's new `engine-core` capability through to real app code, closing the milestone — ✅ (new `size_hint: Optional[Callable[[int], float]]`, `item_extent` now optional — exactly one of the two required, a real `ValueError` otherwise. Resolves *eagerly, once*, at `add_virtual_list` time — calling `size_hint` exactly `item_count` times immediately, building the complete cumulative-offset table via `Tree::set_virtual_list_resolved_offsets` — a real, deliberate, stated `O(item_count)` cost, not a lazy incremental cache (real avoidable complexity this milestone's own one-line architecture text doesn't ask for). New private `ResolvedItemHeights` snapshot feeds `set_virtual_list_window`'s materializer closure each item's own real height (the difference between two adjacent resolved cumulative offsets) — extracted up front, mirroring the pre-existing scalar-extraction shape, since the closure can't also borrow `tree` live from inside itself. New pytest coverage: `size_hint` called exactly once per item, in order, at `add_virtual_list` time, never re-invoked by `set_virtual_list_window`; both/neither-given error paths; exception propagation, matching `materialize`'s own contract. New `examples/variable_height_list.py`: a real, live 500-row list with a genuinely non-uniform, repeating five-step row-height pattern, scrolled for real. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings` (one real `too_many_arguments` lint, resolved with a scoped, justified `#[allow]`)/fmt clean (no `engine-core` change, `engine-py`-only); pytest (100 passed, up from 96, 1 skipped) and all seventeen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 13 — AppShell / Single-Page Navigation (§11.2)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** Scoped together with M14 at the user's own explicit request ("scope milestone 13 and 14"); the user picked both directions from three real, investigated candidates (this one and Form Components & Two-Way Data Binding, both presented as recommended defaults and confirmed; a third, structured logging via `tracing`, §3/§9, was investigated but not chosen for either slot — see M12's own closing "Up next" paragraph). Confirmed real by direct investigation, not assumed: `AppShell` (ARCHITECTURE.md §11.2) is fully specified — a persistent root layout with named `menu_bar`/`toolbar`/`dock`/`status_bar` regions and exactly one stable `content` region, where "navigating" means replacing `content`'s own children, an ordinary, already-supported tree mutation (`Tree::remove`/`add_child`, both real since M3/M6) — but has zero implementation anywhere, confirmed via grep (zero hits for `AppShell` in any crate or in this file's own history). Two real, concrete gaps the implementation will need to resolve, confirmed via direct read: `PyWindow` has no Python-facing way to create a plain `NodeKind::Container` at all — only `Container` in this whole codebase is the window's own root, built once in `PyWindow::new` (`window.rs:219`) — though `Window.add_rect` with a transparent background is already the established practical substitute apps use for a plain layout box (confirmed via every docking/canvas example's own `background=(0, 0, 0, 0)` pattern); and the window's own root `Style` is hardcoded `FlexDirection::Row` (`window.rs`, `PyWindow::new`), which conflicts with a real header/content/footer *column* shell layout, so shell composition needs its own dedicated sub-container, not a reuse of the root's own direction. §11.2's own text confirms no new `engine-core` primitive is needed at all — `taffy`'s existing flexbox and the already-real `add_child`/`remove` cover everything specified; this is purely a Python-facing composition + navigation feature.

### Phase 1 — Real AppShell Composition (§11.2) ✅
- Step 1: A real `Window`-level way to build the shell's named regions (menu bar, toolbar, status bar, and the stable `content` swap region) in one call, as ordinary flex-composed containers positioned by a real header/content/footer column layout — reusing `add_rect`'s own established transparent-container pattern, no new `engine-core` primitive (confirmed by investigation), closing the concrete "fully specified, zero implementation" gap — ✅ (new `Window.build_shell(menu_bar=None, toolbar=None, status_bar=None) -> Node`, returning `content`. **Real finding, caught live by `examples/app_shell.py`'s own `accesskit` validation, not pytest:** the first draft re-parented already-attached regions with the cheap `Tree::add_child`, leaving each listed as a child of both its old parent and the new shell — fixed with `Tree::try_add_child`, which detaches first. New `tests/test_app_shell.py` (4 tests) + new `examples/app_shell.py`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; pytest (104 passed, up from 100, 1 skipped) and all eighteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Real Content Navigation (§11.2, optionally §7.6) ✅
- Step 1: A real `Window`-level "navigate" operation — replaces `content`'s own children for real (`Tree::remove` + `add_child`, both already real), optionally choreographed through the already-real `begin_container_transform`/`end_container_transform` (§7.6) for a cross-fade/transform transition between screens, matching §11.2's own "not a general multi-screen router... matching the single-page-app model" text — closing the milestone — ✅ (new `Node.remove()`, mirroring `add_child`'s own minimal shape, calling the already-real `Tree::remove`. New `tests/test_remove.py`: a genuine detach proven via a new sibling taking the same real position; removal of a subtree doesn't corrupt the rest of the tree. New `examples/navigation.py`: two real screens built into a real `AppShell`'s own `content`, navigated between via remove+add, proven by a real dispatched click landing on the second screen's own card. Deliberately doesn't combine this with `begin_container_transform` in the same script — `container_transform.py` already proves that mechanism on its own. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (no `engine-core` change); pytest (106 passed, up from 104, 1 skipped) and all nineteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 1 — Real Checkbox (§5, §7.3) ✅
- Step 1: `NodeKind::Checkbox(CheckboxState)` with real paint (a checkmark draw path, animated via `check_progress`) and real click-to-toggle interaction reusing the already-real `Click` dispatch and `Node.enable_interaction()`'s own ripple/state-layer mechanism — ✅ (`CheckboxState { checked: bool, check_progress: Animated<f64> }`; `tick_all` gains its first-ever kind-specific payload tick; `build_access_update` derives real `Toggled` directly from `checked`, no duplicated field. `paint_node` strokes a real checkmark `BezPath`, opacity driven by `check_progress` (plain white, not yet theme-aware). `Node.animate`/`.get` gain their first real kind-payload dispatch arm (`"check_progress"`); new `Node.set_checked(bool)`; new `Window.add_checkbox`. ### Phase 3 — Real `Change` EventKind + Two-Way Binding Sugar (§16.7) ✅
- Step 1: `EventKind` gains `Change`, dispatched by `Checkbox`/`Slider` on a real, genuine edit (a toggle, a drag release) — ✅ (`EventKind::Change`/`DispatchOutcome::Changed(NodeId)`, fired two ways per Design Principle 6: mechanically inside `Tree::dispatch`'s `PointerReleased` arm for a real Slider drag-release, checked before `self.dragging` clears; directly by new `Node.set_checked` via `call_handler` (widened to `pub(crate)`) for a Checkbox, since `engine-core` never touches `checked` itself. New `Node.get_checked`/`Node.set_on_change`. New `engine-core` tests (`dispatch_release_ending_a_real_slider_drag_produces_changed`, `dispatch_release_with_no_slider_drag_in_progress_never_produces_changed`) prove the mechanical path; new `tests/test_change_event.py` (5 tests) proves the direct path and FFI wiring.)
- Step 2: `engine-spec`'s `_attach` (§16.2) gains real `two_way: true` binding sugar (§16.7) for `Checkbox`/`Slider`'s own natural edit event — a one-way binding plus an auto-generated `Change` handler that writes the new value back into the bound `Signal`, no new dispatch path — closing the milestone (`TextField` explicitly out of scope, per this milestone's own status line) — ✅ (real, additional scope: `WidgetSpec.checked`/`value`/`two_way` + `NodeKindSpec::Checkbox`/`Slider` make them declarable in `view.yaml` for the first time, needed since two-way binding is a `View`-only concept. `apply_binding_value` gains a `Value::Bool` branch calling `set_checked`; new `TwoWayCallback` pyclass registers as the widget's own `Change` handler, reading the node's value back and writing it into the bound `Signal`. **Real finding #1:** the two-way expression must be a Signal's own `.get()` call (`Expression::Call(Expression::Ident(name), "get")`), not a bare identifier as ARCHITECTURE.md's own illustration shows — a bare identifier resolves to the raw `Signal` object itself, which `apply_binding_value` can't apply forward at all; only caught once the round trip was actually tested. **Real finding #2:** with #1 fixed, a real round trip recursed infinitely (`set_checked` → `Change` → `TwoWayCallback` → `Signal.set` → unconditional notify → the widget's own forward binding → `set_checked` again → ...), each level silently caught and printed by the pre-existing non-fatal-handler policy — only caught by actually running `examples/two_way_binding.py` end to end, not by any unit test. Fixed at the general root cause: `Signal.set`/`Signal.update` now skip notification when the value is unchanged. New `tests/test_two_way_binding.py` (5 tests, incl. a dedicated stderr-must-stay-clean regression test) + 2 new `Signal`-only tests in `tests/test_view_binding.py`; new `examples/two_way_binding.py` + `two_way_binding.yaml`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 94, up from 92; `engine-spec` 33, up from 29); pytest (131 passed, up from 119, 1 skipped) and all twenty-two examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)
