### Phase 1 — Real Slider ArrowLeft/ArrowRight Increment (§10) ✅
- Step 1: A focused `NodeKind::Slider` responds to `Key::ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight` by nudging `thumb_position` a real, fixed step, clamped and applied through the identical `Tree::set_slider_position` mechanism a real drag already uses (immediate, `Duration::ZERO` manual tick) — closing the milestone — ✅ (new `Tree::dispatch_slider_key` mirrors `dispatch_text_field_key`'s own "focused node gets first refusal, `None` means not mine" contract, so `Tab`/`Enter`/`Escape` still fall through for a focused slider too; returns `DispatchOutcome::Changed`, the identical outcome a real drag-release already produces, so a two-way `bindings: {value: ...}` write-back picks it up for free. Fixed `0.05` step. **Real, necessary connected fix, found only by actually trying the feature end to end from Python, not assumed:** `dispatch_slider_key` is only reachable when a slider is genuinely focused, but `Tree::collect_interactive`'s own real Tab-order predicate ("any node with a non-empty `access.actions`") never included a `Slider` at all — `Node.enable_interaction()` only ever sets ripple/hover tint, never touches `access.actions`, so a `Slider` could never actually receive keyboard focus through the ordinary Tab mechanism before this. Fixed by giving `Window.add_slider` the identical real `AccessNodeData::new(Role::Slider).with_action(Action::Focus)` construction-time opt-in `add_text_field` already has — §10's own "keyboard operability ships from day one" text, now actually true for `Slider` too. New `engine-core` tests (4): `ArrowRight`/`ArrowLeft` nudge and return `Changed`; a press at the real `0.0` minimum clamps; the same keys with no slider focused remain the real, already-established no-op. Updated `examples/slider.py`: a real Tab press (now automatic at construction) focuses the slider, two real `ArrowRight` presses nudge it, the resulting value read back and checked stable across a full `App.run` loop. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 144, up from 140); `maturin develop --release` + pytest (187 passed, unchanged, 1 pre-existing skip) and all thirty-two examples confirmed clean — the same two pre-existing, unrelated `on_complete` failures already flagged separately, no new regressions. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

**M24 — Slider Keyboard Increments is now fully complete.** ARCHITECTURE.md §10's own real, explicitly-deferred "when each such component is actually built" gap for `Slider` is closed — keyboard operability (WCAG's own baseline requirement) now genuinely reaches every real interactive component this project has built (`TextField`'s own keyboard editing, M15; `Slider`'s own arrow-key increments, this milestone). The single real finding, in this milestone's own small way the same "verify empirically, don't just implement and assume" discipline M22's GPU-texture pivot and M23's icon investigation both already established: the core dispatch logic worked correctly on the first real test run, but was silently unreachable from any real running Python app until the connected `access.actions` gap — invisible from reading `Tree::dispatch` in isolation — was found by actually running the feature end to end.

**Up next:** M25 — resolving every real, currently-known gap in this workspace as its own phase, per the user's own explicit instruction (mobile check-in, 2026-09-17): "Let's resolve all known gaps, make it milestone 25 and each gap will be a phase." Two real, distinct gaps, confirmed via direct investigation before scoping either: (1) a real, pre-existing bug flagged during M22 but never fixed — `examples/animation_completion.py`/`container_transform.py` both assert a real animation's `on_complete` callback fires exactly once and it never does, confirmed via a real git worktree at the M21-closing commit that this predates every milestone built this session; (2) a real, newly-found systemic gap, discovered while answering the user's own "what's been done" question and re-auditing the render pipeline for anything still incomplete — `PaintProperties.opacity` (the one universal "how visible is this whole node" field every `NodeKind` shares) is silently ignored by six real paint sites: `NodeKind::Canvas`'s own `DrawCommand::FillRect`/`FillCircle`/`StrokePath` (raw color, no `with_opacity` at all); `NodeKind::Image`'s `draw_texture_rects` call (no opacity parameter used at all); `NodeKind::Slider`'s own track fill (only the thumb multiplies by `node.paint.opacity.current`, confirmed via direct read — a real internal inconsistency within one `NodeKind`); `NodeKind::Checkbox`'s own checkmark stroke (multiplies only by `check_progress`, never compounded with `node.paint.opacity.current`, so a checked checkbox mid-fade-out would show its checkmark at full alpha while its own box fades); the real elevation/shadow painting (`shadow_color(0.15)`/`shadow_color(0.3)`, both fixed MD3 alphas, never multiplied by the node's own opacity at all); and the ripple/hover interaction overlay (`hover_opacity`/each ripple's own `opacity`, never compounded with `node.paint.opacity.current` either). Every one of the six shares the identical real root cause and fix shape (multiply the existing color/alpha by `node.paint.opacity.current` before painting, the same pattern `Rect`/`Splitter`/`Checkbox`'s own box fill/`Slider`'s own thumb/`TextField`/`Icon` already established correctly) — scoped as one phase, not six, since splitting one root cause into six near-identical phases would be artificial busywork this codebase's own "don't overengineer process" discipline argues against; the `on_complete` bug is a genuinely separate subsystem (animation-completion draining, not paint), so it gets its own phase.

## Milestone 25 — Known Gap Resolution

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** Directly user-requested (mobile check-in, not the standing autonomous-development authorization this project has otherwise operated under since M20) — see the closing "Up next" paragraph on M24's own section, immediately above, for the full real investigation this scoping is based on.

### Phase 1 — Real Animation Completion Callback Firing (§5, §9) ✅
- Step 1: Diagnose and fix why a real, non-zero-and-zero-duration `Animated<T>` completion never reaches its registered `on_complete` callback in this dev environment, despite `engine-core`'s own `animation.rs` unit tests for zero-duration completion passing — real root cause likely somewhere in `engine-py::App::run`'s own per-frame `Tree::tick_all` → completion-drain → callback-invoke wiring, not `engine-core`'s own `Animated<T>`/`CompletionHandle` mechanism itself (confirmed real and unit-tested since M9) — ✅ (**real finding: there was no engine bug at all.** Traced the full real call chain directly — `Node.animate`'s registration, `Animated::animate_to_with_completion`, `Animated::tick`'s own real `elapsed >= duration` push into `completed`, `app.rs`'s own `run_completions` call — every link was already correct. The real root cause was this *session's own* test-running convention: every example this session was run via `env -u DISPLAY -u WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, stripping a real, working display this sandboxed environment actually has (`DISPLAY=:0`, a live `/tmp/.X11-unix/X0`, confirmed via direct check) — `winit::EventLoop::build()` then fails, and `App::run`'s own real, deliberate, already-correct "gracefully exit `Ok(())` with no display reachable" handling (TRE v1 finding #261's own convention) silently converted that into a zero-real-frames successful return. `animation_completion.py`/`container_transform.py` are the only two example scripts in this workspace that assert on a value only a real per-frame `tick_all` call produces — the one place this was ever visible, not the one thing that was broken. Re-verified with the real display available: both scripts, and all thirty-two examples, now pass with genuine per-frame execution. Confirmed this never touched CI at all — `ci.yml` deliberately has no display (its own header comment) and only ever runs `examples/animate_rect.py`, whose own doc comment already states it exits 0 gracefully with no display and never asserts on a ticked value. No `engine-core`/`engine-py`/`engine-render` code changed — the real fix was correcting this session's own verification methodology. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Real `PaintProperties.opacity` Compounding Across Every Remaining Paint Site (§5, §6) ✅
- Step 1: `NodeKind::Canvas`'s own `DrawCommand::FillRect`/`FillCircle`/`StrokePath`, `NodeKind::Image`'s GPU-texture paint, `NodeKind::Slider`'s own track fill, `NodeKind::Checkbox`'s own checkmark stroke, elevation/shadow painting, and the ripple/hover interaction overlay all multiply their own real color/alpha by `node.paint.opacity.current` before painting — the same real compounding `Rect`/`Splitter`/`Checkbox`'s own box fill/`Slider`'s own thumb/`TextField`/`Icon` already established correctly, closing the milestone — ✅ (`Canvas`/`Slider` track: `with_opacity(*color, node.paint.opacity.current)`. `Checkbox`'s checkmark: `check_progress.current * node.paint.opacity.current` compounded together, two independent real alpha sources multiplied. Elevation/shadow: both `shadow_color` calls multiplied by `node.paint.opacity.current`, now also skipped entirely at `opacity <= 0.0`. Ripple/hover: `hover_opacity`/each `ripple.opacity` each multiplied by `node.paint.opacity.current`. **Real, necessary design decision for `Image`:** `Scene::draw_texture_rects` has no opacity parameter of its own at all (confirmed via direct source read) — wrapped the call in `scene.push_layer(None, None, Some(opacity), None, None)`/`pop_layer()` instead, the identical real "opacity-only layer" mechanism the ripple/hover overlay already relies on. Six new pixel tests, one per site, each proving real compounding (not "doesn't crash"): `canvas_paint.rs`/`image_paint.rs`/`slider_paint.rs` use the same real range-check pattern `animated_rect.rs`'s own mid-flight test already established; `checkbox_paint.rs` proves the checkmark paints genuinely different pixels at opacity 1.0 vs. 0.5; `elevation_shadow.rs` proves a fully faded elevated node casts no shadow; `ripple_hover_dispatch.rs` proves a fully faded node's own ripple is completely invisible. Every pre-existing test passed unmodified — every node's own real default `opacity = 1.0` makes each new multiplication a true no-op. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; `maturin develop --release` + pytest (187 passed, unchanged, 1 pre-existing skip) and all thirty-two examples confirmed clean with the real display (the corrected convention from Phase 1). See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

**M25 — Known Gap Resolution is now fully complete.** Both phases resolved every real, currently-known gap in this workspace — a false-alarm test-methodology bug corrected (Phase 1: no engine code changed, only this session's own verification habit) and a genuine, previously-unnoticed rendering-fidelity gap closed across six real paint sites (Phase 2). The single real theme tying both phases together: neither gap was found by reading code in isolation — Phase 1's real root cause only surfaced by adding `RUST_LOG=warn` and reading the actual runtime clue; Phase 2's six sites were found by systematically auditing every real `scene.set_paint` call site in `paint_node` against the one universal field (`opacity`) every `NodeKind` shares, not by guessing which kinds might be affected.

Updated `docs/guide/declarative-views.md` (replaced the "not wired up yet" admonition with real documentation) and `docs/api/python/view.md` (new constructor signature). ## Milestone 28 — Code Review Follow-Through

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 3 phases done (2026-09-18).** Directly user-requested ("Let's fix these user input required issues") after a `/review-project` multi-agent code review pass identified 5 confirmed medium/high findings, autonomously fixed 2 of them (a real GPU texture leak, two duplicated-logic patterns), and flagged 3 as genuinely needing a human design call rather than a rushed autonomous fix: a missing text-shaping cache combined with an unconditional per-frame redraw loop (Performance), `engine-py::window.rs`'s 1712 lines mixing 4 largely-independent responsibilities (Architecture), and `Tree::remove` not cleaning up `self.overlays` bookkeeping for removed overlay content (Performance, low severity). Each phase below closes one of those three, using the judgment call the review's own report proposed as trade-offs — not a second delegation back to the user, since the standing authorization already covers making these calls directly.

### Phase 1 — Text-Shaping Cache & Overlay-Cleanup Leak (§5, §6, §11.3) ✅
- Step 1: `engine-render::TextRenderer` gains a real per-`NodeId` shaped-`Layout` cache (`engine-render/src/text.rs`) — every `Text`/`TextField` paint used to re-run the full `parley` shaping pipeline (font matching, line-breaking, BiDi, alignment) from scratch on every single frame regardless of whether anything changed. **Real design finding that changed the original trade-off, confirmed by direct investigation before implementing, not assumed:** the review's own report framed a shaping cache as the *riskier* half (manual invalidation) and dirty-gating the redraw loop as the *safer* half — re-investigating `Tree::tick_all`'s own `any_active` return value (already computed every frame, already covering every animated property) showed the opposite is true here. A shaping cache keyed on its own exact inputs (`content`/`font_family`/`font_weight`/`font_size`/`max_width`) needs **no separate invalidation logic at all** — a changed key *is* the cache miss, so there's nothing to forget to invalidate. Correctly gating the redraw loop, by contrast, would need a real dirty-tracking mechanism threaded through every one of `engine-py`'s ~30–40 mutating `PyWindow`/`Node` methods (`tick_all`'s `any_active` alone misses one-shot property writes, `TextField` typing, scroll, drag, focus, and every imperative `add_*`/`set_*` call) — a genuine architectural change to the core render loop every example and the showcase demo depends on, correctly still left for Items Requiring User Input below rather than rushed. Keyed by `NodeId` (not by content string), so cache size tracks the tree's own text-node count, not how many distinct strings a live-updating label has ever shown; `TextRenderer::evict_stale_layouts(&tree)` mirrors `ImageTextureCache::sync`'s own already-fixed eviction pattern (a `NodeId` no longer in the tree is a safe, `slotmap`-generational staleness check), called once per frame from `App::run` right alongside `sync_image_textures`. New `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` in `text.rs` itself (the module is private, so an external integration test can't reach `TextRenderer`'s internals the way `image_cache.rs`'s own test already established the pattern for): proves repainting an unchanged node doesn't grow the cache, a genuinely different node gets its own entry, and removing a node evicts its cached layout. — ✅
- Step 2: `Tree::remove` (`engine-core/src/tree.rs`) now also clears `self.overlays` for the removed id — previously only `close_overlay` ever did, so removing open overlay content through the general-purpose `remove` (or removing one of its ancestors, reached via `remove`'s own recursion into children) left a permanently orphaned `OverlayMeta` entry behind. A plain `HashMap::remove`, so this costs nothing on the (vast majority) common path of removing a node that was never overlay content. New test proves both the direct-removal and removed-ancestor cases. — ✅
- ✅ (Full `cargo test --workspace --release`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean, including both new regression tests (`engine-core` 145 tests, up from 144; `engine-render` 3 unit tests, up from 2). `maturin develop --release` + pytest (187 passed, unchanged, 1 pre-existing skip), all 29 pre-existing examples, and the showcase demo confirmed clean with the real display. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — `window.rs` Split by Responsibility (§4, §8) ✅
- Step 1: `engine-py/src/window.rs` (1712 lines, review-flagged for mixing node-factory methods, synthetic input dispatch, docking delegation, and virtual-list/canvas plumbing in one file) split into `window.rs` (construction/theming/GC lifecycle, kept), `window_factory.rs` (9 `add_*`/`build_shell` methods), `window_input.rs` (11 synthetic-dispatch methods: click/hover/scroll/right_click/press_key/type_text/copy/cut/paste/container-transform), `window_docking.rs` (8 thin `dock.rs` delegation wrappers), and `window_virtual_canvas.rs` (4 methods: `add_virtual_list`/`set_virtual_list_window`/`add_canvas`/`redraw_canvas`) — ✅ (**Real, necessary mechanism, verified before committing to the plan, not assumed:** `PyWindow`'s single `#[pymethods] impl` block can't itself span multiple files without pyo3's own `multiple-pymethods` Cargo feature (confirmed via direct source read of the vendored `pyo3-0.27.2` crate before enabling it on the real `0.29.2` dependency — its own doc comment states the only real cost is `inventory`-based registration not supporting Wasm, which this desktop-only winit/wgpu project never targets). Enabled in `engine-py/Cargo.toml`; every extracted method's own real body moved verbatim (mechanical Python-scripted line-range extraction against exact grep-verified boundaries, not retyped by hand), each new file's own `use` block built from scratch and corrected entirely by the compiler's own missing/unused-import diagnostics rather than guessed. `positioned_style` (shared by both `window_factory.rs`'s seven `add_*` methods and `window_virtual_canvas.rs`'s `add_canvas`) stays `pub(crate)` in `window.rs` itself, belonging to neither group more than the other; `parse_content_fit` (used only by `add_image`) and `ResolvedItemHeights` (used only by `set_virtual_list_window`) each moved fully into their one real caller's file instead. `wrap_node` and the `materializers`/`canvas_draws` fields widened from private to `pub(crate)` — the minimum visibility change needed for the split files to reach them, `PyWindow`'s own already-established field-level `pub(crate)` convention extended, not a new pattern invented. Zero behavior change: every Python-visible method name, signature, and body is byte-for-byte the same code, just relocated — confirmed by the full pytest suite, every example, and the showcase demo all passing unmodified.)
- ✅ (Full `cargo check`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean across all 5 files. `maturin develop --release` + pytest (187 passed, unchanged, 1 pre-existing skip), all 29 examples, and the showcase demo confirmed clean with the real display — the real proof that pyo3 genuinely merges all 5 `#[pymethods]` blocks into one Python-visible `Window` class, not five. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 3 — Verification & Documentation ✅
- Step 1: Full verification chain run once across both phases together (not per-phase in isolation, since Phase 2's file split touches every method Phase 1's new cache/eviction calls are threaded through): `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test --workspace --release`, `maturin develop --release`, `pytest tests/`, all 29 examples, and `demo/showcase.py` — all clean. `BUILD_TRACKER.md`/`PLAN.md`/`LOG.md` updated per this project's own Documentation Policy; memory MCP updated per its own Memory Policy. — ✅

**M28 — Code Review Follow-Through is now fully complete.** All 5 confirmed medium/high review findings are resolved: 2 fixed autonomously in the review pass itself (GPU texture leak, duplicated-logic extraction), and the 3 flagged for human input are now closed too — 2 fully fixed (text-shaping cache + overlay-cleanup leak, window.rs split), with the redraw-loop half of the performance finding correctly re-scoped and left open rather than rushed, once direct investigation showed it needs a genuine architectural change (a real dirty-tracking mechanism across ~30–40 mutating methods) that a shaping cache alone does not.

## Milestone 29 — Render Loop Dirty-Tracking (§5, §6)

**Status: ✅ Complete — both phases done (2026-09-18).** User-requested ("Scope it out as the next milestone", then "Start phase 1 and 2") to close the one item M28 deliberately left open: `engine-platform`'s render loop redraws every frame unconditionally (`ControlFlow::Poll` + an unconditional `request_redraw()` in the `RedrawRequested` handler), regardless of whether anything actually changed. M28's own investigation flagged this as needing "a real dirty-tracking mechanism threaded through ~30–40 mutating `engine-py` methods" — a fresh, deeper investigation before scoping this milestone found that framing was too pessimistic, and the real design was considerably lower-risk than that made it sound.

**The real finding that reshapes this milestone's scope, confirmed by direct source read, not assumed:** `Tree::nodes` (`engine-core/src/tree.rs`) is a private field — no code outside `tree.rs` itself can touch a `Node`'s paint/kind state directly, at all. Every real mutation, from every layer above it (`engine-py`'s ~30–40 `PyWindow`/`Node` methods, `engine-spec`'s reconciler, `dock.rs`'s docking logic), is already forced through one of `Tree`'s own roughly 25 public `&mut self` methods (`insert`, `add_child`/`try_add_child`/`detach`, `get_mut` — the sole chokepoint for `Node.animate()`/`set_checked`/`set_text`/`enable_interaction`'s own raw field writes — `remove`, `set_layout_style`, `open_overlay`/`close_overlay`/`position_overlay_over`, `apply_active_tab`, `set_splitter_position`/`set_slider_position`/`scroll_virtual_list_by`, `set_virtual_list_window`, `set_canvas_content`, `set_access`, `interaction_mut`, `set_all_interaction_tints`/`set_all_component_tints`, `set_focused`, `dispatch`, `cut_text_field_selection`/`set_text_field_cursor`/`extend_text_field_selection`, and `tick_all`, whose own `any_active` return value already reports live animation progress). Spot-checked the two real cases that looked like they might bypass `Tree` — docking (`dock.rs` owns a separate `SharedDockState`) and theming (`window.rs` owns a separate `ThemeState`) — both confirmed, by reading their real call sites in `window_docking.rs`/`window.rs`, to always also touch `Tree` in the same call (`dock::dock_panel`/`set_active_tab` take `&self.tree` directly; `Window.set_theme` unconditionally calls `tree.set_all_interaction_tints`/`set_all_component_tints`). **Net effect: a dirty flag instrumented once, centrally, across `Tree`'s own ~25-method public surface — all in one file — covers the real mutation surface, not ~30–40 call sites scattered across `engine-py`.**

Split into two phases by risk, not by feature, since they're genuinely separable and the second is materially riskier than the first:

### Phase 1 — Centralized Dirty Flag & Skip Idle Frame Work ✅
- Step 1: Add `Tree`'s own dirty flag and its read-and-reset accessor, set by every real mutation — ✅ (`Tree` gained a private `dirty: bool` field, starting `true` so the very first frame always paints, and `pub fn take_dirty(&mut self) -> bool` (`std::mem::replace`-based). All 29 real mutating methods (one more than the ~25 estimated while scoping — the full enumeration found `try_add_child`/`insert` were separate from `add_child`, and `set_virtual_list_resolved_offsets` was missed the first pass) set `self.dirty = true` as literally their first statement — mechanically inserted via a small Python script against exact paren-balanced signature boundaries, not hand-edited one at a time, to rule out a missed or misplaced call site across so many methods. `tick_all` sets it whenever its own `any_active` is `true`, reusing the value it already computes.)
- Step 2: Skip real per-frame work when nothing is dirty — ✅ (`engine-py::App::run`'s per-frame closure, `app.rs`, reads `tree.take_dirty()` once per frame, after `tick_all` and before `compute_layout`; when `false`, skips `compute_layout`/`build_tree_scene`/GPU encode+submit+present entirely and returns early. `ControlFlow::Poll` stayed unchanged for this step, as scoped. **Real, scope-simplifying finding:** window resize (`WindowEvent::Resized`) turned out not to need special-casing here at all — confirmed via grep, this codebase has no resize handling anywhere yet (a real, pre-existing, separate gap, not something this milestone introduces or needs to preserve).)
- Step 3: Regression coverage — ✅ (new `engine-core` test `take_dirty_reports_true_after_each_real_mutation_category_and_false_between` proves `take_dirty()` is `true` after a structural mutation (`insert`), a paint-property mutation via the `get_mut` chokepoint, an interaction mutation via `interaction_mut`, and a mid-flight `tick_all`, and `false` between each pair and after read-only calls (`get`/`focused`). `engine-core` test count: 146, up from 145.)

### Phase 2 — True Idle via `ControlFlow::Wait` ✅
- Step 1: Switch the event loop to Wait-when-idle, wire explicit redraw requests into every real input path — ✅ (`run_windowed_multi`'s `on_frame` bound widened to `FnMut(WindowId, u32) -> bool` (`true` = "still genuinely animating, keep polling me"); `PerWindow` gained an `animating: bool` field, and `RedrawRequested` now sets `event_loop.set_control_flow(...)` to `Poll` while *any* open window last reported animating, `Wait` once every open window has settled (`ControlFlow` is event-loop-wide, not per-window, with more than one window open). Every real input-handling arm in `window_event` (`CursorMoved`, `MouseInput`, `KeyboardInput`, `MouseWheel`, `ThemeChanged`, `Ime`) now calls `win.window.request_redraw()` unconditionally after dispatching — `WindowEvent::Resized`/`ScaleFactorChanged` need no wiring since neither is handled at all yet, same finding as Phase 1 Step 2. `run_windowed`'s own public signature (`rect_window.rs`/`access_button.rs`'s two existing callers) stayed byte-for-byte unchanged — its internal wrapper always reports `true`, preserving their pre-M29 always-polling behavior exactly.)
- Step 2: Audit every non-`WindowEvent` redraw trigger — ✅ (found and closed the one real gap this step's own scoping named: a screen-reader-driven AccessKit `Action::Focus`/`Action::Click` (`ActionRequested`, delivered via `user_event`, not `window_event`) had no redraw wiring at all — added an explicit `request_redraw()` on the target window right after invoking `on_access_action`, so an assistive-technology-driven action still paints its own result under `ControlFlow::Wait`.)
- **Real bug found and fixed only by actually running every example against this change, not assumed:** a window opened with `max_frames: Some(_)` — this codebase's own dominant example/test pattern, "run exactly N frames then exit" — hung indefinitely under the first real implementation, since nothing kept requesting its next redraw once `on_frame` reported "not animating," so it never reached its own frame count at all. Confirmed the hard way: several examples timed out running the real workspace verification pass. Fixed by having `RedrawRequested` treat any window with `max_frames: Some(_)` as always-animating for `ControlFlow` purposes regardless of what `on_frame` itself reports — a bounded run needs to guarantee finishing in bounded wall-clock time; only a genuinely unbounded window (`max_frames: None`, a real interactive app) gets Phase 2's idle-CPU benefit at all. Documented directly in `run_windowed_multi`'s own doc comment so this doesn't get silently re-broken later.
- **Real, empirical idle-CPU verification, not assumed:** a scratch script opening a genuinely unbounded (`max_frames=None`) static window with no animation settled to ~0.8–1.3% CPU over several real seconds of wall-clock time with a real display attached (`ps -o pcpu`), consistent with `ControlFlow::Wait` genuinely taking effect rather than continuing to spin.

**Not scoped by this milestone:** partial/incremental repaint (only redrawing the changed region of the screen) — a materially larger change to `engine-render`'s own whole-tree paint walk, and not what "stop burning GPU on an idle screen" actually needs; this milestone's own dirty flag is coarse (whole-frame yes/no), matching the existing architecture's own already-whole-tree-per-frame paint model. Window resize handling itself (`WindowEvent::Resized`) also stays out of scope — a real, pre-existing, separate gap this milestone's own investigation surfaced but didn't introduce.

**M29 — Render Loop Dirty-Tracking is now fully complete.** `engine-platform`'s render loop no longer redraws unconditionally every frame: a genuinely idle window with no animation and no input settles into `ControlFlow::Wait` and stops doing real per-frame paint/GPU work entirely, verified both by direct code review and by an empirical CPU measurement. Full `cargo check`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean across every touched crate; `cargo test --workspace --release` clean (`engine-core` 146, up from 145; every other crate's suite unchanged); `maturin develop --release` + pytest (187 passed, unchanged, 1 pre-existing skip), all 29 examples (re-run twice, the second time after the `max_frames` fix, to confirm the hang was genuinely resolved and not intermittent), and the showcase demo (which exercises real click/keyboard/drag interaction end to end, not just a static scene) all confirmed clean with the real display. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

---

**Just closed:** M29 — Render Loop Dirty-Tracking (both phases): a centralized `Tree`-level dirty flag skipping real per-frame layout/GPU work on idle frames (Phase 1), and a genuine switch to `ControlFlow::Wait` when no window is animating, with explicit redraw-request wiring at every real input path plus the AccessKit action path (Phase 2) -- closing the one item M28 deliberately left open, with a real `max_frames`-hang bug found and fixed along the way and an empirical idle-CPU measurement confirming the real-world effect, not just the code review.

Real, separate finding made while scoping a proposed M30 around three specific gaps (overlay dismissal, `VirtualList` scroll wiring, a cross-window `Rc::ptr_eq` guard): direct verification found all three already real and working (overlay dismissal and the drop-zone highlight since M10, scroll dispatch already wired, both cross-window guards already present since M6/M10) -- this file's own Known Gaps section had drifted, corrected above rather than scoping a milestone for work that didn't need doing.

## Milestone 30 — MD3 + Desktop Component Catalog (§5, §7, §8)

**Status: ✅ Complete (2026-09-18) — all 10 phases (0-9) done.** User-directed: "Scope the component catalog and I want the full MD3 list and desktop only components identified in tre v1[/pyCopper]. Additionally, pair the Python type stubs for pyo3 API with each component... there are components that are mobile only. Do not include these components as this is a desktop only framework." PyPI publishing is explicitly *not* part of this milestone -- per the user's own direction it's deferred to be the very last thing done, once the Tesserae UI Framework itself is considered complete.

*(Real documentation-drift correction, 2026-09-21: this status line still read "⬜ Scoped, not started" even though every phase below was already marked ✅ and the Top Metrics table already showed this milestone Complete -- found during a direct audit prompted by the user, fixed here rather than left silently wrong, matching Phase 4's own already-recorded correction to this exact same drift class earlier in this milestone's own history.)*

**Real research this scope is built on, not re-derived from scratch:** the mobile-vs-desktop component question was already answered with real, verified work in the sibling `pyCopper` project (`/home/phil/pyDev/projects/pyCopper`) -- a GPU-accelerated declarative desktop GUI framework for Python by the same author, with the identical explicit desktop-only design goal TRE v2 already commits to (§2's own Design Principles), which independently built a real MD3 widget catalog (58 `WidgetKind`s) and recorded its own findings in this session's memory graph. Reused directly rather than re-investigated: **bottom-anchored navigation (Navigation Bar, Bottom App Bar) and Bottom Sheet are explicitly mobile patterns in M3's own catalogue** -- Navigation Rail/Navigation Drawer and Side Sheet are the real desktop counterparts, kept in scope in their place. Desktop-adaptation rules that apply to every component below, not a phase of their own: pointer-precise hit targets, not MD3's 48dp minimum touch target; click-origin ripple, not touch-origin; no compact `<600dp` breakpoint; hover, focus rings and keyboard traversal, right-click context menus, cursor shape, visible scrollbars, and mouse text selection are first-class desktop affordances MD3 itself treats as secondary.

**Already real, not rebuilt by this milestone:** `Checkbox`, `Slider`, `TextField`, `Image`, `Icon`, `Canvas`, `VirtualList`, `Splitter` (all real `NodeKind`s already), and real 5-zone docking with live drag-and-drop (M4 Phase 9) -- already substantially more capable than pyCopper's own static-only `Dock`, nothing to add there. No dedicated `Button` exists yet -- today's examples hand-compose `Rect`+`Text`+ripple for anything button-shaped, a real gap Phase 1 closes.

**Real, important caveat found while researching pyCopper's own history, not assumed to transfer unchanged:** several of pyCopper's own "engine prerequisite" blockers (no diagonal-line primitive beyond a circular arc, no overlay/portal layer) were specific to *its own* custom SDF-shader renderer's real limitations. TRE v2's renderer (`vello_hybrid`/`kurbo`, real arbitrary bezier path and stroke support already) does not obviously share those constraints -- each phase below still needs its own real "does this need new engine-core/engine-render machinery" investigation when it's actually implemented, the same discipline every prior milestone in this file has already applied, not an assumption inherited from a different renderer's own history.

**Type stub convention, applies to every phase below:** each phase that adds real `Window.add_*`/`Node` methods or new state also updates `python/tre/__init__.pyi` with matching signatures in the *same* phase -- paired per phase per the user's own explicit instruction, not a separate end-of-milestone pass. Phase 0 below establishes the stub file and `py.typed` marker (confirmed via direct check: neither exists anywhere in `python/` today) against the *current* real API surface first, so every later phase extends an established file rather than inventing its own stub-authoring approach.

### Phase 0 — Type Stub Infrastructure ✅
- Step 1: `python/tre/py.typed` (PEP 561 marker) plus `python/tre/_core.pyi` covering every real, currently-shipped `Window`/`Node`/`App`/`View`/`CanvasContext` method and class — ✅ (stubs the compiled extension only, not `__init__.py` itself -- its own plain Python source (`Signal`/`ViewModel`) already gives full type inference, and a sibling `.pyi` for the same module would shadow it; `__init__.py`'s `from tre._core import ...` re-export line picks up the new stub's types automatically. **Two real bugs in the stub caught only by cross-checking against the actual Rust signatures, not assumed correct from memory:** `add_virtual_list`'s `materialize` callback returns a plain `(r, g, b, a)` color tuple, not a `Node` as first drafted -- confirmed via direct read of `set_virtual_list_window`'s own body, every materialized row is always a plain colored `Rect`, a real, stated limitation now documented in the stub itself, not silently wrong; `CanvasContext.stroke_path`'s real parameter order is `(points, color, width)`, not `(points, width, color, closed)` -- there is no `closed` parameter at all. **Real, pre-existing export gap found and fixed along the way:** `CanvasContext` was registered in the compiled `_core` module but never re-exported through the public `tre` package (`python/tre/__init__.py`) -- fixed, now importable as `from tre import CanvasContext` like every other real class, not just via the private `tre._core` path. Verified for real, not assumed: installed `mypy` into `.venv`, ran `mypy --strict` against a script exercising real correct usage of every stubbed method (clean) plus four deliberately wrong calls guarded by `# type: ignore` (all four confirmed genuinely needed -- `--warn-unused-ignores` stayed silent, and stripping the ignores made mypy report the exact 4 expected errors with accurate messages, proof the stubs carry real type information rather than silently accepting anything). Full pytest suite (187 passed, unchanged) and all 29 examples re-run clean after the `__init__.py` export change.)

### Phase 1 — Actions ✅
- Step 1: `Button`, MD3's five real variants (Elevated/Filled/Filled Tonal/Outlined/Text) — ✅ (`Window.add_button`, `crates/engine-py/src/window_factory.rs`: a `Rect` container, corner radius `height / 2.0` -- MD3's own "Full" shape family -- with one centered `Text` label child, colors resolved per variant through a new `ThemeState::role` general resolver, `window.rs`, widened beyond the old single-field `on_surface()`. **Two real, genuine engine gaps found and fixed along the way, not assumed already solved:** (1) `engine-render`'s text-shaping pipeline hardcoded `parley::Alignment::Start` unconditionally -- a real, verified blocker for any centered label -- fixed with a new universal `TextState.align: TextAlign` capability (Start/Center/End), proven with a real pixel-readback test (`engine-render/tests/text_align.rs`) showing ink genuinely moves off the left edge under `Center`, the same "universal capability, not component-specific" precedent `PaintProperties.border_color`/`border_width` set earlier this same phase for the Outlined variant's real stroked border (`engine-render/tests/border_paint.rs`). (2) A real, confirmed click-dispatch bug caught by `tests/test_button.py`'s own functional click test, not inferred: `Tree::hit_test_at` recurses into children first with no ancestor bubbling anywhere in `dispatch`, so Button's own centered `Text` child -- sized to fill the container's inner content width -- silently ate every click meant for its parent's registered handler. Fixed at the root: a bare `NodeKind::Text` label now never independently claims a hit, always deferring to whatever's behind it (`tree.rs`); confirmed via grep first that no existing example or test anywhere relies on a standalone `add_text` node being directly clickable, so this closes a real dead end rather than risking a new one -- a future standalone clickable label (`Link`, Phase 8) gets its own dedicated `NodeKind` when that phase investigates it, the same "each interactive component is its own real `NodeKind`" precedent `Checkbox`/`Slider`/`TextField` already establish. Un-themed default uses real Material 3 baseline hex tokens (`ButtonBaseline`), the same "real historical default, not black" contract `Checkbox`'s white mark/`Slider`'s gray track already establish. Verified: full workspace `cargo test --release` (39 binaries, all green, `text_align.rs`/`border_paint.rs` included), `cargo clippy -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 197 passed/1 skipped (10 new in `test_button.py`, zero regressions), all 31 examples (including the new `examples/button.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/button.py` plus a deliberate-error probe confirming the new `.pyi` stub carries real type information.)
- Step 2: Icon Button — ✅ (`Window.add_icon_button`, real MD3 anatomy: a `Rect` container (corner radius `size / 2.0`) with one centered `Icon` child, `Button`'s own container-color machinery reused verbatim (`resolve_button_colors`) rather than a near-duplicate color table. Real MD3 naming fidelity kept even though the underlying colors are shared: Icon Button's four real variants are Filled/Filled Tonal/Outlined/**Standard**, not `Button`'s five -- there is no "Elevated Icon Button" in MD3's own vocabulary, and `"standard"` (not `"text"`) is translated to `resolve_button_colors`'s `"text"` colors at the call site, not aliased inside it, so `add_button`'s own error message never lists a name that isn't real for `Button` itself. Icon stays a fixed real MD3 token (24dp) regardless of the container's own `size`, centered on both axes via `justify_content`/`align_items` -- no `TextAlign`-equivalent needed, since an `Icon`'s own box is already exactly its glyph's bounds. **Proactively closed the identical hit-test gap `Button` (Step 1) found, before it could bite:** `NodeKind::Icon(_) => false` added to `Tree::hit_test_at` alongside the earlier `NodeKind::Text` fix, confirmed first via grep that `demo/showcase.py`'s only `add_icon` usage (its icon gallery) is purely decorative, never independently clicked -- `tests/test_icon_button.py`'s own click-dispatch test passed on the first run because of this, not found by a second failure. Verified: full workspace `cargo test --release` (39 binaries green), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 207 passed/1 skipped (10 new in `test_icon_button.py`, zero regressions), all 32 examples (including the new `examples/icon_button.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/icon_button.py`.)
- Step 3: FAB and Extended FAB — ✅ (`Window.add_fab`/`Window.add_extended_fab`. Real MD3 color-variant system found to be genuinely distinct from `Button`'s own, verified against Material Web's own real component token source (`tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-comp-fab-surface.scss`/`_md-comp-fab-primary.scss`) rather than assumed transferable -- `FAB` has its own four variants (Surface/Primary/Secondary/Tertiary), not `Button`'s five, with Surface (the real default) resolving `container` from `surface_container_high`/`icon` from `primary`, and Primary/Secondary/Tertiary each following the `<name>_container`/`on_<name>_container` pattern MD3 uses everywhere else in the spec. `FAB`'s three real sizes each pair their own independently-specified real shape token, not one proportional formula guessed from a single data point (Small 40dp/`corner-medium` 12dp, Default 56dp/`corner-large` 16dp, Large 96dp/`corner-extra-large` 28dp -- the three real ratios are close but not identical, so a formula would have been a fabricated approximation, not real fidelity). Every `FAB` variant carries a genuine rest-state elevation (level 3, `PaintProperties.elevation`'s own real level-index scale) -- `FAB` is inherently elevated in MD3, unlike `Icon Button`. `Extended FAB` reuses `FAB`'s own color/elevation system with a real icon-plus-label anatomy (icon optional, matching real MD3's own label-only variant -- the real, verified padding genuinely differs between the two cases: 16dp leading with an icon vs. 20dp without, both confirmed against Material Web's own real CSS, not invented), one real fixed size (56dp tall, `corner-large`). Real, small factoring finding along the way: `add_icon`'s own curated-icon-name-to-`BezPath` lookup, previously only duplicated once (by `add_icon_button`, Step 2), crossed this project's own established "2+ call sites" threshold for a shared helper once `add_fab`/`add_extended_fab` needed it a third and fourth time -- factored into `resolve_icon_path`, all four real call sites (`add_icon` included) now share it. `Md3Baseline` (renamed from `ButtonBaseline`, no longer pointing at one consumer) gained `primary_container`/`on_primary_container`/`tertiary_container`/`on_tertiary_container`/`surface_container_high` for `FAB`'s own real un-themed fallback. **Proactively applied the same hit-test-composite discipline a third time:** `Extended FAB`'s own `test_fab.py` click-dispatch test covers a composite `Icon`+`Text` node at once, the real combined case `Tree::hit_test_at`'s two earlier fixes (Steps 1-2) both needed to already be in place for. Verified: full workspace `cargo test --release` (39 binaries green), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 227 passed/1 skipped (20 new in `test_fab.py`, zero regressions), all 33 examples (including the new `examples/fab.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/fab.py`.)
- Step 4: Segmented Button — ✅ (`Window.add_segmented_button`. **Real, honest finding: the scoped "docking tab anatomy" precedent didn't transfer** -- checked directly (`dock.rs`'s own `set_active_tab`) before assuming it would, and docking's own "active tab" is purely logical panel-visibility switching, no real shared-border/divider visual anatomy to reuse at all; genuine independent design was needed, exactly the outcome this milestone's own scope note already allowed for. **One real, new universal engine capability, not a Segmented-Button-specific hack:** `PaintProperties.corner_radii_override: Option<[f64; 4]>` -- MD3's own real anatomy rounds a group's first/last segments only on their outer edge, square on the edge touching the next segment, which the existing single-scalar `corner_radius` can't express; kurbo's own `RoundedRect::new` already accepts independent per-corner radii natively (confirmed via direct source read of the pinned `kurbo 0.13.1`, not assumed), so this exposes an existing real primitive rather than inventing new geometry. Proven with a real pixel-readback test (`engine-render/tests/corner_radii_paint.rs`): a 30px top-left-only radius genuinely clears a pixel 2px from that corner while the other three (radius 0) stay square right up to their own corners. Real anatomy: one shared outline frame (`corner-full`, 1px real outline), a real 1px divider `Rect` between each pair of adjacent segments, per-segment selected/unselected paint (selected: `secondary_container` background, `on_secondary_container` label, a real 18dp checkmark -- deliberately smaller than every other component's 24dp icon token, confirmed from Material Web's own real token source, `tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-comp-outlined-segmented-button.scss`, not assumed consistent; unselected: transparent background, `on_surface` label, reusing `ThemeState::on_surface()` directly rather than a new baseline role). **Real, explicit design decision, not an oversight:** group-exclusivity is deliberately not built into this method -- `BUILD_TRACKER.md`'s own Phase 2 scope for the future `Radio Button` already states "group-exclusivity is application state... not engine-owned," reused here verbatim; the method paints only the real *initial* selected state from `selected` and returns every segment's own real `Node`, with `examples/segmented_button.py` demonstrating an app wiring up real live re-toggling itself using nothing but `set_on_click`/`Node.animate`. Verified: full workspace `cargo test --release` (40 binaries green, `corner_radii_paint.rs` included), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 234 passed/1 skipped (7 new in `test_segmented_button.py`, zero regressions), all 34 examples (including the new `examples/segmented_button.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/segmented_button.py`.)

### Phase 2 — Selection ✅
- Step 1: Radio Button — ✅ (a real, new first-class `NodeKind::RadioButton` (`RadioButtonState { selected, select_progress: Animated<f64>, unselected_tint, selected_tint }`), `CheckboxState`'s own real shape mirrored exactly: `selected` is plain app-owned state, `select_progress` the engine-driven visual consequence, animated toward `1.0`/`0.0` -- `Tree::tick_all` needed its own explicit new ticking arm (an `if let`, not an exhaustive `match`, so this doesn't fail loudly on its own; found by deliberately checking `Checkbox`'s/`Slider`'s own real precedent rather than assuming the compiler would catch a missing tick arm). One real, necessary anatomy difference from `Checkbox`: MD3's real radio button is a stroked *ring* (not a filled box) whose own color genuinely transitions between `unselected_tint`/`selected_tint` as it toggles, not just a static box with an appearing/disappearing mark -- painted via `Interpolate for peniko::Color` (already real since §5's own animation core) using `select_progress` as the blend factor, so the ring's color rides the identical timeline the inner dot's scale-in already does. Real MD3 anatomy verified: a real accessibility mirror added too (`Tree`'s `access_node.set_toggled`, matching `Checkbox`'s own precedent exactly, checked first that `Checkbox` itself sets no explicit AccessKit `Role` either, so this stays honest parity, not new asymmetry). **Real, explicit, stated scope limit, not an oversight:** unlike `Checkbox`/`Slider`/`TextField`, a radio button created *before* `Window.set_theme` is not retroactively re-tinted by a later `set_theme` call -- `Tree::set_all_component_tints` deliberately reuses one shared `on_surface` tint across every component (its own already-documented scope choice), but a radio button genuinely needs two different real roles (`outline`/`primary`), which that single-color mechanism can't express without contradicting its own stated simplification; every radio button still starts correctly themed at construction time. `Node.set_selected`/`get_selected` and the `"select_progress"` arm of `animate`/`get` all mirror `Checkbox`'s own exact real API shape. Group-exclusivity deliberately left to the app (the real point this step's own scope text already named), demonstrated end to end in `examples/radio_button.py`. Verified: full workspace `cargo test --release` (41 binaries green, `engine-render/tests/radio_button_paint.rs` included -- proves the ring's real color interpolation and the dot's real scale-in, not just that the match arm compiles), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 243 passed/1 skipped (9 new in `test_radio_button.py`, zero regressions), all 35 examples (including the new `examples/radio_button.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/radio_button.py`.)
- Step 2: Switch — ✅ (a real, new first-class `NodeKind::Switch` (`SwitchState { on, toggle_progress: Animated<f64>, track_off_tint, track_on_tint, track_outline_tint, handle_off_tint, handle_on_tint }`), `RadioButtonState`'s own real shape mirrored a third time. Real MD3 anatomy verified against Material Web's own token source (`tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-comp-switch.scss`) before any code was written: track 52dp × 32dp, `corner-full`; unselected track = `surface_container_highest` fill + a real, separate `outline`-role stroke (a genuinely different color from the fill, not the same role twice); selected track = `primary` fill, no stroke; handle grows from a real 16dp (unselected, `outline` tint) to 24dp (selected, `on_primary` tint) as it toggles -- not just a color change the way `RadioButton`'s dot is, a real size change too. The handle's own slide-and-grow formula (`cx = h*0.5 + t*(w-h)`, `handle_radius = h*(0.25+0.125*t)`) is derived directly from those real verified ratios, not fitted after the fact: unselected padding-from-edge (`(32-16)/2=8`) plus unselected handle radius (`8`) and selected padding (`(32-24)/2=4`) plus selected handle radius (`12`) both sum to the identical `16 = h*0.5`, which is what makes one clean symmetric travel formula exact for both ends, confirmed algebraically before committing to it rather than assumed to simplify. The track's own real separate outline stroke fades out via opacity (`1.0 - toggle_progress`) as the switch turns on, reusing the exact real "conditionally-drawn stroke, not the universal `PaintProperties` border" approach -- a switch's outline is genuinely a `Switch`-owned concept (a fourth tint no other component needed), not the shared `border_color`/`border_width` fields `Button`'s Outlined variant uses. `add_switch`'s own real, deliberate anatomy difference from `add_radio_button`: two real dimension parameters (`width`/`height`), not one shared `size`, since a real MD3 switch track is genuinely non-square (52×32), unlike a radio button's real circle. Same real accessibility mirror (`set_toggled`) and the same real, honest live-re-theming scope limit `RadioButton` already states (five distinct real roles here, further from `set_all_component_tints`'s single-shared-tint design than even `RadioButton`'s two were -- every switch still starts correctly themed at construction). `Node.set_on`/`get_on` and the `"toggle_progress"` arm of `animate`/`get` mirror `Checkbox`/`RadioButton`'s own exact real API shape a third time. Verified: full workspace `cargo test --release` (42 binaries green, `engine-render/tests/switch_paint.rs` included -- proves the track's real color transition, the outline's real fade, and the handle's real slide *and* grow, not just that the match arm compiles), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 251 passed/1 skipped (8 new in `test_switch.py`, zero regressions), all 36 examples (including the new `examples/switch.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/switch.py`.)

Real, deliberate architectural choice, not an oversight: built as a plain composition (`Rect` + optional leading `Icon` + `Text` + optional trailing `Icon`), not a new first-class `NodeKind` -- confirmed the real dividing line this catalog already draws before choosing it: `Segmented Button`'s own real selection (Filter Chip's genuine toggle-state twin) is already app-owned static composition, while a standalone single control (`Checkbox`/`RadioButton`/`Switch`) gets an engine-owned animated `NodeKind`; Filter Chip's "selected" is fundamentally the same group/app-state shape as a segment's own, so the same real line applies, not a new one invented for this step. Filter Chip's real selected state (the only one with a filled container at all) reuses `Button`'s own Filled Tonal color pattern (`secondary_container`/`on_secondary_container`) exactly. Real MD3 behavior reproduced faithfully: a selected Filter Chip's real checkmark *replaces* any custom leading `icon` rather than showing both. `removable` (a real trailing "close" icon) is independent of `variant` -- any chip can reasonably be made removable, not gated to `"input"` only. Real, deliberate reuse, not a second mechanism: `Tree::open_overlay`/`close_overlay` (§11.3, M3 step 13) already exist precisely for this -- `overlay.rs`'s own module doc comment states "menu bars, dropdown menus, context menus... are all the same missing primitive" -- so `open_menu` calls the identical primitive `dispatch::open_context_menu`'s own right-click path already uses, just exposed as a direct Python-callable method instead of gated behind synthetic secondary-button dispatch, since a real dropdown opens on a plain click (or any app-chosen trigger). The existing context-menu mechanism itself is completely untouched, confirmed by `tests/test_context_menu.py` passing unmodified. Real MD3 anatomy, verified against Material Web's own token source before writing code: **a real, confirmed finding** -- Material Web has no dedicated menu-item token file at all (a direct fetch 404s); a real MD3 menu genuinely reuses the plain List Item's own tokens for its rows (`_md-comp-list.scss`: 56dp height, 24dp icon, 16dp leading space, `on_surface` label, `on_surface_variant` icon), not a separate menu-specific row shape -- confirmed, not assumed. The panel itself has its own real tokens (`_md-comp-menu.scss`): `surface_container` fill, `corner-extra-small` (4dp), a real rest-state elevation (level 2). `build_menu` **moves** each item (`Tree::detach` then `add_child`, the identical real re-parenting mechanism `Tree::close_overlay`'s own doc comment already establishes for overlay content) into the returned panel, itself returned genuinely unattached anywhere -- `open_menu`'s own `open_overlay` call is what actually attaches it, the same "detach, not destroy, ready for later `add_child`" contract context-menu content already has. **Real, confirmed-by-a-failing-test finding, not predicted in advance:** a menu item is genuinely not clickable until its menu is actually open (`window.click()` on an item still attached only to an unattached panel misses, since that subtree was never part of a `compute_layout` pass rooted at the real window root) -- caught by `tests/test_menu.py`'s own first draft of its click-dispatch test, which assumed click-before-open would work; fixed by correcting the test's own premise, not the implementation, since "not clickable while not on-screen" is the real, correct behavior. Same real reopen guard `open_context_menu` already has (`overlay_meta`-checked, a safe no-op if already open). Verified: full workspace `cargo test --release` (42 binaries green, unchanged -- pure composition plus existing overlay primitives, no new `engine-render` capability needed), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 273 passed/1 skipped (10 new in `test_menu.py`, zero regressions, `test_context_menu.py` itself unmodified and still green), all 38 examples (including the new `examples/menu.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/menu.py`.)

### Phase 4 — Overlay-Dependent: Dialog, Snackbar, Side Sheet ✅
- Step 1: Dialog, a real modal — ✅ (`Window.add_dialog`/`open_dialog`/`close_dialog`. **Real, confirmed engine-core gap, investigated directly before writing any code, not assumed solved:** `Tree::dismiss_overlays_outside` only ever blocked a background click when it *also* dismissed the overlay — there was no way to express "block interaction with everything behind this overlay, without dismissing it on an outside click," which a real modal dialog needs (a real dialog only closes via its own explicit action or Escape, never an accidental outside click). Fixed with a new, purely additive `OverlayMeta.modal: bool` field (`false` a true no-op, verified by a new regression test proving `modal: false` preserves the exact old dismiss-lets-clicks-through behavior) plus a new read-only `Tree::press_blocked_by_modal_overlay` helper and one new early-return branch in `dispatch`'s `PointerPressed` arm, placed right after the existing `dismiss_overlays_outside` check — two new `engine-core` unit tests both passed on the first attempt. All 9 pre-existing `OverlayMeta { ... }` construction sites across the codebase updated with `modal: false` to preserve their exact existing behavior. Real MD3 tokens verified against Material Web's own token source (`_md-comp-dialog.scss`) before writing any code: `surface_container_high` panel, real `corner-extra-large` shape (28dp), a real rest-state elevation (level 3), Headline Small (24sp/400 weight, `on_surface`) for the headline and Body Medium (14sp/400 weight, `on_surface_variant`) for the supporting text — a real, easy-to-miss distinction the first draft got wrong (see below); `Md3Baseline` gained `SCRIM`. **Real positioning technique, not a new primitive:** a real dialog is always centered, unlike every other overlay in this catalog (anchor-relative-below) — solved by reusing `open_overlay`'s existing anchor-relative math with a synthetic zero-size anchor pinned at the window origin (resolving to a `(0,0)` inset) combined with ordinary flex `justify_content`/`align_items: Center` on a full-window scrim, avoiding the need for a new/third positioning primitive; the synthetic anchor is tracked via `OverlayMeta.anchor` and explicitly `Tree::remove`d in `close_dialog`, closing a real per-open/close node-leak risk. **Two real bugs caught in my own first draft before it was ever compiled, not shipped and found later:** the draft had no theme resolution at all for `scrim`/`panel`/headline colors (hardcoded baseline constants, inconsistent with every other component this milestone), used `on_surface_variant` for the *headline* when real MD3 says headline uses plain `on_surface` (only the supporting text uses the variant role), and took optional `x`/`y` kwargs that would have silently defeated the deliberate flex-centering design if a caller ever passed them — all three fixed before compiling. Proven end to end through the real Python FFI, not just that the API doesn't raise: `tests/test_dialog.py`'s own click-dispatch test proves a real click on a background node is genuinely swallowed while a modal dialog is open and reaches its handler again once closed — the real, concrete proof the new `OverlayMeta.modal` fix actually works through the full stack. **Real, honest correction to this file's own Top Metrics table, found while updating it for this step:** M30's row had stayed at "0%, not started" through all of Phases 0-3 actually completing — a real drift from this project's own stated "update Top Metrics every time" convention, corrected now rather than left silently wrong. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, 2 new `tree.rs` unit tests included)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 308 passed/1 skipped (7 new in `test_dialog.py`, zero regressions), all 44 examples (including the new `examples/dialog.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/dialog.py`.)
- Step 2: Snackbar — ✅ (`Window.add_snackbar`/`open_snackbar`/`close_snackbar`. ### Phase 6 — Lists & Disclosure ✅
- Step 1: List / ListItem — ✅ (`Window.add_list_item`/`add_list`, a plain, non-virtualized list for small real collections -- `VirtualList` (already real since M4/M8) stays the real choice for large ones, not two unrelated mechanisms, this step's own real scope note now confirmed rather than just asserted. Real per-item anatomy reuses List Item's own token file, already investigated once (Phase 2 Step 4, `Menu`'s own real finding that Material Web has no dedicated menu-item token file and reuses List Item's directly) — `MENU_ITEM_*` constants reused verbatim, plus a real, confirmed new finding this step made: the *two-line* variant (72dp, vs. the already-known one-line 56dp) for a headline + real supporting-text second line, Body Medium (`DIALOG_BODY_FONT_SIZE`/`_WEIGHT` reused directly), `on_surface_variant` — the same real role trailing supporting text (metadata) also uses. No divider token exists in the list's own file (confirmed by the same fetch) — an app composes one itself via the already-real `add_divider`, the same "engine gives primitives, app composes" contract every other bare-container component in this catalog already has. **Real, deliberate design applying `Navigation Rail`/`Tabs`'s own hit-test lesson proactively this time, not found the hard way a third time:** the two-line variant's headline+supporting-text block is a real, necessary `NodeKind::Container` wrapper (two stacked `Text` children need *some* grouping node) — `Tree::set_hit_testable(text_block, false)` applied immediately on insertion, verified again by this step's own click-dispatch test rather than assumed safe from precedent alone; both the one-line and two-line independent-click tests passed on the first run. **Real, deliberate reuse, not a new re-parenting mechanism:** `add_list` moves each item (`Tree::detach` then `add_child`) into the returned frame, the identical real mechanism `build_menu` already established. **Real bug self-caught before compiling, not shipped broken:** a first draft of `add_list` summed each item's own `Tree::layout(...).size.height` to compute the frame's own height — a real mistake, since an item's `Layout` is only meaningful after a real `compute_layout` pass has run at least once, which this method has no guarantee of; fixed to use `auto()` for the frame's own height instead, the identical real "definite cross-axis, auto main-axis sizes from children" pattern `AppShell`'s own `content` region (`flex_grow: 1.0`, no explicit height) already uses. - Step 2: Accordion — ✅ (`Window.add_accordion_header`. No official M3 component page (confirmed by pyCopper's own real research against the full M3 reference library, not re-checked here since the finding transfers directly; grounded instead in the Lists guideline's own "expand and collapse in a folder-like manner" text). **Real, deliberate scope: only the header is this step's own real new anatomy** — the collapsible content region has no distinctive MD3 styling of its own, so no dedicated `add_accordion_content` method was invented; the app composes it from any existing container (`add_rect`/`add_card`), the identical "engine gives primitives, app composes" contract `Card` itself already has. Header reuses List Item's own real anatomy exactly (`MENU_ITEM_*`, the same real "no dedicated token file, reuse List Item's" pattern `Menu`/`List` already established) — 56dp height, `on_surface` headline (Label Large), a trailing 24dp chevron (`on_surface_variant`). **Real, confirmed engine limitation found and honestly worked around, not silently assumed solved:** `Node.animate("transform", ...)` only ever composes translate+scale (`extract_translate_scale`'s own real, stated shape since M6 Phase 2) — there is no rotation capability exposed to Python at all, so a literal spinning chevron isn't buildable through the existing API. The real, working substitute: `Affine::scale(-1.0)` (a uniform negative scale, flipping both axes) is mathematically identical to a 180° rotation for any point-symmetric glyph, and the curated chevron is exactly that — `Node.animate("transform", (0.0, 0.0, -1.0))` on the real, independently-returned `chevron` genuinely flips it into a convincing "expanded" orientation, reusing scale (already real) rather than needing a new rotation primitive; `Node.get` has no way to read a transform's own resulting value back (not among its real scalar-only properties, the same "no pixel/value readback from Python" limitation this codebase already has elsewhere), so this is proven by the call succeeding end to end, not by reading the matrix back. **Real, confirmed asset addition, not shipped without one:** MD3's real `expand_more` chevron icon didn't exist in this project's curated set (only 8 icons existed: home/search/menu/close/check/arrow_back/add/settings) — fetched its real, verbatim SVG path data directly from the identical Google CDN `engine-md3`'s own icon module already cites, the ninth curated icon, growing the set exactly the way its own doc comment says it will ("additive... when a real need asks for more"); its own real parse-as-BezPath test covers it automatically, no new test needed. **Real API gap found while writing the example, not shipped silently wrong:** `Node.remove()` is full, irreversible destruction (`Tree::remove`), not a detach — this codebase exposes no Python-level "hide without destroying" for an arbitrary node the way `Tree::close_overlay` already has internally for real overlays; `examples/accordion.py` toggles the content region's own real `opacity` instead (already-general, avoids the destroy pitfall entirely). Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (`engine-core`/`engine-md3` unaffected in count, `engine-md3`'s own icon-parsing test automatically covers the new glyph)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 390 passed/1 skipped (5 new in `test_accordion.py`, zero regressions), all 53 examples (including the new `examples/accordion.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/accordion.py`.)
- Step 3: Tree View — ✅ (`Window.add_tree_node`, the identical real grounding `Accordion` already established (no official M3 page, the Lists guideline's own "expand and collapse" text), applied recursively. Real, deliberate design: a tree row is `Accordion`'s own header anatomy again (`MENU_ITEM_*`/`ACCORDION_CHEVRON_ICON` reused verbatim, not re-declared), with one real, new addition a flat accordion header never needed — a per-node `depth: usize` real left-indent (24dp per level, not a discrete MD3 token, the identical honest caveat `Dialog`'s own padding constants carry), the one real, load-bearing difference "recursively" actually means here. Real, honest design choice: `leaf: bool` omits the chevron entirely for a childless node — a leaf has nothing to expand, matching real desktop file-browser convention, not left for the app to fake with an invisible one. Returns `(header, chevron)`, `chevron` `None` for a leaf; toggling reuses the identical real contract `Accordion`'s own header already has (`Node.animate("transform", (0.0, 0.0, -1.0))`, the same real `Affine::scale(-1.0)`-as-180°-flip substitute, this engine still having no rotation primitive). **Real, deliberate regression check, not assumed safe from `Accordion`'s own precedent alone:** a dedicated test proves a deeply-nested (`depth=3`) row's own extra left padding doesn't reintroduce a hit-test problem — passed on the first run, confirming the added indentation composes cleanly with the already-working header anatomy. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, unchanged — a pure composition reusing `Accordion`'s own real anatomy needed no new `engine-render` capability), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 399 passed/1 skipped (9 new in `test_tree_view.py`, zero regressions), all 54 examples (including the new `examples/tree_view.py`, a small file-browser-shaped tree) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/tree_view.py`.)

When true, adds a real trailing `chevron_right` icon child, MD3's own real "this item opens a nested menu" convention — the twelfth curated icon, fetched verbatim from the same real CDN this catalog's icon module already cites. The real submenu itself needed no new engine primitive at all: it's just another real `build_menu`/`open_menu` pair, opened with the parent item's own returned `Node` as the anchor, the identical real `Tree::open_overlay` primitive every other overlay in this catalog already reuses. **A real, confirmed bug found live, not predicted in advance:** `examples/main_menu_submenus.py`'s own first real run genuinely failed — `AssertionError: a real click on a submenu item must reach its own registered handler`. Root-caused to `Tree::dismiss_overlays_outside`: `open_overlay` always positions content anchor-relative-*below*, so a submenu anchored to an item inside an already-open parent menu genuinely sits outside the parent menu's own bounds; the filter only ever checked a *candidate* overlay's own bounds against the press point, so a press genuinely inside the submenu was wrongly read as "outside" the *parent* menu, dismissing it and consuming the press via `dispatch`'s own early-return before the submenu item's own handler ever ran. Fixed with a new `inside_any_overlay` check added to the filter — a press inside *any* currently-open overlay can no longer dismiss a *different* one, since the user is still interacting with the overlay system as a whole; for every existing single-overlay caller (Menu, Tooltip, Search View, Popover) this is a true no-op, confirmed by the full pre-existing `engine-core` suite passing unmodified. Proven directly with a new, dedicated `engine-core` unit test (`a_press_inside_a_nested_submenu_does_not_dismiss_its_own_parent_menu`, engine-core 151 up from 150) building a genuine two-overlay nested scene rather than reusing the single-overlay `overlay_scene` helper, plus a new Python-level test (`test_the_parent_menu_survives_a_real_click_on_its_own_submenu_item`) proving the parent menu's own survival behaviorally through a real `window.click()`-driven reproduction, not `open_menu` called directly. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, `engine-core` 151 up from 150)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 446 passed/1 skipped (6 in `test_main_menu_submenus.py`, up from the original 5 draft, zero regressions), all 62 examples (including the new `examples/main_menu_submenus.py`, which failed for real before the fix and now exits cleanly) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/main_menu_submenus.py`.)

### Phase 9 — Content Specialty ✅
- Step 1: Video — ✅ (`Window.add_video`/`Node.push_frame`. No official MD3 page (confirmed via the same real directory-listing technique this milestone already uses). **Real, deliberate reuse of `NodeKind::Image` directly, not a new `NodeKind`, grounded in real, directly-applicable precedent from the sibling `pyCopper` project's own `Video` widget** (same author, same explicit desktop-only design goal, confirmed via direct source read): `Video` is a *frame sink*, not a decoder — nothing in this codebase depends on a codec library (adding one, realistically PyAV/FFmpeg, would mean taking on its install size and licensing considerations for every TRE application, not just the ones that show video), so the application decodes however it likes and calls `push_frame(rgba, width, height)` at whatever cadence it decides. `add_video(width, height, fit, x, y)` mirrors `add_image`'s own identical real contract exactly — `width`/`height` are the node's own real, fixed box (unlike pyCopper's own intrinsic-sizing `Image`, which lays out `0x0` until a frame arrives); a pushed frame's own resolution is resolved against that fixed box by `content_fit` at paint time, so a real, live resolution renegotiation between frames needs zero layout involvement. Initialized with a single fully-transparent placeholder pixel so the node paints as genuinely empty until the app's first real `push_frame` call. `Node.push_frame` reuses `set_text`'s own established chokepoint pattern (`tree.get_mut` + `NodeKind` match, `EngineError::UnknownProperty` for any non-`Image` node) — validates `rgba.len() == width * height * 4` with a real, clear `PyValueError` (`add_icon`'s own "fail loudly on pure validation" convention), not routed through `EngineError` since no I/O is involved. **Real, confirmed architecture gap found and fixed alongside this step, not silently missed:** `engine-render`'s own `ImageTextureCache::sync` (M22 Phase 1) uploaded a real `Image` node's GPU texture exactly once, keyed only on node presence — correct when an `Image` node's pixel data never changed after creation (true before this phase), but would have silently kept painting a video's very first frame forever otherwise. Fixed by keying re-upload on real content identity instead of node presence: `peniko::Blob<u8>` carries a real, unique, monotonically-assigned `id()` (confirmed via direct source read of the vendored `linebender_resource_handle` crate, not assumed) — a cheap O(1) comparison, never a byte-level memcmp of the pixel buffer itself, which would be a real, meaningful per-frame cost at video resolutions. **Real, deliberate scope simplification, stated not silently dropped:** a changed frame always recreates the whole GPU texture rather than reusing the existing one via `write_texture` alone when the resolution is unchanged — correct for both same-size and resized frames uniformly, at the real cost of a full allocation per pushed frame; `Video`'s own scope (a frame sink for whatever cadence the app feeds it, not a real-time decode pipeline this codebase has no codec dependency for) doesn't need the incremental fast path yet. Proven directly at the `engine-render` level with a new, dedicated unit test (`sync_reuploads_a_texture_when_its_own_node_content_genuinely_changes`, `engine-core`/`engine-render` test count unaffected at the core level, `engine-render` 4 up from 3) building a real two-frame scene and asserting the cache's own tracked content id actually changes on re-sync. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (44 binaries green)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 457 passed/1 skipped (11 new in `test_video.py`, zero regressions), all 63 examples (including the new `examples/video.py`, a real synthetic decode loop pushing 5 distinct frames including a mid-stream resolution change) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/video.py`.)
- Step 2: Node Graph — ✅ (`Window.add_node_graph`/`Window.add_graph_node`, closing the real gap M5 Phase 4's own `PLAN.md` named and `examples/positioned_graph.py` (M6 Phase 3) only ever demonstrated as raw composition -- interchangeable circles, no real node anatomy, no reparent/position automation an app didn't have to hand-roll itself.
