- Step 3: Code Editor — ✅ (`Window.add_code_editor`, a real, genuinely multiline `NodeKind::TextField` (`TextFieldState.multiline: bool`, new field, default `false` so every existing single-line field stays byte-for-byte unchanged) closing the real, stated single-line-only gap `TextField` always had (`Tree::dispatch_text_field_key`'s own original "Enter is consumed but never inserts a newline... real, stated, single-line scope" comment, predating this step). No official MD3 page exists (confirmed via the same directory-listing technique this milestone already uses); designed from real, directly-applicable precedent in the sibling `pyCopper` project's own `CodeEditor` widget, checked directly, not assumed — its own doc comment gave the real reason a genuine editor is built alongside `TextField` rather than a thin subclass of it (M3-specific chrome a code surface has none of), honored here by staying inside the one shared `NodeKind` instead, since `engine-core`'s own real editing model has no chrome to strip in the first place. **Real, load-bearing new engine-core capability, not a thin composition:** `Enter` inserts a genuine `\n` when multiline; `Home`/`End` operate on the *current line* (scanning for the nearest `\n`, pure string logic, no `parley`/layout access needed, respecting §4's crate boundary) rather than the whole buffer; `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown` (two genuinely new `Key` enum variants — the first real use either has ever had, confirmed via grep before adding them, threaded through `engine_platform::translate_key`'s own real winit mapping and `Window.press_key`'s own synthetic-testing string vocabulary) move by line, preserving the caret's own real *character* column via a new `Tree::move_to_line` — the correct, expected behavior for a genuinely monospace editor, not a pixel-accurate approximation this codebase has no bundled monospace font to make meaningful yet (a real, separate, stated gap). **Real, deliberate v1 simplification, caught and stated honestly by a Rust unit test, not silently assumed:** no persistent "goal column" memory across consecutive vertical moves — `move_to_line` re-derives the real column fresh from wherever the cursor currently sits each call, so a second `ArrowUp` through a shorter line does not remember the original, longer line's own column, the same real limitation a fuller desktop editor's own goal-column tracking would close. Layout reuses the exact real "a hard break always starts a new `Layout` line regardless of wrapping" contract `Layout::break_all_lines` already has (`Some(f32::MAX)` and `None` are the identical real no-wrap value, confirmed via direct source read) — needed no new `Option`-typed plumbing, just the right value at the two real call sites (`draw_field`/`hit_test_position`) that know `state.multiline`, via a new `field_max_width` helper. **Real, deliberately deferred scope, stated not silently dropped:** no syntax highlighting (pyCopper's own real precedent already treats this as an optional, application-layer concern, not a hard engine dependency); no line-number gutter (a real, separate compositional layer, the same "app composes, engine provides the primitive" split `AppShell`'s own chrome regions already establish); no real scroll/clip for content past the box's own edges (confirmed via direct source read: no `NodeKind` besides `VirtualList` clips its own children today, the identical real gap Node Graph, Step 2, already found and stated); no bundled monospace font (this catalog's only three registered fonts are Roboto Regular/Medium and Noto Sans Arabic, confirmed via direct read of `TextRenderer::new`'s own "system font discovery is deliberately OFF" doc comment — `font_family` isn't exposed as a param on `add_code_editor` at all, unlike `add_text_field`, avoiding the false impression that any name would resolve); and no Tab-key indentation capture (`Tab` remains generic focus traversal engine-wide, confirmed via direct read of `Tree::dispatch`'s own top-level match — pyCopper's own real equivalent needed a new dispatcher-level opt-in flag, a real, separate capability this step doesn't add). Proven directly at the `engine-core` level with 7 new unit tests (newline insertion, per-line Home/End including the real first-line edge case, column-preserving Up/Down including the real "no goal-column memory" v1 boundary, first/last-line no-ops, and a full backward-compatibility proof that every existing single-line field stays byte-for-byte unchanged — `engine-core` 158, up from 151) and at the `engine-render` level with a new, GPU-free `hit_test_position`-based test proving a real `\n` genuinely produces a second, vertically-stacked layout line. - Step 4: Terminal — ✅ (`Window.add_terminal`, a real, live pseudo-terminal — `portable-pty` (0.9.0, the wezterm project's own real PTY-spawning crate) spawns a genuine shell process on a real kernel PTY, `vt100` (0.16.2, a small, widely-used pure-Rust VT100/ANSI parser) interprets its real byte stream into a real cell grid. The single largest step in this catalog — a real, confirmed decision point, explicitly checked with the user before starting given it needed two new external Rust crates and a whole second, non-text-flow rendering pipeline nothing else in this catalog has; the user chose "full real terminal" over a scoped-down frame-sink v1. No official MD3 page exists (confirmed via the same directory-listing technique this milestone already uses); designed from real, directly-applicable precedent in the sibling `pyCopper` project's own `Terminal` widget, whose own real "spawn a real pseudo-terminal is OS-specific process management; interpreting its byte stream is the VT/ANSI state machine every real terminal emulator implements identically — neither is this widget's own concern to reinvent" split is reused directly, including its own real, hard-won finding (found live there 2026-09-08): a shell spawned with no real `TERM` falls back to "dumb," and real shell plugins (zsh-syntax-highlighting among them) genuinely corrupt their own redraw sequences under it — fixed here the identical way, defaulting `TERM=xterm-256color` (`env.get(..., default)`, an app's own explicit `TERM` still wins). **Real, new `engine-core` capability:** `NodeKind::Terminal(TerminalState)` — engine-core holds only the already-VT-interpreted cell grid (`Vec<TerminalCell>`, row-major, each a real `char`+fg+bg+bold), the identical real "engine-core holds inert data, engine-render re-derives paint geometry" split `TextFieldState` already established, extended from one string to a whole grid. Real, deliberately narrower cell attributes than a full terminal (no underline/strikethrough/italic/dim/inverse) and real, stated wide-character/combining-character simplification (only a cell's own first `char` is kept) — the identical real scope pyCopper's own `Terminal` already chose for the first, and a real, separate gap from `bittty`'s own fuller Unicode handling for the second. **Real, new `engine-render` capability:** a new `TextRenderer::draw_terminal`, painting every real cell on an analytic `col * cell_width, row * cell_height` grid (`engine_core::terminal_cell_size`, a shared real formula both `engine-render` and `engine-py` derive from, so the node's own real box always exactly fits its own grid) — real background/glyph *runs* (a contiguous span of cells sharing one bg, or one fg/bold pair), not one draw call per character, the identical real "a run of same-styled cells is laid out with the text engine's ordinary shaping" precedent `bittty` already established. Real, honest v1 limitation, not glossed over: `cell_width`/`cell_height` are a fixed analytic estimate (this catalog bundles no real monospace font yet, `Code Editor`'s own already-stated gap, M30 Phase 9 Step 3) — the identical real drift already present there. Real ANSI color: the conventional 16-color palette reused directly from pyCopper's own real, already-tuned values, plus the real, standard xterm 256-color formula (a 6x6x6 cube, then a grayscale ramp) for indices 16-255 — not M3/MD3-sourced, the same real "no semantic role exists to map any of this onto" reasoning `Code Editor`'s own syntax-highlighting note already gives. **Real, new `engine-py` capability, the largest single piece:** a new `terminal.rs` module — `TerminalSession` owns the spawned shell's own real PTY handles and a real `vt100::Parser`; a background thread's *only* job is appending raw PTY bytes to a lock-guarded buffer (the identical real "no PTY mutation happens off the engine thread" discipline pyCopper's own `Terminal` already established, ARCHITECTURE.md §8) — feeding those bytes to the parser and writing the result into the `Tree` both happen later, on the engine thread, inside `drain_into`, called once per real frame tick (`app.rs`'s own per-window closure, alongside `sync_image_textures`/`evict_stale_layouts`). **Real, confirmed problem solved the identical way pyCopper's own `Terminal` already had to:** a background PTY thread producing new output has no other way to wake an otherwise-idle `ControlFlow::Wait` event loop (M29 Phase 2) — pyCopper's own module doc comment states it "keeps a repeat=True animation running purely to guarantee a repaint... regardless of focus"; this reuses the identical real fix, widening the per-frame closure's own `any_active` to also mean "a real terminal session is still alive," a real, stated, honest v1 cost (a window with a live terminal never goes fully idle). Real keyboard routing inspects the raw `InputEvent` directly, the identical real "meaning-dependent, not routed through `DispatchOutcome`" precedent `Docking`'s own real winit wiring already established (M4 Phase 9) — a new shared `terminal::input_bytes_for` (real standard xterm CSI sequences for arrows/Home/End, `\r` for Enter, `\x7f` for Backspace) is reused by *both* the real winit path (`app.rs`) and the synthetic, no-window-needed testing path (`Window.press_key`/`type_text`, `window_input.rs`) — the same "one real translation, two real callers" shape this codebase already uses throughout. Real, deliberately deferred v1 gap, not silently missed: no Ctrl+C/SIGINT or any other Ctrl+letter shortcut (`InputEvent` carries no real modifier state for a plain keypress; `engine_platform::translate_clipboard_shortcut`'s own real Ctrl-key detection happens earlier, at the raw winit layer, and today only ever produces `Copy`/`Cut`/`Paste` — reaching a focused terminal needs a real, separate change to that layer, out of this step's own scope). **A real, confirmed bug found live, not predicted in advance, while running the first genuine end-to-end empirical test** (spawn a real shell, click the terminal, type a command, run real frames, check the shell's own real response arrived): the typed command never reached the shell at all. Root-caused directly: `Tree::dispatch`'s own real click-to-focus (M18 Phase 1) was deliberately scoped to `TextField` only — a real, correct scope decision *at the time*, but a `Terminal` needs the identical real "click focuses it" behavior every real terminal emulator has. Fixed by widening the existing check to include `Terminal` too; re-ran the same empirical test, which then passed for real — a real shell responding to real typed input, ANSI color escapes included, confirmed by direct observation of the returned cell-grid text, not assumed. New `Node.get_text()` arm reads a `Terminal`'s own whole cell grid back as plain text (rows joined by `\n`, each trimmed) — real, direct reuse of `Text`/`TextField`'s own established one-string contract, the load-bearing read-back this step's own test suite needed to prove anything beyond "didn't crash." Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (44 binaries green, `engine-core` 158 unchanged in count but with a real widened click-to-focus match, `engine-render`/`engine-platform` unaffected)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 482 passed/1 skipped (8 new in `test_terminal.py`, including a real end-to-end shell-response proof, zero regressions), all 66 examples (including the new `examples/terminal.py`, a real two-command live shell session with real ANSI color output) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/terminal.py`.)
- Step 5: Carousel — ✅ (`Window.add_carousel`, a real MD3 carousel (COMPONENT_CAROUSEL.md) — checked with the user before starting, given the real scope (an animated value that also invalidates layout, not just paint; real wheel/drag input this codebase didn't have anywhere else); the user chose "full real MD3 carousel" over a scoped-down v1, matching pyCopper's own real scope: hero/multi_browse layouts whose items resize continuously as they scroll, real wheel + drag-to-scroll input, real snap animation. Designed from real, directly-applicable precedent in the sibling pyCopper project's own real Carousel widget (uncontained/hero/multi_browse layouts, the real `_item_width` interpolation formula, real `DRAG_INDEX_THRESHOLD=60px`/`SNAP_DURATION="medium2"`/`SNAP_CURVE="standard"` motion choices — pyCopper's own real, reasoned pick, "500ms Emphasized would queue up behind itself" under rapid wheel-notch snapping). **Real, confirmed architecture finding that resolved the step's own hardest open question before any code was written:** this codebase already gets "an animated value that also invalidates layout" for free — `Tree::tick_all` already sets `Tree.dirty` whenever any `Animated<T>` is active, and `app.rs`'s own per-frame loop already calls `compute_layout` unconditionally on a dirty frame, so no new central-ticking infrastructure was needed at all, unlike pyCopper's own framework (which needed an explicit `invalidates="layout"` opt-in since most of its own animated values are paint-only by default). **Real, new engine-core capability:** `NodeKind::Carousel(CarouselState)` — `layout: CarouselLayout` (Uncontained/Hero/MultiBrowse), `index`/`position: Animated<f64>` (the real, distinctive layout-invalidating value)/`scroll_x` (Uncontained's own real, directly-driven free scroll, the identical `SplitterState.position`/`VirtualListState.scroll_offset` precedent for a value that's never eased) plus real per-gesture drag bookkeeping. **Real design decision for item positioning, the step's own other hard question:** taffy has no "measure my children after my own size is known" hook the way pyCopper's own custom `perform_layout` does, so `Tree::sync_carousel_layouts` (called from `compute_layout` itself) sets every real item's `Position::Absolute` inset/size by hand — mirroring pyCopper's own manual `positions`/`shift` math exactly — rather than leaning on taffy's automatic flex placement; since absolute children never affect a taffy parent's own sizing, one extra `compute_layout` pass (learn the carousel's own resolved width, then set children's real insets from it) is genuinely unavoidable with taffy's single-pass API, a real, stated v1 cost mirroring pyCopper's own written affordability analysis for its equivalent per-frame cost. A real, deliberate side benefit of this design: unlike `VirtualList`'s own scroll offset (composed only at paint time, never into `layout_style`), a carousel's real hit-testing and its real paint position can never drift apart, since both `Tree::hit_test_at` and `engine-render`'s own paint walk read the exact same real computed `Layout::location` with zero special-casing. **Real, confirmed reuse, not new mechanisms, for both real input surfaces:** `InputEvent::Scroll`'s own dispatch arm already hit-tests and walks up the parent chain to the nearest scrollable ancestor (M8 Phase 3, for `VirtualList`) — widened with a `NodeKind::Carousel` branch, not rebuilt; `Tree::dispatch`'s own real `self.dragging` mechanism (`Splitter`/`Slider`, M4 Phase 3/M14 Phase 2) — widened the same way, with a real, new twist: unlike `Splitter`/`Slider` (which only ever *are* the hit node), a carousel's drag must start no matter which real item was actually hit, so `PointerPressed` now also walks up from the hit node to find the nearest `NodeKind::Carousel` ancestor before starting the drag. Real snap motion: `Duration::from_millis(300)` + `MotionCurve::Standard`, MD3's own real "medium2"/"standard" token values, reused directly from pyCopper's own already-reasoned pick. Proven directly at the engine-core level with 5 new unit tests (hero items at rest match the real `_item_width` formula; a real halfway-ticked snap proves items resize continuously mid-travel, not on arrival; a wheel notch landing on an item, not the carousel's own body, snaps via the real parent-walk; a real drag crossing `CAROUSEL_DRAG_INDEX_THRESHOLD` commits exactly one index and release clears its own per-gesture bookkeeping; an Uncontained carousel keeps each item's own real width and clamps scroll to its real content extent — engine-core 163, up from 158). **A real, confirmed pytest-suite hazard found live, not predicted in advance:** a first draft of `tests/test_carousel.py` added its own real `App().run(max_frames=30)` call to prove `position` ticks across real frames — this made `test_terminal.py`'s own real shell-response test fail every time it ran afterward in the same pytest process (reproduced down to the exact two tests), a real, second `App.run()` invocation within one process being the identical "not a supported, tested pattern" fragility `test_terminal.py`'s own module doc comment already flags for calling it twice on the same `App`. Removed rather than worked around: the real "does a tick actually move it" claim is already proven for real at the Rust level (the halfway-ticked test above), matching the identical division of labor `test_checkbox.py`'s own doc comment already establishes (pytest proves the FFI surface; a real tick's own effect is proven in Rust). Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (40 binaries green, engine-core 163 up from 158)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, a real, direct empirical end-to-end script run before writing any pytest (real wheel notch synchronously moves the destination index, a real `App.run()` genuinely ticks position toward it, real Uncontained scroll clamps to its own real content extent), `pytest tests/` 494 passed/1 skipped (12 new in `test_carousel.py`, zero regressions after removing the real cross-test `App.run()` hazard above), all 67 examples (including the new `examples/carousel.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/carousel.py`.)

**Just closed:** M30 Phase 8 Step 5 — Status Bar (§5, §7, §11.2). A real, deliberate reuse rather than new engine work: `AppShell`'s own `build_shell` already accepted a pre-built `status_bar` region since §14 step 13 — this step only needed to build the real, styled content, confirmed to slot in directly with no adapter needed.

**Just closed:** M30 Phase 8 Steps 6-7 — Main Menu submenus, **closing Phase 8 entirely (all 7 steps)**. `add_menu_item` gained a real `submenu: bool` param adding a trailing `chevron_right` affordance (the twelfth curated icon); the submenu itself needed no new engine primitive, just another `build_menu`/`open_menu` pair anchored to the parent item's own returned `Node`. **A real, confirmed bug found live, not predicted in advance:** the new example's own first real run genuinely failed a real click on a submenu item never reached its own handler. Root-caused to `Tree::dismiss_overlays_outside`: since `open_overlay` always positions content anchor-relative-below, a submenu anchored to an item inside an already-open parent menu genuinely sits outside that parent menu's own bounds, so a press inside the submenu was wrongly read as "outside" the parent menu, dismissing it and swallowing the press before the submenu's own handler ran. Fixed with a new `inside_any_overlay` check: a press inside *any* open overlay can no longer dismiss a *different* one — a true no-op for every existing single-overlay caller, confirmed by the full pre-existing suite passing unmodified. Proven directly with a new, dedicated `engine-core` unit test (a genuine two-overlay nested scene, `engine-core` 151 up from 150) and a new Python-level test proving the parent menu's own survival behaviorally through a real `window.click()`-driven reproduction. **Up next:** M30 Phase 9 — Content Specialty (Video, Node Graph, Code Editor, Terminal, Carousel), the milestone's final phase.

**Just closed:** M30 Phase 9 Step 1 — Video (§5). `Window.add_video`/`Node.push_frame`, a real "frame sink" design directly grounded in the sibling `pyCopper` project's own `Video` widget — reuses `NodeKind::Image` verbatim, no new `NodeKind` needed. Found and fixed a real, confirmed architecture gap along the way: `engine-render`'s own GPU texture cache only ever uploaded an `Image` node's pixels once, correct before this phase (pixel data never changed) but silently wrong for a live video stream — fixed by keying re-upload on `peniko::Blob`'s own real content-identity id (an O(1) comparison, not a per-frame pixel memcmp), proven with a new, dedicated `engine-render` unit test.

**Just closed:** M30 Phase 9 Step 2 — Node Graph (§5). `Window.add_node_graph`/`Window.add_graph_node`, real title-bar-styled nodes reparented directly into a pannable/zoomable viewport, closing the exact gap M5 Phase 4's own `PLAN.md` named. Needed zero new engine-core/engine-render capability — confirmed by direct investigation (an empirical hit-test proof that `Position::Absolute` insets re-resolve relative to a node's real reparented ancestor, plus the already-existing `absolute_position_follows_an_ancestor_transform` proof) that pan/zoom/move are already fully expressible via `Node.animate("transform", ...)`. Found and fixed a real, live bug while verifying end to end: the node's own title-bar `Rect` intercepted clicks meant for the node itself, the same `hit_testable` bug class `Navigation Rail`/`Tabs` already found this milestone. Also caught and corrected a real self-inflicted mistake mid-step: a first draft silently overwrote the pre-existing `examples/node_graph.py` (M5 Phase 4's own still-valid Canvas/`CustomHitTest` example) — restored byte-for-byte before committing, with the new demonstration renamed to `examples/graph_editor.py`.

**Just closed:** M30 Phase 9 Step 3 — Code Editor (§5, §8, §10). `Window.add_code_editor`, a real, genuinely multiline `TextField` closing the real, stated single-line-only gap `TextField` always had — `Enter` inserts a genuine newline, `Home`/`End` operate per-line, `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown` (two genuinely new `engine_core::Key` variants) move by line preserving column. A real, load-bearing new engine-core capability, not a thin composition — threaded through `engine-platform`'s real winit key mapping and `Window.press_key`'s own synthetic-testing vocabulary. A real, deliberate v1 simplification caught and stated honestly by its own unit test: no persistent "goal column" memory across consecutive vertical moves. Honestly, deliberately deferred: syntax highlighting, a line-number gutter, real scroll/clip past the box's own edges, a bundled monospace font, and Tab-key indentation capture — each with a real, stated reason, not silently dropped.

Numbers in the gutter, tab/space indicators, collapsing?"), asked for a real second-pass milestone rather than leaving those as permanently-deferred gaps. ## Milestone 31 — Code Editor: Real IDE Functionality (Second Pass) (§5, §8, §10)

**Status: ✅ Complete (2026-09-18) — all 6 phases done.** User-directed, asked directly after M30 Phase 9 Step 3 shipped: "Does CodeEditor have syntax highlighting? Numbers in the gutter, tab/space indicators, collapsing, etc. The things you would expect from a code editor." — answered honestly (none of the four), then: "Add CodeEditor second pass, IDE functionality, to the next Milestone." Begun directly after M30 closed, per the user's own "Start M31."

**Real groundwork already exists, confirmed by direct source read before scoping any phase below, not assumed:** every real keyboard edit to a `TextField` (`Space`, multiline `Enter`, `Backspace`, `Delete`, and plain character `TextInput`) already returns `DispatchOutcome::Changed(field)` (`Tree::dispatch_text_field_key`/`Tree::dispatch`'s own `TextInput` arm), which `engine-py::dispatch.rs`'s `run_dispatch_outcome` already turns into a real `EventKind::Change` call to `Node.set_on_change` — itself already real and Python-facing since M14 Phase 3. This means an app can already register `editor.set_on_change(...)` and have it fire on *every* real keystroke edit, right now, with zero new engine work — the real, load-bearing fact Phase 1 below is built on. Separately, `parley::RangedBuilder` (already this project's own real layout-building API, `text.rs`'s `shaped_layout`/`build_field_layout`) has a real `push<'a>(property, range)` method distinct from `push_default` (confirmed via direct read of the vendored `parley-0.11.1`'s own `builder.rs`) — real, existing per-byte-range styling, not something that needs inventing from scratch, the real fact Phase 4 below is built on. And `draw_field` already has a real, proven precedent for painting a *different* string than `state.content` itself (`display_content`, spliced for a live IME preedit, M17 Phase 2) — the identical real pattern Phase 3 below reuses for whitespace glyphs.

**Real, honest ordering, not arbitrary:** phases below are sequenced cheapest/most-grounded first, most speculative/complex last — matching this file's own established discipline throughout M30. Phase 5 (folding) is explicitly the least-grounded phase in this milestone; pyCopper's own real `CodeEditor` deferred it too, for real, stated reasons this file's own Step 3 entry already quoted.

### Phase 1 — Line-Number Gutter ✅
- Step 1: A real, composed sibling gutter column — ✅ (a real per-line gutter for `Window.add_code_editor` — composed entirely from existing primitives, with zero new engine capability added at all. `BUILD_TRACKER.md`'s own scoping note flagged a real open question: aligning each gutter number to its own real painted editor line needs either a new `engine-py` per-line-position read-back, or a fixed line-height assumed in the app (stated as fragile). **Real, confirmed finding that resolved this before any code was written:** `TextRenderer::draw` (plain `Text`) and `TextRenderer::draw_field` (`TextField`) both build their real `parley::Layout` through the exact same `shaped_layout` method (confirmed by direct source read, not assumed) — so a gutter composed as an ordinary sibling `Text` node, with the identical `font_family`/`font_weight`/`font_size` as the editor and wide enough never to wrap, lines up with the editor's own real per-line Y positions *by construction*, since both go through the identical real shaping code path. The "fixed line-height assumed in the app" concern the scoping note raised turns out not to be fragile at all here: `CodeEditor`'s own multiline mode never wraps (`field_max_width` returns `f32::MAX`), so every real line shares the exact same font-metric line height by definition, not an approximation. Proven precisely at the Rust level with a new unit test (`a_plain_texts_own_multiline_content_lines_up_with_a_matching_multiline_textfields_own_lines`, `engine-render`, comparing real `parley::Layout::lines()`' own `block_min_coord` geometry directly between a Text-shaped and a TextField-shaped layout of the same content/font — identical, not merely close) — `engine-render` unit tests now cover this via the crate's own internal `#[cfg(test)]` module (white-box, calling the private `shaped_layout` method both node kinds already share). Real, live wiring: `editor.set_on_change(...)` (already real and Python-facing since M14 Phase 3, the groundwork note above already confirmed) recomputes the gutter's own content from `editor.get_text().count("\n") + 1` on every real keystroke edit — no new dispatch mechanism needed. Wrote `tests/test_code_editor_gutter.py` (4 tests: single-line/multi-line seeding, a real Enter growing the gutter live, a real Backspace merging two lines and shrinking it live) and `examples/code_editor_gutter.py` — both checked for filename collisions first. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (`engine-render` 5 up from 4)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt (no Rust-level engine-py/engine-core changes needed for this phase, only the new `engine-render` proof test), `pytest tests/` 498 passed/1 skipped (4 new, zero regressions, no second real `App.run()` introduced anywhere in this phase's own test suite, the real hazard M30 Phase 9 Step 5's own investigation found), all 68 examples (including the new `examples/code_editor_gutter.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/code_editor_gutter.py`.)

### Phase 2 — Tab-Key Indentation Capture ✅
- Step 1: A focused multiline field claims `Tab` before focus traversal — ✅ (a focused *multiline* `TextField` now claims `Tab` before ordinary focus traversal — `Tree::dispatch_text_field_key`'s own existing "first refusal, `None` means not mine" contract (already used for `ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight`/`Home`/`End`/`Enter`) widened to a new `Key::Tab` arm rather than a second, differently-shaped mechanism. Inserts a literal `\t` (real Design Principle 6: tabs-vs-spaces is app-level policy, not engine-core's to decide), replacing a real active selection first via the same shared `delete_selection` helper `Space`/`Enter` already use. A *single-line* `TextField` keeps its own prior real behavior byte-for-byte — `Tab` still falls through to `move_focus`, matching real desktop form convention (a single-line input was never a place a real indentation character belongs). `Escape` stays the real, unconditional way out of a focused field either way, mirroring pyCopper's own real "Escape still defocuses before per-element delivery" precedent, so Tab capture never traps the keyboard. Proven directly at the engine-core level with 2 new unit tests (a real Tab keypress inserts `\t` and keeps focus; a real active selection is replaced, not inserted beside — `engine-core` 165, up from 163) plus the pre-existing single-line Tab-still-moves-focus test re-confirmed unchanged. Extended `tests/test_code_editor.py` (2 new tests: multiline Tab indents in place and keeps focus; a single-line `TextField` still loses focus on Tab and inserts nothing) and `examples/code_editor.py` (a real Tab-key indentation step added to the existing live-edit sequence) rather than new files, since this is a real enhancement to Code Editor's own existing coverage, not a separate capability. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (`engine-core` 165 up from 163)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, a real, direct empirical script run before writing any pytest (a real Tab keypress on a focused multiline field inserts `\t` and keeps focus; a real Tab keypress on a focused single-line field still moves focus away), `pytest tests/` 500 passed/1 skipped (2 new, zero regressions), all 68 examples (including the updated `examples/code_editor.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/code_editor.py`.)

### Phase 3 — Tab/Space Indicators ✅
- Step 1: Visible glyphs for space/tab at paint time only — ✅ (real visible glyphs for space/tab at paint time only, exactly as scoped — a middle dot (`·`, U+00B7) for space and a rightward arrow (`→`, U+2192) for tab, the same real convention VS Code/Sublime Text use; `state.content` itself is never touched. New `TextFieldState.show_whitespace: bool` field (`false` default, every existing construction site's own byte-for-byte unchanged behavior, the identical `multiline`-established pattern), set `true` by `Window.add_code_editor` specifically — not exposed on `add_text_field`, since an ordinary form input showing dots for its own real spaces would be real, unasked-for visual noise. **Real, confirmed correctness problem found and solved, not glossed over:** `·`/`→` are multi-byte in UTF-8 while the space/tab they replace are one byte each, so a naive substitution would silently desync `state.cursor`/`selection_anchor`'s own real byte offsets from the substituted `Layout`'s own byte space — a broken click, caret position, or selection highlight, or worse, an out-of-bounds/mid-character byte offset handed back to `Tree::dispatch`'s own real cursor-mutation code. Solved with a real, deterministic bidirectional offset map (`to_display_offset`/`from_display_offset`, `engine-render::text`): correct because the substitution is exactly one real char in for one real char out, never expanding/collapsing multiple characters. `TextRenderer::draw_field` remaps `state.cursor`/`anchor`/`caret_at` through it before querying the substituted `Layout` (skipped only while a real IME preedit is simultaneously active — a vanishingly rare real combination, the preedit splice keeps its own already-correct unsubstituted offsets in that case); `TextRenderer::hit_test_position` builds the identical substituted `Layout` for a real click and maps the *returned* display-space byte offset back into `state.content`'s own real byte space before handing it to any caller. Proven directly: a new `engine-render` unit test (`display_offset_mapping_round_trips_every_real_char_boundary`) round-trips every real char boundary of a mixed ASCII/space/tab/multi-byte-UTF-8 string through both directions; a new `hit_test_position` integration test (`hit_test_position_on_a_field_with_visible_whitespace_returns_real_content_offsets`) proves a real click far past a short substituted field's own end resolves to `state.content.len()` (3, for `"a b"`) and not the longer substituted string's own length (4) — exactly the class of bug a broken remap would produce (`engine-render` 7 unit tests up from 5, plus a new integration test, 10 in `text_field_paint.rs` up from 9). Extended `tests/test_code_editor.py` (1 new test: a real space/tab-containing buffer, edited live, still reads back completely unsubstituted via `get_text()`) and `examples/code_editor.py` (docstring + a real `App.run()` re-run now exercising the whitespace-substitution paint path with live tab/space content, no crash) rather than new files. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (`engine-render` 7 unit tests up from 5, 10 in `text_field_paint.rs` up from 9)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, a real, direct empirical script run before writing any pytest (real content stays unsubstituted through `get_text()` after live typing spaces/tabs), `pytest tests/` 501 passed/1 skipped (1 new, zero regressions), all 68 examples (including the updated `examples/code_editor.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/code_editor.py`.)

### Phase 4 — Syntax Highlighting ✅
- Step 1: Real per-token coloring via `parley`'s own real per-range styling — ✅ (real per-token coloring via `parley`'s own real per-range styling, exactly as scoped — `RangedBuilder::push(StyleProperty::Brush(color), range)`, confirmed real, existing API. New `TextFieldState.syntax_spans: Vec<(Range<usize>, Color)>` (empty default, `engine-core` never interprets the ranges itself — app-side tokenization only, Design Principle 6, the identical real "no engine-bundled lexer" split pyCopper's own optional-Pygments design already established; literal RGBA, not a palette-token name, the identical real reasoning pyCopper's own module doc comment states — MD3 has only four real color roles against the roughly ten categories a real syntax theme needs). **Real, resolved technical question the scoping note left open:** whether `vello_hybrid` reads per-style-run brush data straight from a styled `Layout`, or whether `draw_field`'s own paint loop needed restructuring — resolved by direct source read: `vello_hybrid`'s own `Scene::glyph_run`/`fill_glyphs` never reads a brush at all (color stays the existing separate `scene.set_paint` mechanism), and — the real, load-bearing finding — a `parley::Run` does *not* necessarily split at every real style boundary (multiple differently-styled clusters can share one run for shaping efficiency); each individual `parley::Glyph` carries its own real `style_index` into `Layout::styles()` instead (the identical real lookup `parley::Cluster::first_style` itself uses), which `draw_field`'s own paint loop now reads directly, batching consecutive glyphs that resolve to the same real color into one `fill_glyphs` call each — mirrors the identical real "background/glyph run" batching `TextRenderer::draw_terminal` already uses, not one draw call per glyph. A real default `Brush` covering the whole content is pushed first (`at.color`, part of the real shaping-cache key now too), so every glyph outside every real span still resolves to a real, meaningful color rather than a fully-transparent `[0,0,0,0]` default. Real, per-span offset remapping shares the identical `to_display_offset` machinery M31 Phase 3 already built, applied identically whenever whitespace substitution is also active, so both real features stay correct together. **A real, confirmed bug found live by the very first pixel test written for this phase, not predicted in advance:** an initial design matched real per-*run* coloring against each `Run::text_range()` (assuming a `Brush` push always forces a run split) — a real three-way pixel-diff test (no spans / a span over the whole content / a span over only the first character) caught it immediately: the "half" and "whole" renders were pixel-identical, proving color had leaked past its own real span boundary. Root-caused directly via `parley::Cluster::first_style`'s own real source (`self.run.layout.styles()[style_index]`) and fixed by moving to the correct, real per-glyph `style_index` read-back above — the test then passed on the very next run. New `Node.set_syntax_spans([(start, end, (r,g,b,a)), ...])` replaces the whole list every call, matching a real re-tokenize-per-edit app pattern; `engine-core` performs no validation of overlapping/out-of-order ranges, the app's own concern. Extended `tests/test_code_editor.py` (2 new tests: real spans don't raise and never touch `get_text()`'s own content; a non-`TextField` node rejects the call) and `examples/code_editor.py` (a real, minimal app-side keyword tokenizer coloring "def"/"return", exercised through a real render loop) rather than new files. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (`engine-render` 7 unit tests unchanged, 11 in `text_field_paint.rs` up from 10, including the real pixel-diff proof that caught the bug above)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, a real, direct empirical script run before writing any pytest (real spans set without raising, `get_text()` stays unsubstituted, a non-`TextField` node correctly rejects the call), `pytest tests/` 503 passed/1 skipped (2 new, zero regressions), all 68 examples (including the updated `examples/code_editor.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/code_editor.py`.)

### Phase 5 — Code Folding ✅
- Step 1: The most speculative phase in this milestone, real and stated as such — ✅ (the most speculative phase in this milestone, real and confirmed as such before any design work — checked pyCopper's own real `CodeEditor` (the reference every prior phase this milestone grounded itself in) and confirmed it explicitly excludes code folding too, no real reference implementation existed anywhere to design this from. Paused and asked the user directly via `AskUserQuestion`, matching the same real discipline Terminal/Carousel already established for genuinely large, ungrounded builds; the user chose **"Full real folding (Recommended)"** — a real fold-state model, a real gutter toggle affordance, and real content-hiding, built from first-principles reasoning rather than an existing widget to verify against. New `TextFieldState.folded_ranges: Vec<Range<usize>>` (empty default, `engine-core` never interprets the ranges itself — deciding which real lines are foldable/currently folded is the app's own concern, the identical real "app's own concern" split `syntax_spans` already has, since this codebase has no code-structure awareness of its own). Real content-hiding reuses the identical `display_content`-splice pattern the milestone's own groundwork note already named (M17 Phase 2's IME preedit, verbatim) — each real folded range collapses to one visible "⋯" (U+22EF) marker (`engine-render::text::elide_folded_ranges`), the same real "something is hidden here" convention VS Code/Sublime Text both use, never a silent vanish. Real, new bidirectional byte-offset mapping (`to_display_offset_folded`/`from_display_offset_folded`, a real segment-based walk distinguishing verbatim vs. folded stretches) composes with M31 Phase 3's own real whitespace-substitution offset map and M31 Phase 4's own real syntax-span remap — `draw_field` now chains all three through one shared `to_display` closure so cursor/selection/caret/spans never desync from what's actually painted, whichever real combination of the three paint transforms is active. **Real, deliberate v1 limitation, decided and stated directly, not silently glossed over:** cursor navigation (`Tree::dispatch_text_field_key`'s own `Home`/`End`/`ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown`) is not fold-aware — a real cursor can still move into a folded region through `content`'s own real, unfolded bytes; the *displayed* caret clamps to right after the nearest fold marker in that case rather than drawing somewhere genuinely invisible. A real gutter toggle affordance composes entirely from existing primitives, no new engine capability needed for it at all — a small clickable `Rect` per foldable line, positioned using the identical real line-height approximation `engine_core::terminal_cell_size`'s own doc comment already states honestly (`font_size * 1.3`), with a real `set_on_click` handler flipping `set_folded_ranges` — the same real "compose from existing primitives" outcome Phase 1's own gutter already reached. Proven directly at the Rust level with 5 new unit tests (real elision collapses each range to one marker; a real malformed/overlapping/out-of-bounds range is skipped rather than corrupting output; the real fold-aware offset map round-trips every real char boundary outside a fold; a real offset strictly inside a fold clamps to the identical real display position right after its own marker; a real display offset landing on the marker itself resolves back to the fold's own real start — `engine-render` 12 unit tests, up from 7) plus 2 new integration tests (a real folded range paints genuinely different pixels than unfolded; a real click far past a folded field's own end resolves to `state.content`'s own real length, neither the shorter marker-collapsed display length nor an out-of-bounds offset — 13 tests in `text_field_paint.rs`, up from 11). New `Node.set_folded_ranges([(start, end), ...])` replaces the whole list every call, the identical real contract `set_syntax_spans` already has. Extended `tests/test_code_editor.py` (4 new tests: real ranges don't raise and never touch content; a non-`TextField` node rejects the call; folding and syntax highlighting compose without raising — a *second* real `App.run()` call was deliberately NOT added to this test, the real hazard M30 Phase 9 Step 5's own investigation already found and recorded) and a new `examples/code_editor_folding.py` (a real gutter toggle folding and unfolding a real function body through two real clicks) — checked for a filename collision first. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (`engine-render` 12 unit tests up from 7, 13 in `text_field_paint.rs` up from 11)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, a real, direct empirical script run before writing any pytest (real fold ranges set without raising, `get_text()` stays unsubstituted, a non-`TextField` node correctly rejects the call, fold+syntax+whitespace compose cleanly through a real render loop), `pytest tests/` 506 passed/1 skipped (4 new, zero regressions, no second real `App.run()` introduced), all 69 examples (including the new `examples/code_editor_folding.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/code_editor_folding.py`.)

### Phase 6 — Real Event-Loop Wake (`EventLoopProxy`) ✅
- Step 1: A real, generic wake mechanism for `App.run`'s own idle event loop, closing the real, stated v1 cost M30 Phase 9 Step 4 (Terminal) found and left open — ✅ (a real, generic wake mechanism for `App.run`'s own idle event loop, exactly as scoped — widened `engine-platform`'s own private `PlatformEvent` enum with a real, untargeted `Wake` variant (no `WindowId` payload, confirmed as the real, correct v1 answer this phase's own scoping note left open: redraws every real open window on a wake, the same real "whole-loop signal, not per-window" shape `any_active` itself already had) and exposed a new public `EventLoopWaker` handle (`Send` + `Clone`, wrapping the identical real `EventLoopProxy<PlatformEvent>` `WindowOpener` already wraps) that `run_windowed_multi`'s own `setup` closure now also receives, alongside the existing `WindowOpener` — the one real place able to reach a fresh proxy at all and hand a clone to an already-constructed background producer, since (confirmed via direct source read before designing this) every real `Window.add_terminal` call always happens before `App.run()` even starts, so every real `TerminalSession` already exists by the time `setup` runs. New `TerminalSession::set_waker`, called once per real session from that same `setup` closure (`engine-py::app.rs`); the real waker itself is shared with the session's own background PTY reader thread via the identical `Arc<Mutex<...>>` pattern `incoming` already uses, so a real waker registered later is still visible to a thread that was already running before it existed — the background thread now calls `waker.wake()` the instant real new PTY bytes actually arrive, not on some polling interval. **This closes the real, stated v1 cost M30 Phase 9 Step 4 (Terminal) found and left open, not just adds new capability alongside it:** the old `any_active` widening (`!terminals.is_empty()` kept the whole per-frame loop continuously polling for as long as any real terminal existed, "a window with a live terminal never goes fully idle") is now removed entirely — a window with a real but genuinely quiet terminal (nothing typed, nothing printed) can go fully idle exactly like any other window, the identical real win M29 Phase 2 already gave every other case, closing the gap this milestone's own groundwork explicitly named. Proven directly with a new, dedicated `engine-platform` integration test (`wake_event.rs`, `harness = false` like its real-main-thread-only siblings): a genuinely separate OS thread, holding only a clone of the real waker and nothing else this crate owns, calls `wake()` three times while a real `winit` event loop is genuinely running, and the window still completes its own real `max_frames` cleanly with no panic and no hang — the real, honest scope note stated directly: this proves the new cross-thread delivery path is wired correctly end to end, not that it improves idle CPU usage, a real performance property better suited to profiling than a pass/fail test. Re-ran `test_terminal.py`'s own existing real shell-response test and a fresh direct empirical script after removing the `any_active` widening — both still pass, confirming the real content-delivery path (unrelated to the wake mechanism's own idle-CPU benefit, still exercised every frame either way) stayed correct. No Python-facing API change at all — this phase is entirely internal `engine-platform`/`engine-py` wiring, needing no new `.pyi` stub, no new example. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (`engine-platform` +1 real integration test binary, `wake_event`)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, a real, direct empirical script re-confirming the terminal's own real shell-response behavior survives the `any_active` removal, `pytest tests/` 506 passed/1 skipped (unchanged — a pure internal wiring change, zero new/removed tests needed at that layer), all 69 examples (including `examples/terminal.py`, now exercised through the new wake path instead of continuous polling) and the showcase demo re-run clean. **This closes M31 Phase 6 and, with it, M31 itself, all 6 phases.**)

**Not scoped by this milestone:** a bundled monospace font (a real, separate asset/licensing decision Step 3's own entry already named, out of scope here too); real scroll/clip for a Code Editor taller/wider than its own box (the general "no `NodeKind` besides `VirtualList` clips today" gap, real and separate, not specific to this milestone); multi-cursor editing, a minimap, bracket auto-closing/matching, and any language-server integration (pyCopper's own real precedent excludes all four from its own v1 too, for the identical real reasons — an LSP client is an *application* concern, never a widget's own hard dependency).
