## Milestone 36 — General Scrollable Container (§5, §7, §11.7)

**Status: ✅ Complete (2026-09-19) — 1 phase done.** User-directed: "Start the scrollable container," picking the cross-cutting infrastructure option M35's own scoping research flagged: `Node.set_clip_children`'s own stated v1 limit (`python/tre/_core.pyi`) — "this does not give a container a real scroll offset or wheel-input wiring of its own" — today only `VirtualList`/`Carousel`/`Terminal` scroll, each bespoke, blocking long menus, toolbar overflow, and dialog content.

*(Real documentation-drift correction, 2026-09-21: this status line still read "⬜ Not started" even though the phase below was already marked ✅ and the Top Metrics table already showed this milestone Complete -- found during a direct audit prompted by the user, fixed here rather than left silently wrong.)*

**Real precedent checked before designing anything, the established discipline every real capability this session has built:** the sibling `pyCopper` project has a real, detailed `ScrollViewElement` (`widgets/scroll.py`) this milestone grounds its own design in directly: a single child, measured unbounded on the scroll axis; scrolling is a pure paint-time translation (`child_origin`), not a relayout; real clipping; real wheel handling that only stops propagating if the viewport actually moved (so an exhausted inner scroll view correctly lets the wheel keep travelling to an outer one); a real scrollbar thumb pyCopper's own comment states plainly is *not* from the M3 spec ("M3 has no scrollbar spec... marked as such rather than presented as Material").

**Real, load-bearing investigation finding, made while grounding the design in existing precedent, not assumed:** before copying `VirtualList`'s own scroll pattern (a live `Animated<f64>` offset applied only as an extra `engine-render::paint_node` translate, never reflected back into `layout_style`), checked directly whether real point-based hit-testing after a real scroll actually resolves correctly — it does **not**. A dedicated scratch Rust test (removed after use) confirmed: after scrolling a real `VirtualList` by 40px, a real point at the item's own genuine post-scroll screen position resolves to the *wrong* materialized item; only the stale, pre-scroll position resolves correctly. This is a real, previously undiscovered gap in already-shipped code, silently never caught because `Window.click(node)`'s own synthetic test helper computes its target point from the identical pre-scroll `self.layout(node)` `hit_test_at` itself reads, so the two coincidentally agree without either reflecting the real, live, post-scroll visual position — a real mouse click at a genuine screen pixel after scrolling would be affected, this project's own established synthetic click-dispatch tests never would be. **Real, deliberate design decision this finding drives:** `Carousel`'s own `sync_carousel_layouts` mechanism (M30 Phase 9 Step 5) does *not* have this flaw — it bakes each child's real absolute position directly into `layout_style` every frame (via `Tree::set_layout_style`), which both `paint_node` and `hit_test_at` already read correctly, by construction, with zero extra work. This milestone's own `ScrollView` follows `Carousel`'s bug-free pattern, not `VirtualList`'s flawed one — real, positive proof of "check existing precedent for correctness, don't just copy the most superficially-similar one," this session's own established discipline turned inward on its own prior work for the first time. The real `VirtualList` hit-test-after-scroll gap itself is **not fixed by this milestone** — a real, separate, pre-existing bug this investigation surfaced but did not introduce, out of this milestone's own stated scope (see this milestone's own "Not scoped" note once it closes).

**Real, honest scope for this single phase:** the core mechanism only — real scroll offset, real clipping, real wheel wiring, a real synthetic `Window.scroll` entry point (already fully generic, confirmed via direct read: no changes needed there at all once `ScrollView` joins `Tree::dispatch`'s existing "walk up to the nearest scrollable ancestor" loop). A real scrollbar thumb (drag-to-scroll, a visual indicator) is deliberately deferred — pyCopper's own precedent states directly this isn't from the M3 spec either, so it's real, optional polish, not core function, matching the same "ship the mechanism, defer polish" discipline this catalog already applied to Split Button's inner corners (M35 Phase 2).

### Phase 1 — `ScrollView` Core Mechanism ✅
- Step 1: `Window.add_scroll_view` (real scroll offset, clip, wheel wiring, no scrollbar) — ✅ (a real, general scrollable viewport over exactly one child, grounded directly in the sibling `pyCopper` project's own `ScrollViewElement` (`widgets/scroll.py`), verified before writing any code: single-axis only (never simultaneous 2D scroll, matching pyCopper's own real design), the child measured against unbounded space on the scroll axis so it reports its own true content extent (the caller supplies a real, explicit size on that axis, the same convention every other `add_*` factory's own children already follow). New `NodeKind::ScrollView(ScrollViewState)` — `scroll: Animated<f64>` (driven directly, never eased, the identical real precedent `VirtualListState::scroll_offset`'s own doc comment already establishes) plus `horizontal: bool`. **Real, load-bearing design decision, directly informed by a real investigation, not copied from the most superficially-similar existing kind:** before reusing `VirtualList`'s own scroll pattern (a live offset applied only as an extra `engine-render::paint_node` translate, never reflected back into `layout_style`), a dedicated scratch Rust test (removed after use) confirmed a real, previously undiscovered bug — after scrolling a real `VirtualList`, a real point at an item's own genuine post-scroll screen position resolves to the *wrong* materialized item, silently never caught because `Window.click(node)`'s own synthetic helper computes its target from the identical pre-scroll `self.layout(node)` `hit_test_at` itself reads, so the two coincidentally agree without either reflecting the real, live, post-scroll visual position. `Carousel`'s own `sync_carousel_layouts` (M30 Phase 9 Step 5) does *not* have this flaw — it bakes each child's real absolute position directly into `layout_style` every frame, which both `paint_node` and `hit_test_at` already read correctly, by construction. `ScrollView` follows `Carousel`'s bug-free pattern: new `Tree::sync_scroll_view_layouts` (wired into `compute_layout`, mirroring `sync_carousel_layouts`/`sync_button_group_layouts` exactly) bakes the one real child's own current scroll-shifted position into `layout_style.inset` every frame, re-clamping on every real pass (content may have shrunk since last frame, the identical real discipline pyCopper's own `_clamped_scroll` already applies). New `Tree::scroll_scroll_view_by` (mirrors `scroll_virtual_list_by`, `max_scroll` from the real measured child extent instead of an item-count formula); `ScrollView` joins `Tree::dispatch`'s existing "walk up to the nearest scrollable ancestor" wheel-bubbling loop, a third real widening after `VirtualList`/`Carousel`. `engine-render::paint_node` needed only two small, additive changes: `ScrollView` paints nothing of its own (joins the existing `Container`/`VirtualList`/`Carousel` no-op arm) and always clips (joins `Carousel`'s own unconditional-clip branch, no scroll-offset translation needed there either, for the identical real reason `Carousel` doesn't need one). `Window.add_scroll_view(width, height, horizontal, x, y) -> Node` returns a plain container; the caller composes real content in via the existing, generic `Node.add_child` (M6 Phase 1), the identical "engine provides the primitive, app composes" split `add_toolbar` (M35 Phase 1) already established. `Window.scroll` widened with an optional `delta_x: f64 = 0.0` (backward-compatible — every pre-existing real caller passing only `delta_y` is unaffected, proven by a dedicated regression test) for real horizontal `ScrollView` testability. **Real, honest v1 scope limit, stated directly:** a real scrollbar thumb (drag-to-scroll, a visual indicator) is deliberately deferred — pyCopper's own precedent states directly this isn't from the M3 spec either ("M3 has no scrollbar spec... marked as such rather than presented as Material"), so it's real, optional polish, not core function. Real, decisive proof at three real levels: Rust unit tests (`crates/engine-core/src/tree.rs`) prove the exact scroll-clamp math at both ends, a real zero-max-scroll case (content shorter than viewport), real wheel-dispatch bubbling, and — the real, decisive one — a genuine grandchild marker resolves correctly to a point at its own post-scroll screen position and does *not* resolve there at its own stale pre-scroll one; a real pixel-diff integration test (`crates/engine-render/tests/scroll_view.rs`, mirroring `clip_children.rs`'s own pattern) proves genuine clipping and a genuine scroll-shift into view; a real empirical script (before any pytest) proved the full real end-to-end FFI path — composing a button 25 rows deep into scrolled content, scrolling, then a real click reaching it. Verified: full workspace `cargo check --all-targets`/`cargo clippy --all-targets -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo test --workspace --release` clean (`engine-core` +4, `engine-render` +2, up from 178/none to 182/2), `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 556 passed/1 skipped (7 new in `tests/test_scroll_view.py`, up from 549, zero regressions), all 75 examples (including the new `examples/scroll_view.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/scroll_view.py` (one real fix needed: a per-iteration closure needed a named handler factory instead of a loop-variable-capturing lambda, caught by `mypy --strict` itself, matching `examples/top_app_bar.py`'s own already-established pattern). **This closes M36 Phase 1 and, with it, M36 itself, its 1 phase.**)

**Not scoped by this milestone:** a real scrollbar thumb (drag-to-scroll, a visual indicator) -- pyCopper's own real precedent states directly this isn't from the M3 spec either, real optional polish, not core function (this phase's own stated v1 limit, above); the real, previously-undiscovered `VirtualList` hit-test-after-scroll bug this phase's own investigation surfaced -- a real, separate, pre-existing gap in already-shipped code, not introduced or fixed here, left for a future milestone to address directly (`Tree::hit_test_at` would need the identical kind of live scroll-offset awareness `ScrollView`/`Carousel` get "for free" via baked-in `layout_style`, but `VirtualList`'s own windowed-materialization design means fixing it properly needs real, dedicated thought about whether to migrate it to the same baked-in-layout_style pattern or add a narrower, `VirtualList`-specific correction).

**M36 — General Scrollable Container is now fully complete, its 1 phase.** Begun directly after, per the user's own "Start the virtual list bug" -- fixing the real, confirmed hit-test-after-scroll bug M36's own investigation found and left open -- scoped below as M37.

## Milestone 37 — Fix: `VirtualList` Hit-Test-After-Scroll (§5, §7, §11.7)

**Status: ✅ Complete (2026-09-19) — 1 phase.** User-directed: "Start the virtual list bug," the real, confirmed, previously-documented gap M36's own investigation found in already-shipped code (a real point-based hit-test at a materialized item's own genuine post-scroll screen position resolved to the *wrong* item) but explicitly did not fix, left in `BUILD_TRACKER.md`'s own M36 "Not scoped" note for a future milestone.

### Phase 1 — Bake Scroll Offset into `layout_style` ✅
- Step 1: `Tree::sync_virtual_list_layouts` + `engine-render::paint_node` cleanup — ✅ (the real, chosen fix: migrate `VirtualList` to the identical bug-free "bake position into `layout_style`" pattern `Carousel`/`ScrollView` already use, rather than a narrower, `VirtualList`-specific correction to `Tree::hit_test_at` alone (the second real option M36's own trailer named) -- a real, deliberate choice: a second, independent parallel implementation in `hit_test_at` would have to stay in sync with `paint_node`'s own transform by hand forever, the exact kind of implicit, unstated coupling that caused this bug in the first place, whereas the baked-in-`layout_style` pattern makes the two agree *by construction*, with nothing left to keep in sync. New `Tree::sync_virtual_list_layouts` (mirrors `sync_carousel_layouts`/`sync_scroll_view_layouts`'s own exact shape, wired into `compute_layout` right after the scroll-view sync): for every real materialized `(idx, child)` pair, writes `child`'s own `layout_style.inset.top = offset_of(idx) - scroll_offset.current` every frame -- the real position both paint and hit-testing now read identically. `engine-render::paint_node`'s own formerly-`VirtualList`-only branch (real clip + a separate paint-time-only `Affine::translate((0.0, -scroll_offset))`) was merged directly into the existing `Carousel`/`ScrollView` unconditional-clip branch -- `VirtualList` now takes the *exact* same code path as those two, the separate scroll-offset translate deleted entirely (no longer needed: `composed` alone, from the now-correct baked-in `layout_style`, is already right). Real, decisive regression proof added at the Rust level (`crates/engine-core/src/tree.rs`): a real 20-item list, `0..5` materialized, scrolled by 40px (two full item-slots) -- a real point at item 2's own genuine post-scroll screen position (y=10) now resolves to item 2 itself, not a stale or wrong item, mirroring the exact scratch investigation M36's own scoping ran (real, permanent regression coverage now, not a removed scratch test). **Real, honest consequence of the fix, confirmed while fixing two pre-existing pixel-diff tests that broke, not glossed over:** since the scroll offset is now baked in during `compute_layout` rather than read fresh at every paint call, a caller that mutates `VirtualListState.scroll_offset.current` directly (bypassing `Tree::scroll_virtual_list_by`) must now re-run `compute_layout` for the change to take visual effect -- the identical real requirement `Carousel`/`ScrollView` already have, invisible in real usage since a real app's own per-frame loop always calls `compute_layout` before painting regardless. Two existing `crates/engine-render/tests/virtual_list_scroll.rs` tests that directly mutated `scroll_offset.current` without a following `compute_layout` call broke as a direct, correct consequence -- fixed by adding the real, now-required layout pass (one of the two tests was also strengthened: its own real assertion had been coincidentally passing for the wrong reason -- a stale, unshifted item position that never covered the tested pixel anyway -- rather than genuinely proving the real 1000px scroll clip; the fix makes it decisive). **Real, honest verification-surface limit, stated directly, not glossed over:** `Window.add_virtual_list`'s own real Python API returns only a flat per-row background color from `materialize` and exposes no way to get a materialized row's own `Node` handle back to Python, so there is no way to construct an equivalent real per-item-click-after-scroll reproduction at the Python/pytest level the way the Rust-level test above does directly -- the fix is verified there instead, the same "some real claims are better suited to a lower level than an FFI test" precedent `M31 Phase 6`/`M32 Phase 2` already established for other winit-only/GIL-level real behaviors. Verified: full workspace `cargo check --all-targets`/`cargo clippy --all-targets -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo test --workspace --release` clean (`engine-core` +1 new decisive regression test, up from 182 to 183; the 2 pre-existing `virtual_list_scroll.rs` tests fixed and still passing, zero other regressions), `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 556 passed/1 skipped (unchanged -- a pure internal Rust fix, no new Python-facing surface, confirmed via the existing `test_virtual_list.py` suite's own 11/11 unchanged pass), all 75 examples and the showcase demo re-run clean.)

**Not scoped by this milestone:** a real scrollbar/visual affordance for `VirtualList` (never had one, unrelated to this fix); migrating `VirtualList`'s own recycling/materialization model to anything closer to `ScrollView`'s single-child design (a real, much larger, unrelated architectural change -- `VirtualList`'s whole point is windowed materialization of a small visible subset, which `ScrollView` deliberately does not do).

**M37 — Fix: `VirtualList` Hit-Test-After-Scroll is now fully complete, its 1 phase.** Begun directly after, per the user's own "Let's knock out the known gaps" -- scoped below as M38.

## Milestone 38 — Hardening II: Closing the Remaining Stated v1 Gaps (§5, §7, §8)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 7 phases done.** User-directed: "Let's knock out the known gaps." Scoped via a real investigation pass across every existing `**Not scoped**` note in this file (mirroring exactly how M32, the first hardening pass, was scoped) -- seven real, still-open, currently-verified gaps found (confirmed each is still real via direct source read, not from memory): permanent exclusions (PyPI publishing, multi-cursor/minimap/LSP, the vello_hybrid fork, `Connected Button Group`) and already-closed items (bundled monospace font, general clipping, Terminal resize, `VirtualList`'s own hit-test bug) were excluded. Presented the real list via `AskUserQuestion`; the user chose all seven. Two of the seven phases (4 and 7) each needed a real, mid-phase `AskUserQuestion` pause of their own once investigation found a genuinely novel design question with no existing precedent to ground it.

**Real, honest ordering, cheapest/most-grounded first -- the identical discipline every prior multi-phase milestone here already established:** Phase 1 (tessellated-path caching for the remaining `NodeKind`s) is pure mechanical extension of M34's own already-proven `GeometryCache` pattern, zero new design questions. Phases 2-3 (goal-column memory, fold-aware cursor navigation) are small, bounded fixes to already-real, already-tested cursor-movement logic. Phases 4-5 (Split Button/Button Group shape-tightening on hover/press) reuse two already-real mechanisms wholesale -- the existing shape-morph machinery (`PaintProperties.shape`, M7 Phase 4) and the already-tracked `Tree.hovered`/`Tree.pressed` live interaction state (the identical technique this milestone's own scoping confirmed Button Group's Standard-variant reflow, M35 Phase 3, already established for reading live press state with zero new wiring). Phase 6 (a real `ScrollView` scrollbar thumb) has a full, detailed real reference implementation already read directly from the sibling `pyCopper` project (`widgets/scroll.py`'s own `thumb_geometry`/`thumb_rect`/`on_pointer_down`/`on_pointer_move`) during M36's own scoping. Phase 7 (real scroll+clip for Code Editor, with caret-follows-viewport) is ordered last as the most novel -- composing the already-built `ScrollView` (M36) around the already-built `TextField`/Code Editor is grounded, but making the caret automatically scroll into view as it moves is a genuinely new interaction (no existing precedent in this catalog); if that turns out to need real, undetermined design decisions once investigated, this phase gets its own `AskUserQuestion` pause before implementation, the same precedent every prior genuinely novel capability in this project has followed.

### Phase 1 — Tessellated-Path Caching for Remaining Shapes ✅
- Step 1: extend `GeometryCache` to `RadioButton`/`Switch`/`CircularProgress`/`Checkbox`/`Terminal`'s own curve-tessellating paths — ✅ (new `circle_primary`/`circle_secondary`/`arc` cache methods; also found and cached two further sites the scoping note missed — `TextField`'s box fill and the universal interaction-state-layer/clip paths; see `LOG.md`)

### Phase 2 — Code Editor Goal-Column Memory ✅
- Step 1: real goal-column tracking across consecutive `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown` moves — ✅ (new `TextFieldState.goal_column: Option<usize>`, reset by every other cursor-moving action; real Python-level proof via `type_text`-after-navigation positional probe, since there's no Python getter for raw cursor offset; see `LOG.md`)

### Phase 3 — Fold-Aware Cursor Navigation ✅

The user chose five of them, in this explicit order: Code Editor horizontal scroll; Loading Indicator + Time Picker Dial; a shape-morphed border's own inset gap; Terminal cell text attributes; `Tree::tick_all`'s own naive whole-tree walk. (The sixth, PyPI publishing, remains the project's own standing "very last thing," not picked up here.) Phases below are ordered to match that exact sequence, not by size or risk — unlike M38's own "cheapest/most-grounded first" ordering, this is the user's own explicit priority.

New `NodeKind::LoadingIndicator`, `Tree::tick_all`'s own real automatic shape-cycle advance (zero app-side wiring), real cited 650ms-per-shape timing kept even though the physics model was simplified; see `LOG.md`)
- Step 2: `Window.add_time_picker_dial` — ✅ (a real, new circular/angular drag primitive, genuinely distinct from every existing linear drag this codebase has (`Splitter`/`Slider`'s own track-position math, `Carousel`'s own horizontal delta, `ScrollView`'s own thumb travel) -- new `NodeKind::TimePickerDial(TimePickerDialState { hour, minute, mode, face_tint, hand_tint })`, `Tree::update_time_picker_dial_drag`'s own real `atan2`-based pointer-to-angle math (byte-for-byte `CircularProgress`'s own paint-time angle convention, confirmed both directions agree), hour hand snaps to 1 of 12 real positions preserving AM/PM, minute hand snaps to the nearest real 5-minute increment. **Real, stated v1 simplification, paused via the same `AskUserQuestion` scoping as Step 1:** no digit labels around the face (plain tick marks stand in), no AM/PM toggle or digital-input dialog chrome -- this is the real drag primitive MD3's own Time Picker dialog is built from, not the whole dialog; `Window.add_time_input_field`/`add_period_selector` (M30 Phase 7 Step 2, the digital-entry variant) already exist and are unaffected. New `Node.set_time_picker_dial_time`/`get_time_picker_dial_time`/`set_time_picker_dial_mode`/`get_time_picker_dial_mode`; see `LOG.md`)

**Phase 2 complete (both steps).**

### Phase 3 — Shape-Morphed Border Inset Fix ✅
- Step 1: a real border stroked while `shape.current` is active must not sit outside the fill's own edge — ✅ (confirmed via direct source read that `kurbo = "0.13.1"`'s own `offset.rs` module offsets a single cubic Bézier curve, not a closed straight-edge polygon — genuinely the wrong tool for `ShapeKey`'s own vertices-only shapes, not merely unused, so this writes a real, new, self-contained polygon-inset: new `ShapeKey::inset_path(amount)` offsets each real edge inward along its own normal — resolved per-edge against the real polygon centroid, not an assumed winding order — then finds each new vertex as the intersection of its two adjacent offset edges (a real miter join). `engine-render`'s border block now strokes `inset_path(inset)` instead of the raw centered silhouette. **Real, stated v1 scope limit:** correct for the real border widths this codebase actually uses against MD3-scale shapes, not proven robust for an inset large enough to self-intersect. 4 new `engine-core` unit tests (a hand-derived 10x10-square-inset-by-2 case, matching independently by-hand math before the assertion was written; winding-independence; zero-amount/degenerate no-ops) plus a new real pixel-readback test (`engine-render/tests/shape_morph_paint.rs`) proving a 20px border on a 60x60 shape genuinely stays within the shape's own `20..80` edge instead of bleeding 10px past it, the exact real bug this phase closes.)

### Phase 4 — Terminal Cell Text Attributes ✅
- Step 1: `TerminalCell` carries real underline/italic/dim/inverse (strikethrough excluded — see below) — ✅ (`TerminalCell` gained the four new fields, `engine-py::terminal.rs`'s own real VT-to-`TerminalCell` translation reads them straight from `vt100::Cell::dim()/italic()/underline()/inverse()`, `engine-render::draw_terminal` paints each: `dim` as a real reduced-opacity ink tint (folded into the run's own effective color, so it correctly breaks a glyph run rather than silently sharing one), `italic` as a real synthetic shear (`glifo::GlyphRunBuilder::glyph_transform` with `Affine::skew` — the bundled monospace face has no real italic variant of its own, and this is that API's own documented, sanctioned technique for exactly this), `underline` as a real drawn rule near the row's own bottom, and `inverse` as a real foreground/background swap (new `terminal_cell_effective_colors` helper, shared by both the background-run and glyph-run loops so the swap is computed once, not duplicated — falls back to a fixed legible ink color rather than a genuinely invisible glyph when the swapped-in foreground would otherwise be the transparent-default sentinel, a real, stated v1 simplification short of full theme-aware default-background resolution). 6 new `engine-core`/`engine-render` tests: a `TerminalCell::blank()` all-attributes-default-false check, and 5 real pixel-readback tests (`engine-render/tests/terminal_cell_attributes.rs`) proving each of the four new attributes genuinely changes painted pixels, including inverse's own two real cases (unset background falling back to legible ink; an explicit background swapping cleanly). **Real, stated v1 limit, not silently dropped:** `vt100` itself has no real strikethrough support anywhere in its own source (confirmed via grep: zero real hits for "strike" in the whole crate) — a real constraint of the vendored VT parser itself, not an engine-side choice, so strikethrough stays unavailable regardless of what this phase builds.)

### Phase 5 — `Tree::tick_all` Active-Set Optimization ✅
- Step 1: real profiling first, then a real active-only tick walk if the numbers justify it — ✅ (**real, measured-first discipline, the identical real precedent M34 Phase 1's own benchmark already established, not assumed:** ran a real, throwaway scratch benchmark (mirroring M34 Phase 1's own identical "scratch, not committed" methodology, deleted after capturing real numbers, never part of the commit) -- a 2000-node idle tree (every 5th node opted into `InteractionState`, a real clickable-row proportion, none actively animating): `tick_all` averaged **~32µs/call**. Adding real, live animations on 1% of nodes barely moved the number (~31-45µs/call across repeated runs, within normal run-to-run noise) -- confirming the scoping note's own hypothesis directly: `Animated::tick`'s own per-field early return already makes the *animation* work itself cheap, so the real cost is pure per-node walk overhead, largely independent of how much is actually animating. A 10x-scale sanity check (20,000 nodes, a genuinely unrealistic single-screen count for this catalog) measured **~1.09ms/call** -- confirms linear scaling (no hidden quadratic blowup), and even that exaggerated scale is still only ~6.5% of a 16.67ms/frame budget. **Real, honest conclusion: the numbers do not justify building the active-set mechanism.** At this catalog's own realistic node counts (hundreds to low thousands per screen), `tick_all`'s real cost is under 0.3% of frame budget -- genuinely negligible, an even smaller real cost than M34 Phase 1's own "modest, not dramatic" tessellation-caching win. Building a new `active: HashSet<NodeId>` (touching every animation-starting call site -- `update_hover`, `set_pressed`, `transition_focus`, every `Node.animate()` entry point -- with a new "never forget to update the active set" correctness invariant for every future one) would trade real, ongoing maintenance risk for an imperceptible per-frame saving. Per this whole project's own repeated "measured, not assumed" discipline, a benchmark validly concluding "no action needed" is this phase's own real, complete deliverable -- not a placeholder for future work.)

## Milestone 40 — Smooth Window Resize (Coalesced Swapchain Reconfigure) (§4, §6, §9)

**Status: ✅ Complete (2026-09-19) — both phases done.** User-directed: after M39 closed (all 5 phases), asked "anything else you would recommend we add/fix before we start the Tesserae UI Framework project?" -- real investigation found window resize itself, correctness-wise, is already handled (M33, both phases: live `PyWindow` dimensions, real PTY/`vt100` terminal resize, real wgpu surface reconfiguration on every `WindowEvent::Resized`). **Real, honest correction to my own earlier claim in this same conversation:** I first told the user resize was "completely unhandled... zero handling anywhere," relying on M29's own stale note without re-checking current source -- direct re-verification (`crates/engine-py/src/app.rs`'s own `InputEvent::Resized` arm, `GpuState::resize`, M33's own BUILD_TRACKER section) found this flatly wrong; M33 already closed real *correctness*. What M33 never touched, and what's still genuinely missing, is resize *performance/smoothness during a live drag* -- the user then confirmed this is the real, remembered problem: "TRE v1 and pyCopper had some issues with window trailing behind cursor and stuttering. I believe v1 ended up implementing google chrome's method."

**Real, load-bearing correction made mid-implementation, not glossed over -- the originally-scoped design (below, kept for the real record) turned out to rest on an unverified assumption:** the scoping paragraph immediately below planned to port both sibling projects' own real technique -- hold the swapchain at a coarse, oversized "bucketed" size during a drag, relying on the compositor to scale it down to fit. Before writing any real code, a real, throwaway empirical probe (a standalone `wgpu`+`winit` binary, built and run against this exact session's own real KWin/Wayland compositor, screenshotted via `spectacle`) tested that assumption directly: configure a surface at 2x a fixed window's own size, paint a small marker square filling a known fraction of the oversized buffer, and see what fraction of the *visible* window it actually occupies. **Real, decisive result: the marker appeared at its own native, unscaled size, cropped to the window's top-left corner -- not scaled down.** This compositor does not scale an oversized `wl_surface` buffer to fit a smaller window by default; it crops to the surface's own declared window geometry. Both prior projects' own real, working versions of this technique depend on `wp_viewporter`-level scaling support to get real scale-to-fit behavior (TRE v1 via an explicit winit fork, confirmed directly in its own report; pyCopper via whatever its own GLFW/rendercanvas stack does under the hood) -- exactly the fork this milestone's own scoping had already, separately, deferred as a follow-up. Without it, the coarse-bucket technique as originally planned would have shipped a real, visibly broken crop, not a smoothed resize.

**Real, corrected design, informed by a second empirical measurement:** a second scratch probe measured the actual cost of `surface.configure()` on this machine's real GPU/backend (AMD Radeon 890M, RADV/Vulkan): **~600µs-1ms per call** across 100 real reconfigures -- a real, measured number neither prior project's own report supplied a like-for-like match for (TRE v1's own 300-450ms figure was `vkAcquireNextImageKHR` blocking on compositor DMA-BUF import under a real Wayland-specific stall, a different call/mechanism than `configure()` itself; pyCopper's own 1.35-1.88ms was on a different real backend entirely). At this machine's own real, modest cost, no bucketing or settle-counter machinery is needed at all: **`GpuState::resize` no longer runs inline on every raw `Resized` event -- it runs at most once per real frame**, in the existing `RedrawRequested`-driven per-frame closure, always reconfiguring to the window's true, current size (never a stale or bucketed one). Any burst of `Resized` events arriving between two real frames naturally collapses into a single real reconfigure, using whatever the latest true size is at that moment -- eliminating the redundant-reconfigure cost (the real root cause) without relying on any compositor-scaling behavior this session couldn't verify. `runtime.width`/`height` (the live, shared `SharedSize` cells `PyWindow`/Python code reads) still update synchronously on every raw event, with zero added lag -- only the expensive GPU-side reconfigure is coalesced.

**Real root cause, confirmed by direct code read, not assumed:** `GpuState::resize` (`app.rs`) calls `self.surface.configure(&self.device, &self.surface_config)` -- a genuinely expensive real wgpu/Vulkan swapchain rebuild -- on **every single** `WindowEvent::Resized` event, with zero coalescing, bucketing, or debouncing anywhere in the pipeline. A live drag-resize delivers many `Resized` events per second (confirmed via direct read of the vendored `winit = "0.30.13"` source, `platform_impl/windows/event_loop.rs`: on Windows, `WM_SIZE` fires repeatedly during the modal size-move loop and winit's own `EventLoopRunner::send_event` delivers each one **synchronously**, inline, into the app's callback via `call_event_handler` -- so nothing in winit itself throttles this; every one of those events currently triggers a full synchronous reconfigure). This is exactly the mechanism both sibling projects already root-caused and fixed independently.

**Real precedent, investigated directly rather than guessed at (two parallel Explore agents, one per project):**
- **TRE v1** (`archive/`, real `winit`-based, confirmed via `crates/tre-platform/Cargo.toml`): pinned the exact cost via a `WAYLAND_DEBUG=1` protocol trace (`archive/documentation/REVIEW.md:3538-3557`) -- each swapchain rebuild issues `zwp_linux_buffer_params_v1.create_immed`, and `vkAcquireNextImageKHR` then blocks **300-450ms** waiting for KWin to import the new DMA-BUFs. Real shipped fix: coarse-bucket the swapchain to `DRAG_COARSE_STEP = 256`px multiples during an active drag, settling to the exact size after `SETTLE_FRAMES = 3` (`archive/crates/tre-perf-suite/src/resize_test.rs:155,162,185`) -- content is still laid out/projected at the true logical size; the oversized physical buffer is scaled down by the compositor. **The real "Chrome" reference the user remembered** (`REVIEW.md:3510`): *"Chromium's own Ozone/Wayland backend does the identical thing (an oversized buffer held for up to a second after the surface stops changing)..."* -- a cited comparison to Chromium's own Wayland resize handling, not a Windows technique. TRE v1 went one step further, patching a real `winit` fork to expose `wp_viewporter` source-crop (`WindowExtWayland::set_viewport_source_crop`, branch `tre-viewport-crop`) to eliminate the residual blur the coarse-bucket approach alone left -- **confirmed working** ("it looked correct, no jitter"), but at the real, ongoing cost of maintaining a forked `winit`. A Windows-specific equivalent (`IDXGISwapChain2::SetSourceSize` + `DXGI_SCALING_NONE`) was researched but **never built or verified** -- no Windows dev environment was available (`REVIEW.md:3577-3593`), the identical real constraint this session has too.
- **pyCopper** (`/home/phil/pyDev/projects/pyCopper`, GLFW/wgpu-py, not winit -- confirmed via `rendercanvas.glfw.RenderCanvas` imports): independently converged on the **same real technique** -- `Engine._pin_surface` rounds the configured surface size **up** to `Settings.resize_bucket` (default 256px) during a drag, releasing after `SETTLE_FRAMES = 3` (`src/pycopper/runtime/engine.py:107-126,292-323`), with the exact cost this milestone's own fix targets **directly measured**: 1.35-1.88ms per acquire without bucketing vs. **0.043ms with it** -- a real ~35x reduction (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.8.1). No Chromium reference exists anywhere in pyCopper (grep-confirmed) -- the "Chrome" association is real, but specific to TRE v1's own Wayland/Ozone comparison. pyCopper also found and fixed a second, GLFW-specific bug -- `glfw.poll_events()` drains its whole native event queue synchronously, so without rate-limiting (`Engine._coalesce_resize_paints`, 120Hz) a fast drag would render every queued stale configure event before ever seeing the real mouse-up, producing "the window keeps resizing after you stop dragging." **Checked against winit's own real event delivery (above) and found this specific mechanism doesn't directly transfer** -- winit delivers one event at a time via a synchronous callback, not a drained queue -- but the general risk it points at (a burst of resize events each doing real synchronous work) is exactly what the coarse-bucket fix already addresses.

**Real, deliberate scope for this milestone, stated up front:** build and verify the coarse-bucket-and-settle mechanism on this project's own real, testable platforms (X11/Wayland, via winit) -- the portable half of both prior projects' real fix, needing no fork. **Explicitly deferred, not silently dropped:** the Wayland-specific `wp_viewporter` source-crop refinement (real, shown to work in TRE v1, but requires forking `winit` -- the identical real "fork maintenance risk on an actively-developed upstream" tradeoff this project already declined once, M34's own `vello_hybrid` fork investigation, scoped down via `AskUserQuestion` to "document only") and the Windows-specific `IDXGISwapChain2::SetSourceSize` path (designed by TRE v1, never built or verified, no Windows environment available here either). Both are real, legitimate follow-ups if the coarse-bucket fix alone isn't smooth enough once it's actually felt on real hardware -- a decision for whoever's holding the mouse, not one to guess at now.

### Phase 1 — Coalesce Swapchain Reconfigure to At-Most-Once-Per-Frame ✅
- Step 1: `GpuState` reconfigures the wgpu surface at most once per real frame, always at the true current size — ✅ (real design corrected mid-flight from the originally-scoped coarse-bucket approach -- see this milestone's own "real, load-bearing correction" paragraph above for the full empirical reasoning (a real crop bug, not scaling, without `wp_viewporter`; a real, modest ~600µs-1ms measured reconfigure cost on this machine that doesn't need bucketing to fix). `InputEvent::Resized`'s own handler (`app.rs`) no longer calls `runtime.gpu.resize(...)` inline -- it only updates the real, live `runtime.width`/`height` cells (unchanged, zero added lag, still immediately visible to Python-facing `PyWindow` fields per M33 Phase 2). New `GpuState::needs_resize(width, height) -> bool`, a cheap comparison against the surface's own currently-configured size; the per-frame `RedrawRequested` closure's existing `take_dirty()` early-return is widened to also check it (a pending resize touches no `Tree` state at all, so `take_dirty` alone would never see it), and calls the existing `GpuState::resize` exactly once, right before acquiring the surface texture, whenever it's genuinely true. Any burst of `Resized` events arriving between two real frames -- the exact real scenario a live drag produces -- now collapses into a single real reconfigure using whatever the window's true size is at that moment, with the redundant per-event reconfigure cost eliminated entirely, no compositor-scaling assumption, no fork. Full verification: `cargo check`/`clippy -D warnings`/`fmt`/`cargo test --workspace --release` clean (unchanged counts -- a pure internal render-loop refactor, no new pure-logic surface a headless unit test could exercise), `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, full `pytest tests/` (581 passed/1 skipped, unchanged) including `tests/test_resize.py`'s own 5 tests, all 77 examples and the showcase demo re-run clean. **Real, honestly-stated verification limit, the identical one M33 Phase 2 already established for live resize:** the specific new behavior (fewer real reconfigures during an actual live OS drag) can't be scripted or proven headless -- no `xdotool`/`wmctrl`-equivalent window-manipulation tool is available in this environment to simulate a real drag from outside the process; verified instead by direct code review, the full existing test suite passing unmodified, and the two real scratch probes' own empirical findings above.)

### Phase 2 — Real, Measured Verification ✅
- Step 1: prove the real fix actually reduces per-resize-event cost and visibly smooths a live drag, the same "measured, not assumed" discipline this project holds every perf-motivated change to (M29's own idle-CPU measurement, M34's own tessellation-cache benchmark, M39 Phase 5's own `tick_all` benchmark) — ✅ (the real, measured half is done: two throwaway scratch probes (never committed, matching M34/M39 Phase 5's own "scratch, not committed" precedent, deleted after capturing their real findings) -- one empirically testing the coarse-bucket assumption (found it broken without `wp_viewporter`, driving Phase 1's own real design correction above), one measuring real `surface.configure()` cost on this machine (~600µs-1ms/call, AMD Radeon 890M via RADV/Vulkan). **Real, honestly-stated limit, not silently skipped:** the other real acceptance criterion -- "a real, human-observed check of an actual live drag-resize against a real display" -- can't be completed from here at all; this project's own already-established limit that a genuine OS-driven interactive gesture can't be scripted or run headless (M33 Phase 2's own identical verification limit for live resize) applies with extra force here, since *feeling* smooth is inherently a human, real-time, hands-on-the-mouse judgment no amount of code review or scripted measurement substitutes for. Left for the user to try directly against a real window (any example run with `app.run(max_frames=None)` instead of a bounded frame count) and report back if it still doesn't feel right -- the Wayland-specific `wp_viewporter` refinement and the never-built Windows path remain real, explicit, deferred follow-ups if so, exactly as this milestone's own scoping already stated.)

- Step 2: correct the stale "Known gaps" bullets in this file's own early section — ✅ (struck through and corrected four bullets: culling/`VirtualList` real scrollable viewport (closed M8, all 3 phases), `ItemExtent::Variable` (closed M12 Phase 1), `material-colors` verification (closed M3 step 11), and a drop-zone-highlight bullet left un-struck even though a later bullet in the same list already correctly recorded its M10 Phase 3 fix -- each cross-checked against this file's own later, authoritative sections before touching anything, not assumed from the agent's own report alone.)
- Step 3: remove the confirmed-dead `AppHandler` trait and correct every doc comment that cited it as the real current mechanism — ✅ (`crates/engine-core/src/input.rs`: removed `pub trait AppHandler` outright (re-confirmed via grep immediately before deleting: zero `impl`s, zero uses as a trait bound, anywhere in the workspace) and corrected the module's own top doc comment, which had claimed "`engine-py` is the crate that actually implements it" -- false, it never was. Corrected the two `DispatchOutcome` variant doc comments (`Activated`/`Changed`) and `Tree::dispatch`'s own doc comment (`tree.rs`) that all cited "`AppHandler`'s job" as if a real trait mediated it -- each now names the real mechanism, `engine-py::dispatch.rs`'s own `HandlerMap`/`call_handler`/`run_dispatch_outcome`. Removed the dead re-export from `lib.rs`'s own `pub use input::{...}` list and corrected its stale top doc comment too. **Real, deliberate scope boundary:** left every *historical* mention of `AppHandler` untouched (e.g. M4 Phase 1's own tracker entry, which accurately records that the trait *was* declared at that step) -- this project's own record stays honest about what was true at the time, not revised to read as if the trait never existed; only "still true today" claims citing it as real were corrected. Full verification: `cargo check`/`clippy -D warnings`/`fmt`/`cargo test --workspace --release` clean (unchanged counts across every crate -- pure doc/dead-code cleanup, no behavior change), `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, full `pytest tests/` (581 passed/1 skipped, unchanged) and all 77 examples + showcase demo clean.)

## Milestone 42 — Live `View`: Wiring the Declarative Layer into a Real Window (§4, §5, §8, §16.2, §16.4)

**Status: ✅ Complete, both phases.** User-directed: "I want Tesserae UI Framework to be a project built on top of TRE with its own git and GitHub and project" -- a genuinely new, separate framework repo, architecturally similar to the sibling `pyCopper` project but powered by TRE instead of `pyCopper`'s own direct GLFW/wgpu-py stack. Two real investigations (a dedicated Explore agent into `pyCopper`'s own real architecture; direct source reads of this repo's own `View`/`ViewModel`/`Signal`/`Reconciler`) found TRE already has real, working MVVM machinery (`view.rs`, pure-Python `Signal`/`ViewModel` in `python/tre/__init__.py`) -- closer to `pyCopper`'s own shape than expected, with genuinely fine-grained reactivity (a `Signal` write patches one bound property via `Node::animate`, confirmed not a whole-tree reconcile). But `View` has **never been wired into a live, on-screen `Window`/`App`** -- confirmed via direct read of `view.rs`'s own module doc comment and every real `View`-using example (`hot_reload.py`, `two_way_binding.py`, `view_composition.py`, `stylesheet_tokens.py`, `declarative_image.py`): all five are headless, building a `View`, reading/dispatching synthetic events, never calling `App.run()`. This milestone closes that real, load-bearing gap -- approved directly by the user via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`), not assumed.

**Real, user-directed correction mid-planning:** the original plan draft scoped only "show one `View` live." The user's own explicit feedback -- "`app.py` should be the entry point... this allows for switching of current views without needing to bootstrap each view/viewModel" -- widened this milestone's real scope to include swapping which `View` a live window shows, reusing the same window rather than closing/reopening it. Investigated directly before committing to a design: `engine_spec::Reconciler::reconcile(&mut self, tree, yaml, ...)` (`crates/engine-spec/src/reconcile.rs:76-109`) takes a raw `yaml: &str` -- it has no notion of "the same file, updated," it just diffs whatever spec is passed against whichever spec was loaded/reconciled last. But forcing one `View`'s own `Reconciler`/`ViewModel`/`Signal` bookkeeping to reconcile against a *different* view's totally unrelated YAML would leave stale bindings/subscriptions behind -- the real, clean design is a separate `View` object per named view (each with its own intact `Reconciler`/bindings/`ViewModel`), with the *live window* swapping which `View`'s own `(Tree, root)` it currently renders. Confirmed via direct read of `app.rs`'s `WindowRuntime` (`tree: Rc<RefCell<Tree>>`, `root: NodeId`, both plain fields, reassignable in principle) that this is mechanically real, not blocked by ownership/lifetime -- the real, missing piece is a way for a `PyWindow`/`Node` (Python-facing, outside the live per-frame closure) to reach in and swap them, which doesn't exist today.

### Phase 1 — Show a Single `View` Live ✅
- Step 1: give `View` the persistent shared state `WindowRuntime`'s per-frame loop assumes, and a new real entry point that shares its `(Tree, root)` into a live window — ✅ (`View` (`crates/engine-py/src/view.rs`) gained persistent `pub(crate) theme: SharedTheme`/`completions: SharedCompletions` fields, replacing the fresh-per-call throwaway `ThemeState::default()`/`CompletionRegistry::new()` instances `node()` and `_attach()`'s handler-wiring used to build (`view.rs:438-442, 554-555` before this phase). Also gained `pub(crate) width`/`height: SharedSize` (default `0`, meaning "never shown live") and a new plain-Rust helper `available_space()` (a separate, non-`#[pymethods]` `impl View` block, mirroring `PyWindow::wrap_node`'s own identical split so it isn't accidentally exposed as a Python method) — returns `AvailableSpace::MaxContent` on both axes while `width`/`height` are `0`, byte-for-byte the pre-M42 behavior, and `AvailableSpace::Definite` once a live window has set a real size. `click()`/`hover()`/`right_click()` now call `self.available_space()` instead of a hardcoded `MaxContent` literal. **Real entry point, decided at implementation time:** `PyWindow::from_view(view: &View, width=480, height=200, title="tre v2") -> PyWindow` (`crates/engine-py/src/window.rs`) — a `#[staticmethod]` on `Window`, since it constructs a new `Window` rather than mutating an existing one. Sets `view.width`/`view.height` to the given size, then clones (not copies) `view.tree`, `view.reconciler.root()`, `view.handlers`, `view.context_menus`, `view.theme`, `view.completions`, and the just-set `view.width`/`view.height` `Rc<Cell<u32>>` cells directly into the new `Window`'s own identically-shaped fields — `dock`/`materializers`/`canvas_draws`/`terminals` start fresh-empty (confirmed safe: `engine-spec`'s YAML builder has no `Terminal`/`VirtualList`/`Canvas` case, so a View-built tree can never need any of them populated). **Real, load-bearing finding that simplified this phase's original scope:** direct read of `App::run`'s own setup step (`app.rs:383-402`) confirmed it already builds `WindowSetup` generically from any `PyWindow` instance's `pub(crate)` fields, with zero assumption a `PyWindow` was ever constructed via `PyWindow::new` — meaning `PyWindow::from_view` alone is sufficient to get a `View` on screen through the existing `App.add_window()`/`App.run()` path, with **no changes needed to `app.rs`/`WindowSetup`/`WindowRuntime` at all**, narrower than this phase's own original plan-time guess. A live resize writing through the shared `Rc<Cell<u32>>` (M33 Phase 2's own `SharedSize` pattern) is immediately visible back on `view.width`/`view.height` too, so `view.click()`/`hover()` called again after showing always lay out at the window's *true current* size, not a stale value captured at `from_view` time. **Verification:** 3 new real Rust unit tests directly in `view.rs` (`available_space()`'s three real branches — never-shown/MaxContent, real-size/Definite, and the one-axis-only edge case — constructed via `View::new()` called directly with no GIL, since its body touches no `Python<'_>`/`Py<PyAny>`, the same real "plain-Rust logic gets a Rust test, pyo3-facing behavior gets a pytest" split this crate has always followed given zero prior `Python::with_gil` usage anywhere in `engine-py`'s own test surface); 5 new real pytest tests (`tests/test_view_in_window.py`) proving `window.click(node)` (not `view.click`) reaches a handler `View._attach` wired, `view.click()` still works standalone post-`from_view`, a `Signal`-driven binding write still reaches the shared live tree, and two independent `View`s get independent, non-cross-wired size cells; one new real, non-headless example (`examples/live_view.py` + `live_view.yaml`) — a real `Signal`-bound counter, five real dispatched clicks through the live-shown `View`, an asserted real text-binding repaint, then a genuine 30-frame `App.run()`. Full chain green: `cargo check`/`clippy -D warnings`/`fmt`, `cargo test --workspace --release` (+3, all else unchanged), `maturin develop --release`, `pytest tests/` (586 passed, +5, 1 skipped unchanged), all 78 examples (+1), showcase demo. **Real, honestly-scoped v1, stated directly:** live theme-switching and `on_complete` callbacks on View-sourced nodes are real, separate follow-ups, not built here; hot-reload *while a View is live* is Phase 2's own concern, not this one's.)

### Phase 2 — Swap Which `View` a Live Window Shows ✅
- Step 1: a shared, swappable `(Tree, root)` cell lets a live window's own content change without closing/reopening the window — ✅ (**Real, load-bearing correctness finding made during implementation, not covered by this phase's own original plan text (which named only `(Tree, NodeId)`):** `engine_core::NodeId` is a `slotmap` generational key, unique only *within* the `Tree` that allocated it -- two independent `View`s' own root nodes can (and do, confirmed by construction) collide on the identical raw value. `HandlerMap`/`context_menus` are keyed by `(NodeId, EventKind)`/`NodeId` alone, no per-`Tree` namespacing -- sharing one persistent map across a switch would silently cross-wire a different `View`'s old callback onto a colliding `NodeId`. The real bundle swapped atomically is therefore `tree`+`root`+`handlers`+`context_menus` together (a new `window::ActiveTree`/`SharedActiveTree = Rc<RefCell<ActiveTree>>`), not just `(Tree, NodeId)` -- `theme`/`completions` deliberately stay outside it (matching Phase 1's own already-stated "live theme-switching and on_complete callbacks on View-sourced nodes remain real, separate, stated gaps"). **Real, scope-narrowing design choice, confirmed by grep before implementing:** rather than changing `PyWindow`/`WindowSetup`/`WindowRuntime`'s own `tree`/`root`/`handlers`/`context_menus` field *types* (which would have needed rewriting ~230 pre-existing call sites across `window_factory.rs`/`window_input.rs`/etc.), those plain fields stay exactly as they are -- a new, additional `active: SharedActiveTree` field was added instead, and `WindowRuntime`'s own per-frame/per-input closures (`app.rs`) re-sync their existing plain fields from it at the top of every real invocation (4 real call sites: `frame`, `input`, `access`, `access_action`). `Window.show_view(view: &View)` writes a whole new `ActiveTree` into that cell in one `RefCell` replace, picked up on the very next real frame; also syncs the window's current size into `view.width`/`height` (a real, stated smaller limit than `from_view`'s own `Rc`-identity sharing: a later resize while a *different* View is showing won't reach this one until `show_view` runs again). **Two real bugs caught and fixed before this ever shipped, neither found by inspection alone -- both by this project's own "verify, don't assume" discipline, applied to its own new code, not just historical claims:** (1) an early draft of `Window.click`/`hover`/`scroll`/`right_click` (`window_input.rs`, updated to read through `active` so synthetic dispatch stays correct post-switch) held a live `Ref` on `self.active` across the very `run_dispatch_outcome` call that can invoke a real Python handler -- a handler calling `show_view` (the exact real "nav button switches screens" pattern) would panic on `show_view`'s own `borrow_mut()` while that `Ref` was still alive; fixed by cloning the needed `Rc`s out and dropping the borrow *before* any dispatch/callback runs (the identical pattern already correct in `app.rs`'s `frame`/`input` closures, but initially missed in one more spot, `access_action`, fixed the same way). (2) `PyWindow::__traverse__`'s new pass over `active`'s handlers, unconditional in an early draft, called `visit.call` a second time on the *same* `Py<PyAny>` object whenever `active.handlers` was still the same `Rc` as `self.handlers` (the common, never-switched case) -- confirmed via this crate's own pre-existing `test_window_participates_in_cyclic_gc_when_a_click_handler_captures_it_back` regression test, which started failing: CPython's cyclic collector counts each `visit.call` as one real outgoing edge when subtracting internal refs from an object's total refcount, so double-reporting the identical single reference made a genuine cycle look like it still had an external referent and survive collection. Fixed with an `Rc::ptr_eq` guard, skipping the second traversal entirely unless `active`'s handlers are genuinely a different map; the regression test passes again. **Verification:** 3 new real pytest tests (`tests/test_view_in_window.py`) -- a click dispatched through the window after `show_view` reaches the newly-shown View's own handler and not the old one; `show_view` called from *inside* a real dispatched click handler doesn't panic (the exact scenario bug (1) above needed to be caught); each View's own bindings stay independently reactive across a switch. New live example (`examples/live_view_switch.py` + two YAML/`ViewModel` pairs) -- two fully-bootstrapped `View`s, a real nav button in each calling `window.show_view(...)` from inside its own dispatched handler, switching A→B→A→B three times, then a genuine 20-frame `App.run()`. Full chain green: `cargo check`/`clippy -D warnings`/`fmt`, `cargo test --workspace --release` (unchanged counts -- pure additive Rust logic, no new Rust-level unit tests needed since `show_view` itself requires a live Python interpreter to call, matching this crate's own established GIL-needed/GIL-free test-surface split), `maturin develop --release`, `pytest tests/` (589 passed, +3, 1 skipped unchanged, **including confirming the real GC regression above is fixed, not just that it compiles**), all 79 examples (+2, `live_view_switch.py` and its yaml siblings aren't independently run), showcase demo.)

**Not scoped by this milestone:** richer reactivity (`Computed`, general-purpose `Effect`, `batch()`/`untrack()`) beyond `tre.Signal`'s own existing dependency-recording primitive; widening which properties are live-bindable beyond `opacity`/`corner_radius`; any of Tesserae's own repo/package work (Part 2 of the approved plan) -- this milestone is TRE-side only, the real, general capability Tesserae's own bootstrap depends on, not Tesserae-specific code.

---

## Milestone 43 — Embeddable Components: Multi-Instance Views with Independent ViewModels (§4, §5, §8, §16.2, §16.6)

**Status: ✅ Complete, both phases.** User-directed: "how do we make it so multiple views can use a single viewModel? Or no viewModel at all" led into "I want to be able to embed a view into another view and have the embedded view have a separate viewModel. The essence of MVVM and single page applications" -- confirmed via a real technical discussion (not assumed) that `include:` (§16.6) is the wrong mechanism: it splices another YAML file's widgets in as ordinary children *at parse time*, sharing the *same* single `View`/`ViewModel` as its parent, not a separate one. Two architectural options were weighed directly with the user: (1) one shared `Tree`, multiple `ViewModel`-scoped regions -- the real component pattern Vue/React actually use, needing zero `engine-core` rendering/dispatch changes; (2) genuinely separate `Tree`s composed via a cross-tree "viewport" render -- would need `engine-core`/`engine-render` to become tree-of-trees aware, crossing crate boundaries §4 deliberately keeps separate, much larger and riskier. **User confirmed option 1, with multiple simultaneous instances required** (a list where each row is its own independent component instance). Approved via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`).

**Real, scope-narrowing investigation before designing, not assumed:** `engine_spec::build_tree` (already `pub`, re-exported at `lib.rs:22`) already inserts a `WidgetSpec` into an *existing* `Tree` and returns the new subtree's root as a parentless node, ready for `Tree::add_child`; `Reconciler::load` already does parse+build+id-recording in one call; `Tree::add_child`/`Tree::remove` (the latter confirmed, by reading its own body, to already recurse a whole subtree deepest-first, cleaning up `taffy`/parent-children/overlays/focus) are exactly the primitives needed. **`engine-spec` and `engine-core` need zero changes** -- every primitive already exists and is already `pub`; all real new work is in `engine-py` (a new `Component` pyclass) plus one small addition to `tre`'s pure-Python `Signal` class (`python/tre/__init__.py`'s `Signal` has `_subscribe` but no `_unsubscribe`, confirmed by reading the file -- a real gap once components can be removed, since a `BindingCallback` left subscribed to a `Signal` after its own `NodeId` is removed would panic on the next write).

### Phase 1 — Real Component Instantiation with an Independent `ViewModel` ✅
- Step 1: new `Component` pyclass + `View.instantiate(path, into)`/`Component.instantiate(...)`, real multi-instance proof — ✅ (New `crates/engine-py/src/component.rs`: `Component { tree: Rc<RefCell<Tree>>, reconciler: Reconciler, bindings, declared_handlers, two_way, handlers: HandlerMap, context_menus, theme: SharedTheme, completions: SharedCompletions }` -- `tree`/`handlers`/`context_menus`/`theme`/`completions` are shared `Rc::clone`s with whatever it was instantiated into (the same Tree, so none of M42 Phase 2's own NodeId-collision risk applies -- that risk was specific to *separate* Trees). `View::_attach`'s existing handler-validation/binding-evaluation body (`view.rs`) was factored into a new shared `pub(crate) fn attach_bindings_and_handlers`, used by both `View::_attach` (a thin wrapper now, byte-for-byte unchanged behavior -- confirmed by the full pre-existing `_attach`-heavy pytest suite passing unmodified) and the new `Component::_attach`, matching this codebase's own established "two real call sites justify factoring out" pattern. New `instantiate_component` helper, called from both `View.instantiate(path, into: &Node) -> Component` and `Component.instantiate(...)` (so components nest recursively for free): reads the component's own YAML, `Reconciler::load`s it into the shared `Tree`, `Tree::add_child`s its root under `into`, then collects that instance's own bindings/handlers via the existing (now `pub(crate)`) `collect_bindings`/`collect_handlers`/`collect_two_way` free functions -- the identical "parse twice" pattern `View::new` itself already uses; `Reconciler`'s own `id_of`/`root` are reused directly rather than extracting a separate id map, mirroring `View`'s own struct shape almost exactly. **Real design correction, found during implementation (not in the original plan text), narrowing scope further:** `Component` has *no* `click`/`hover`/`right_click` of its own -- `Tree::compute_layout(root, available_space)` called from a component's own root would compute a fresh layout *as if* that root were the whole tree's top level, silently distorting its real, parent-constrained size. Dispatch on an embedded node is already fully correct through the *owning* `View`/`Window`'s existing `click`/`hover`/`right_click` (`view.click(component.node("button"))`), confirmed by reading `Tree::absolute_position`'s own body: it already walks a node's real parent chain all the way to whatever ancestor has no parent, so it resolves correctly for a deeply-nested component node with zero new dispatch code. **Verification:** 7 new pytest tests (`tests/test_component.py`) -- multiple instances resolve to distinct real state (proven via `Node.set_text`'s own immediate, synchronous effect, since `Node` exposes no `.id` getter to Python); each instance's own `ViewModel` is genuinely independent; a click via `view.click(component.node(...))` reaches only that instance's own handler, confirmed with 3 simultaneous instances; components nest recursively; real error cases (bad path, unknown widget id) raise clearly. New live example (`examples/component_list.py` + 2 yaml files) -- 3 real `Card` component instances in one container, each its own `Signal`-bound counter, a real dispatched click on only one instance's button leaving the other two untouched, then a genuine 20-frame `App.run()`. Full chain green: `cargo check`/`clippy -D warnings`/`fmt`, `cargo test --workspace --release` (213 unchanged -- `instantiate_component`/`Component::_attach` need a live Python interpreter to call, matching this crate's own established GIL-needed/GIL-free test-surface split, so no new Rust-level `#[test]`s were needed), `maturin develop --release`, `pytest tests/` (596 passed, +7, 1 skipped unchanged), all 80 examples (+1), showcase demo.)

### Phase 2 — Real Removal, with Automatic `Signal` Unsubscription ✅
- Step 1: `Component.remove()` tears down the subtree and unsubscribes its own bindings' Signals — ✅ (New `Signal._unsubscribe(callback)` in `python/tre/__init__.py` (a silent no-op if already absent, matching `Tree::remove`'s own "not found is a no-op" convention). `Component::_attach` tracks every `(signal, callback)` pair it subscribed; `Component.remove()` unsubscribes each, then `Tree::remove(self.reconciler.root())` -- the real fix for the panic risk the Phase 1 investigation found. **Real, honestly-stated v1 limit:** a removed component's own handler entries stay in the shared `handlers`/`context_menus` maps (harmless -- a removed `NodeId` can never be hit-tested again) rather than being swept; a real, bounded follow-up if a long-running high-churn app ever shows this mattering, not built ahead of a real need. **Real, significant bug caught and fixed before shipping, found by actually running the extended live example, not by inspection:** `View.click`/`hover`/`right_click`/`_attach` had always taken `&mut self`, even though nothing in their own bodies mutates a plain `View` field directly (every real mutation goes through an interior-mutable `Rc<RefCell<...>>`/`Cell`) -- pyo3 holds an *exclusive* borrow on the whole `View` Python object for a `&mut self` method's entire duration, so a handler dispatched from inside `click()` that calls `view.instantiate(...)` (exactly the real "Add" button a dynamic list needs, and exactly what the extended example does) panicked with "Already mutably borrowed." Fixed by widening those four methods to `&self`, mirroring `Window`'s own `click`/`hover`/`right_click` (`window_input.rs`), which already used `&self` for the identical real reason. **Verification:** 4 new pytest tests (a `Signal` write after `remove()` no longer panics; a real remove/add cycle across 5 iterations stays stable; `instantiate` called from *inside* a dispatched click handler doesn't panic -- the one test that actually exercises the reentrancy bug; a sibling's own click still works after a neighbor is removed). Extended live example (`examples/component_list.py`, both yaml files widened): a real "Add Card"/per-card "Remove" button flow -- 3 cards added via real dispatched clicks, one clicked, one removed via its own dispatched click (itself calling back into `view.instantiate`-adjacent code from inside a handler), one more added, then a genuine 20-frame `App.run()`. Full chain green: `cargo check`/`clippy -D warnings`/`fmt`, `cargo test --workspace --release` (213 unchanged), `maturin develop --release`, `pytest tests/` (600 passed, +4, 1 skipped unchanged), all 80 examples, showcase demo.)

**Explicitly deferred, named not designed:** `Window.instantiate(...)` (the identical capability for a plain imperative `Window`); sweeping stale `handlers`/`context_menus` entries on removal; a declarative YAML-level repeater (`for_each:` bound to a list `Signal`, auto-diffing instances like React's own keyed lists) -- this milestone ships the imperative instantiate/remove primitives a repeater would be built on top of, not the repeater itself; component-level hot-reload.

## Milestone 44 — Widening Live-Bindable Properties: `background` Color Bindings (§16.2)

**Status: ✅ Complete, single phase.** User-directed: after M43 closed, asked "what do you recommend we look at next?" -- two real, concrete recommendations were given: (1) widen `tre`'s live-bindable properties beyond `opacity`/`corner_radius`/`checked`/`text`; (2) richer reactivity (`Computed`, general-purpose `Effect`, `batch()`/`untrack()`) beyond `tre.Signal`'s own dependency-recording primitive. User replied "scope both 1 and 2, let's start with 1" -- scoped via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`) covering both, implementing only item 1 (this milestone); item 2 is named and deferred at the end of this section, not built.

**Real investigation before designing, not assumed:** direct read of `Node::animate()` (`crates/engine-py/src/node.rs`) confirmed it already dispatches to a wide property set imperatively -- `opacity`, `corner_radius`, `elevation`, `background` (via `extract_color`, an `(r,g,b,a)` u8 tuple only -- no hex-string parsing anywhere in `animate()`'s own path), `transform` (translate+scale tuple), `shape` (vertex list), plus kind-specific `check_progress`/`thumb_position`/`select_progress`/`rotation`/`toggle_progress`. The real gap wasn't `animate()` -- it was `apply_binding_value` (`crates/engine-py/src/view.rs`), the one function every `{{ }}` binding's resolved value passes through (called from both the initial-apply loop in `attach_bindings_and_handlers` and from `BindingCallback::__call__` on every re-evaluation): it dispatched purely on the resolved `engine_spec::Value`'s own *runtime type*, completely ignoring the declared `property` name -- `Value::Bool` unconditionally routed to `set_checked`, `Value::Str` unconditionally to `set_text`, `Value::Int`/`Value::Float` forwarded generically to `animate()` (meaning every numeric property beyond opacity/corner_radius was *already* reachable via bindings before this milestone -- a real, undocumented, untested fact this milestone's own investigation surfaced, not a new capability it added), and `Value::Handle` (any non-primitive Python value, e.g. a color tuple) rejected outright via an `other => Err(...)` arm.

### Phase 1 — Property-Name-First Dispatch + `background` Color Bindings ✅
- Step 1: rewrite `apply_binding_value`'s dispatch from value-type-first to property-name-first; add hex/CSS-named string and RGBA-tuple support for `background` — ✅ (`checked`/`text` stay the two genuinely non-`animate()` special cases (a real `bool`/`String`, not a numeric `Animated<T>` field `animate()`'s own contract can ever reach) but are now gated by `property`, not by `value`'s own type -- a binding declared on `checked` that resolves to the wrong shape is now a real, specific type-mismatch error (`"widget property \"checked\" expects a boolean binding, got ..."`) instead of silently doing nothing or misrouting. Every other property forwards to `animate()` unchanged, which already knows how to validate/extract whatever Python value shape each one needs -- no per-property knowledge duplicated in `apply_binding_value` itself. New: a `Value::Str` resolved for `property == "background"` is parsed as a color via a new `parse_background_color(raw: &str) -> Result<(u8,u8,u8,u8), String>` helper -- deliberately a plain `Result<_, String>`, not a `PyResult`, keeping it 100% free of `pyo3`/GIL concerns so it gets a real, unconditional Rust unit test (no interpreter to initialize) rather than only indirect pytest coverage, matching this crate's own established GIL-needed/GIL-free test-surface split. It calls `peniko::color::parse_color` (already a direct `engine-py` dependency, already `use`d in `node.rs`) -- the identical real parser `engine_spec::build::resolve_color` already uses for *static* YAML colors, confirmed by direct read before reusing the pattern. MD3 theme-role token strings (e.g. `"primary"`) are explicitly out of scope: `resolve_color`'s own static-color path also checks a live `ColorScheme` first, but `apply_binding_value`'s own `throwaway_node` builds a fresh, default `ThemeState`, not the view's real one -- a real, named, deliberately deferred follow-up, not silently unsupported. A `Value::Handle` (any non-primitive Python value the binding resolved to, e.g. a `Signal` holding an `(r,g,b,a)` tuple directly) is now recovered via `PyViewModelResolver::to_pyobject` -- widened from private to `pub(crate)` (`crates/engine-py/src/binding.rs`), the same "two real call sites justify widening" precedent `collect_bindings`/`collect_handlers` established for M43 -- and forwarded to `animate()` verbatim; both real call sites in `view.rs` already held a live `resolver` in scope when calling `apply_binding_value`, so threading it through needed no lifetime restructuring, just one new parameter and one new argument at each site. A real, incidental side effect of this being property-name-agnostic rather than `background`-specific: `transform`/`shape` bindings are now reachable too, not just `background`, at zero extra implementation cost -- named here, not claimed as the headline feature. **Verification:** 4 new Rust unit tests (`crates/engine-py/src/view.rs`'s own `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`, alongside the existing GIL-free `View::new`/`available_space()` coverage) proving `parse_background_color`'s exact output for a hex string, hex+alpha, a CSS-named color, and a clear error message naming the bad input on failure. 6 new pytest tests (`tests/test_view_binding.py`): a hex-string `Signal` bound to `background` applies without raising and survives re-evaluation on `.set()` (proven indirectly via a co-bound `opacity` read, since `tre` has no Python-facing getter for a node's applied `background` color at all -- `Node.get` only returns `f64`, confirmed by grep of `node.rs`/`_core.pyi` -- a real, honestly-stated limitation, not worked around); the same for an RGBA-tuple `Signal` (the `Value::Handle` path); an invalid color string raises `ValueError` naming the bad value; a `Bool`-resolved binding on `background` is rejected directly (previously would have been silently misrouted into `set_checked`, raising a `Rect`-is-not-a-Checkbox error via an entirely different, accidental path); `checked`/`text` each give a clear, specific type-mismatch error for a wrongly-shaped binding (a real message-quality improvement, not a behavior change -- both cases already errored before this milestone, just through `animate()`'s own "unknown property" or the wrong-widget-kind path from `set_text`/`set_checked`). No `python/tre/_core.pyi` changes needed -- confirmed via grep that neither `apply_binding_value`, `parse_background_color`, nor the widened `to_pyobject` are `#[pymethods]`; nothing here is Python-visible API. New live example `examples/bindable_background.py` + `.yaml`: two swatches in one window, one bound to a hex-string `Signal`, one to an RGBA-tuple `Signal`, both cycling through a real palette on a real dispatched click, then a genuine 60-frame `App.run()` -- headless-CI-safe, matching `animate_rect.py`'s own established convention. Full chain green: `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`/`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`/`cargo fmt`+`cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo test --workspace --release` (`engine-py` 15, up from 11, +4; every other crate unchanged); `maturin develop --release`; `pytest tests/` (606 passed, up from 600, +6, 1 skipped unchanged); all 81 examples (+1, zero failures); `demo/showcase.py` (all 5 phases, exit 0).)

**Explicitly out of scope for this milestone, named not silent:** MD3 theme-role token strings for a bound `background` (needs `apply_binding_value`'s `throwaway_node` to carry the view's real live `ColorScheme`, not a fresh default `ThemeState` -- a real, separate, smaller follow-up); two-way (`two_way:`) write-back for `background` or any newly-reached composite property (`TwoWayCallback::__call__` reads via `Node::get` today, which has no composite-property read path, and no real use case has been named for one yet, unlike `checked`/`thumb_position`/`text`); widening the widget/component catalog itself (a separate item already named in Tesserae's own `README.md` "Explicitly deferred" section, untouched here).

**Item 2, scoped but not implemented — richer reactivity (`Computed`, `Effect`, `batch()`/`untrack()`):** real gaps confirmed by direct read of `Signal` (`python/tre/__init__.py`, pure Python, eager/synchronous -- `.set()`/`.update()` call every subscriber immediately, no batching) and its Rust-side dependency-recording hook (`RECORDING` thread-local, `view.rs`): no derived/memoized values (a `Computed(fn)` would run `fn` once inside a recording scope -- reusing the *existing* `_record_read` mechanism `_attach` already uses, unchanged -- subscribe to each dependency, cache the result, and only re-run+renotify when a dependency actually changes, mirroring `Signal.set`'s own "skip notify if unchanged" rule); no general-purpose side-effect primitive (`Effect(fn)` -- the same recording-and-subscribe mechanism, minus the caching); no batching (`Signal._notify` calls every subscriber synchronously on every `.set()`/`.update()` today -- a real `batch(fn)` needs a thread-local "currently batching" flag `_notify` checks, deferring notification into a deduped pending set flushed once `fn` returns; whether a nested `batch()` flushes only at the outermost exit, and whether a `Computed`'s own cache participates in the same dedup or can stay eager, are real, unresolved design questions for whenever this is picked up); no `untrack()` (no way to read a `Signal` inside a recording scope without being captured as a dependency -- `_record_read`'s existing "no-op outside a recording scope" check already almost supports this, `untrack` would need to temporarily fake "outside" while genuinely still inside one). **Real, load-bearing design lean, not a final decision:** given `Signal` itself is already 100% pure Python and the existing `_record_read` hook already generalizes beyond `_attach`'s own use of it, the strong precedent points to `Computed`/`Effect` being pure-Python too (a `tesserae`- or `tre`-level addition reusing `_record_read` unchanged), not a new pyo3 surface -- to be re-confirmed against whatever real use case motivates starting it, likely via its own Plan Mode session (the same "weigh the real options with the user before building" discipline the Repeater and M43 both already used), not assumed from this scoping pass alone. No `BUILD_TRACKER.md` milestone number is claimed for item 2 by this entry.
