## Milestone 45 — Richer Reactivity: `Computed`, `Effect`, `batch()`, `untrack()` (§16.2)

**Status: ✅ Complete, single phase.** User-directed: M44's own scoping named item 2 ("richer reactivity") and deliberately deferred it, ending with "no `BUILD_TRACKER.md` milestone number is claimed for item 2 by this entry." The user later said simply "Start it." Scoped via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`) before implementing, per this session's own established discipline for genuinely novel-capability work.

**Real investigation before designing, not assumed -- and a real correction to M44's own scoping text:** M44's item-2 note assumed `_record_read`'s underlying `begin_recording`/`end_recording` machinery was reachable for pure-Python reuse. Direct read of `view.rs` found they were plain, private `fn`s -- never `#[pyfunction]`s, called only internally by `attach_bindings_and_handlers`. `Computed`/`Effect`/`untrack` need to open/close a tracked evaluation scope the identical way a YAML binding's own evaluation already does, so this milestone's real first step is exposing that pair to Python, not merely writing pure-Python classes on top of an already-exposed hook.

**A second, more consequential real finding, from tracing an actual reachable scenario (not a hypothetical) before writing any Python code:** `RECORDING` (`view.rs`) was `RefCell<Option<Vec<Py<PyAny>>>>` -- a single flat slot, not a stack. The binding grammar (`engine_spec::binding::Expression::Call`) already permits a zero-arg method call with real side effects (confirmed via its own doc comment: "attribute access, indexing, comparison, arithmetic, boolean logic, zero-arg method calls"). Trace: a binding evaluates inside `begin_recording()`/`end_recording()`; if evaluating it calls a method that does `some_signal.set(...)`, that fires `_notify()` *synchronously* while the outer recording is still open; if any subscriber of `some_signal` is a `Computed`/`Effect` that eagerly re-tracks itself (opening its *own* nested recording scope), the inner `begin_recording()` call **overwrote the flat `Option` slot**, and the inner `end_recording()` **cleared it to `None`** -- the outer binding's own `end_recording()` then returned an empty list, silently and permanently losing every dependency it had read, with no error at all. Unreachable before this milestone (nothing previously opened a nested recording scope); `Computed`/`Effect`'s own eager re-tracking is exactly the mechanism that would first make it reachable, so this was fixed as a real prerequisite, not deferred as a hypothetical hardening.

### Phase 1 — `Computed`, `Effect`, `batch()`, `untrack()`, on a fixed `RECORDING` stack ✅
- Step 1: turn `RECORDING` into a real stack; expose `_begin_recording`/`_end_recording` to Python — ✅ (`crates/engine-py/src/view.rs`: `RECORDING: RefCell<Vec<Vec<Py<PyAny>>>>`, replacing the flat `Option`. `_record_read` now touches only `.last_mut()` -- a nested `begin`/`end` pair genuinely nests (LIFO) without disturbing an outer frame. `begin_recording`/`end_recording` widened to `#[pyfunction] pub(crate) fn _begin_recording()`/`_end_recording() -> Vec<Py<PyAny>>`, registered in `lib.rs` alongside the existing `_record_read` -- confirmed directly (by compiling, not assuming) that a `#[pyfunction]`-annotated fn stays a normal, directly-callable Rust item under its own name, so `attach_bindings_and_handlers`'s two existing internal call sites needed only a name change (`begin_recording()` -> `_begin_recording()`), no restructuring. **A real design simplification found during this same investigation:** `untrack(fn)` needs no third primitive at all -- calling `_begin_recording()`/`_end_recording()` around `fn()` and discarding the returned list already hides its own reads from whatever frame is beneath it on the stack (`_record_read` only ever touches the top), exactly `untrack`'s contract, for free. `python/tre/_core.pyi` gained matching stubs next to the existing `_record_read` one.)
- Step 2: `Computed`/`Effect`/`batch`/`untrack` (`python/tre/__init__.py`) — ✅ (`Computed(fn)` runs `fn` once inside `_begin_recording`/`_end_recording`, subscribes to every dependency discovered, caches the result, and only re-runs+renotifies when a dependency actually changes (mirroring `Signal.set`'s own skip-if-unchanged rule) -- dependencies are unconditionally unsubscribed-then-resubscribed on every recompute rather than diffed, a deliberate simplicity choice (the real lists this is for are small). `Effect(fn)` is the identical mechanism minus the cache/downstream-subscribers, for side effects only; `dispose()` unsubscribes from every tracked dependency (named to mirror SolidJS/MobX's own established term, not `Component.remove()` -- that name is specific to tearing down a live `Tree` subtree, which an `Effect` never has). Both duck-type against `Signal`'s own `_subscribe`/`_unsubscribe`/`.get()` shape -- confirmed by reading the real subscribe call site (`view.rs`: `signal.call_method1("_subscribe", ...)`, with **no type check at all**) before relying on it, so a `{{ }}` binding can point straight at a `Computed.get()` value with zero Rust changes; proven end to end, not just asserted. `batch(fn)` uses a plain module-level depth counter (nested `batch()` calls flush only at the outermost exit -- confirmed correct without a real stack, since nothing needs per-level data) and a module-global pending list, not `threading.local()` (this engine's whole render/dispatch loop is single-threaded, confirmed by reading `app.rs`'s real per-frame closures before choosing the simpler mechanism). `Signal.set`/`.update`'s final `self._notify()` call became `_schedule_notify(self)`; since nothing calls `batch()` in any pre-M45 code path, `_batch_depth` stays `0` everywhere else, making this a true no-op refactor of all existing behavior -- confirmed by the full pre-existing suite passing unmodified. A `Computed`'s own downstream notification routes through the identical `_schedule_notify`, so it participates in batching automatically, with **zero `Computed`-specific batching code anywhere**: its recompute is just a normal subscriber callback, and deferring its dependencies' own notifications already defers it too.)

**Three further real bugs, all caught by actually running the code -- writing the pytest suite and the live example, not by inspection -- on top of the two anticipated during scoping:**
1. **`batch()`'s dedup granularity was wrong on the first working version.** It deduplicated by *Signal*, then called each pending Signal's own `._notify()` -- which still re-invoked a callback subscribed to *multiple* pending Signals once per Signal it happened to depend on (a `Computed` depending on two Signals both written inside one `batch()` recomputed twice, not once, directly contradicting this milestone's own stated goal). Root-caused and fixed by deduplicating at the *callback* level instead: confirmed directly (not assumed) that Python bound methods compare and hash by `(__self__, __func__)` identity even though each attribute access creates a structurally-distinct wrapper object (`obj.method == obj.method` is `True`, `obj.method is obj.method` is `False`, but both go in the same `set()` slot) -- so a plain `set()` of callbacks, built while walking each pending Signal's subscribers, dedupes correctly across different Signals' own subscriber lists with no extra bookkeeping.
2. **`Signal._notify`/`Computed._notify` iterated `self._subscribers` live**, not a snapshot -- both had done this since before M45 existed, but nothing previously mutated a `_subscribers` list *from inside* one of its own callbacks. A `Computed`-of-`Computed` chain does exactly that: the first subscriber `_notify()` invokes can itself unsubscribe-then-resubscribe from that very list as part of its own `_recompute()`, and Python's `for` loop over a list uses a live index position, not a snapshot -- the removal-then-append shifted every later element down one slot, silently *skipping* the next real subscriber for that notify pass and *re-invoking the mutating one a second time* instead. Caught by `examples/reactivity.py`'s own `Effect` log ending up `[10, 60, 60]` instead of `[10, 30, 60, 100]` -- a concrete, wrong, observable result, not a crash, which is exactly why running real code mattered here over inspection alone. Fixed by snapshotting (`list(self._subscribers)`) before iterating, in both `_notify` implementations and `batch()`'s own flush loop (the third live-iteration site, same root cause).
3. **A related, real hazard, found and deliberately routed around while writing the `RECORDING`-stack regression test itself, not fixed (a separate, genuinely pathological pattern, out of this milestone's own scope):** a `Signal.get()` override that reads a *different* Signal's current value via `.get()` before writing to it, while an outer recording scope is open, gets that different Signal misattributed as a dependency of the outer scope too -- recording is ambient, it has no notion of "how deep the call stack is" or "which read logically belongs to which caller." Combined with the outer binding's own evaluation also *writing* to that same Signal, this produces a binding that ends up subscribed to a Signal its own evaluation writes to -- a self-referential hazard that exists independent of `Computed`/`Effect` (a plain ViewModel method with the same shape would trigger it identically) and isn't something a "fix the recording mechanism" change can close without a much larger redesign (e.g. every plain `Signal.get()` opening its own isolated frame, which nothing in this codebase's existing binding semantics calls for). The regression test's own trigger deliberately avoids it (sets a value it already has in hand, never reading the target Signal first) to stay isolated to the one real bug this milestone's Phase 1 fixes -- named here for the record, not silently worked around with no explanation.

**Verification:** Rust-level coverage for the `RECORDING` stack itself stays pytest-only (it's inherently pyo3/GIL-bound -- `Py<PyAny>` needs a live interpreter -- matching this crate's own established GIL-needed/GIL-free test-surface split; no new `#[test]`s were added or needed). 17 new pytest tests (`tests/test_reactivity.py`): `Computed` initial derivation, recomputation on a real dependency change, skip-notify-if-unchanged, `Computed`-of-`Computed` chaining, a Signal never read never triggering a recompute, and a `{{ }}` binding pointed directly at a `Computed` (the real, concrete duck-typing composition proof); `Effect` running on construction, rerunning exactly once per real dependency change, `dispose()` stopping further reruns, and only tracking whatever a branch-dependent body actually read on its *last* run; `batch()` collapsing multiple writes into one notification pass, a shared `Computed` recomputing exactly once (the real bug-1 regression proof), nested `batch()` only flushing at the outermost exit, and pending notifications still flushing (via `try`/`finally`) when the wrapped function raises; `untrack()` hiding a read from the enclosing `Effect`'s own dependency list and returning the wrapped function's result; and the nested-recording-stack regression test itself (real-bug-2's own discovery scenario, isolated from real-hazard-3 above by construction). New live example `examples/reactivity.py` + `.yaml`: a `Computed`-of-`Computed` price/quantity/total/label chain, a `{{ }}` binding pointed straight at the outer `Computed`, a real `Effect` logging every genuine total change, and `batch()` collapsing two related writes per click into exactly one recompute -- through a genuine 30-frame `App.run()`, headless-CI-safe, matching `live_view.py`'s established convention. Full chain green: `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`/`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`/`cargo fmt`+`cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo test --workspace --release` (`engine-py` 15, unchanged -- no new Rust-level tests needed, per the note above); `maturin develop --release`; `pytest tests/` (623 passed, up from 606, +17, 1 skipped unchanged); all 82 examples (+1, zero failures); `demo/showcase.py` (all 5 phases, exit 0).

**Explicitly out of scope for this milestone, named not silent:** the self-referential Signal-read-during-a-different-Signal's-write hazard named above (real, separate, deliberately routed around rather than fixed -- a much larger redesign would be needed to close it generally, and nothing in this codebase's existing binding semantics has ever called for it); diffing `Computed`/`Effect`'s own dependency re-subscription on recompute instead of unsubscribing-then-resubscribing every dependency unconditionally (a real, deliberate simplicity choice for now -- the real dependency lists this is for are small); a `tesserae`-level re-export of `Computed`/`Effect`/`batch`/`untrack` (a small, separate follow-up in that repo, matching how `Signal`/`ViewModel`/`View`/`Component` are already re-exported there, committed under `tesserae`'s own "push after each complete unit of work, verified in CI" convention).

## Milestone 46 — Reentrant-Notification Guard for the Read-Before-Write Hazard (§16.2)

**Status: ✅ Complete, single phase.** User-directed: M45's own explicitly-out-of-scope note named a real hazard and deliberately left it unfixed. The user asked directly: "Scope and fix the Signal read-before-write on a different Signal during an open recording scope hazards." Scoped via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`) before implementing, per this session's own established discipline.

**Real investigation before designing:** re-traced the exact original failure (`sys.setrecursionlimit` + instrumented stack prints, kept from M45's own debugging session) and confirmed the dangerous *consequence* -- unbounded reentrant recursion -- has a narrow, well-defined shape regardless of what caused it: a single Signal/Computed's own `_notify()` calling itself several frames down, never a genuinely unbounded chain of distinct objects. Concretely, in the original repro: `b._notify()` -> a subscriber over-broadly attached to `b` evaluates a binding that writes `b` again -> `b._notify()` is entered a second time while the first call is still on the stack.

**Real, deliberate scope decision, not silently assumed:** closing the *cause* -- so an incidental read of a different Signal, buried arbitrarily deep in a call stack, never gets misattributed as a dependency of an unrelated outer recording scope in the first place -- would need every plain `Signal.get()` call to open its own isolated recording frame, an invasive change to the hot path of *every* Signal read, to control for a call-stack-depth distinction nothing else in this engine's binding semantics has ever needed. `untrack()` (M45) already exists as the exact, targeted escape hatch for the read that shouldn't count as a dependency. This milestone fixes the *consequence* instead: a per-object reentrancy guard that converts the crash into one clear, immediate, actionable error the moment the pattern occurs -- for any cause, not just the one scenario already found.

**A real gap found while designing the fix, checked directly rather than assumed:** `batch()`'s own flush loop (`python/tre/__init__.py`, M45) does not call `signal._notify()` at all -- a deliberate M45 fix for cross-signal callback dedup means it iterates subscriber lists and invokes callbacks directly instead. A guard placed only inside the ordinary immediate-notify path (`_schedule_notify` -> `signal_like._notify()`) would therefore never trigger during a batch flush, leaving the identical hazard silently reachable there.

### Phase 1 — `_Notifiable` base + reentrancy guard, shared by both notification paths ✅
- Step 1: factor `Signal`/`Computed`'s already-duplicated subscriber machinery into a shared base; add the guard there — ✅ (`Signal` and `Computed` (`python/tre/__init__.py`) already had byte-for-byte identical `_subscribe`/`_unsubscribe` bodies and near-identical `_notify` bodies, both independently fixed for the same live-iteration-during-mutation bug in M45 -- real duplication that made "add the same new guard to both separately" the wrong move. New `_Notifiable` base class carries `_subscribers`, the new `_notifying` boolean flag, `_subscribe`/`_unsubscribe` (moved verbatim), and a widened `_notify(already_invoked=None)`: if `self._notifying` is already `True` (this exact object's own notify is already running further up the call stack), it raises immediately with a message naming the object, explaining what happened, and pointing at `tre.untrack(...)` around the *offending read* (not the write -- the error's first draft suggested wrapping the write, which is wrong: `untrack()` only affects dependency *recording*, not notification, so it's specifically the read that caused the over-broad subscription that needs wrapping, confirmed by actually verifying the suggested fix works before shipping the message that recommends it). Otherwise it sets the flag, walks a *snapshot* of `self._subscribers` (the M45 fix, now defined in one place), deduplicates against an optional shared `already_invoked` set, and always clears the flag in a `finally`. `Signal(_Notifiable)`/`Computed(_Notifiable)` call `super().__init__()` and drop their own now-redundant copies. `batch()`'s own flush loop drops its duplicated snapshot/dedup loop and instead calls `signal_like._notify(invoked_callbacks)` per pending signal, threading its shared dedup set straight into the same guarded method -- closing the batch-path gap named above with the identical code path the immediate path already uses, not a second copy of the fix. **Real design question resolved, not assumed:** does `Computed`/`Effect`'s own re-tracking (`_recompute`/`_run`) need a *second*, matching guard? No -- both already unsubscribe from their old dependencies *before* running their own `fn`/`self._fn` and only resubscribe *after* it returns, confirmed by re-reading both bodies; a write inside `fn` to one of its own about-to-be-re-established dependencies can't directly re-invoke that same `_recompute`/`_run` through a dependency's own `_notify()`, because it isn't currently subscribed to anything at that moment. The one guard on `_notify` was the whole real gap, not the first of two. `__all__` also gained `Computed`/`Effect`/`batch`/`untrack`, missing since M45 shipped them -- a real, small, honest correction made while already touching this file, not scope creep (`from tre import *` previously wouldn't have exported any of the four, even though direct `from tre import Computed` always worked).)

**Verification:** 6 new pytest tests (`tests/test_reactivity.py`): `test_reentrant_notify_raises_a_clear_error_instead_of_recursing` (the minimal, isolated proof -- a bare Signal whose own subscriber writes back to it, no View/binding machinery involved); `test_reentrant_notify_guard_resets_after_a_caught_exception` (the `_notifying` flag must clear via `finally`, not stay permanently tripped -- a later, unrelated write on the same Signal must still work); `test_a_legitimate_non_cyclic_chain_does_not_trip_the_guard` (a real Signal -> Computed -> Computed -> Effect chain with no path back notifies cleanly -- the guard is for an object re-entering *its own* notify, not for depth in general); `test_batch_flush_also_hits_the_reentrant_notify_guard` (the real proof the batch-path gap is closed, not just the immediate path); `test_the_original_read_before_write_binding_hazard_now_raises_clearly` (the exact, real, originally-observed YAML-binding scenario now raises the same clear error instead of recursing); `test_untrack_is_the_real_documented_fix_for_the_read_before_write_hazard` (the error message's own suggested remedy, verified end to end -- wrapping the offending read, not the write, in `untrack()` genuinely resolves the hazard with no error and correct behavior, not just asserted to work). Full pre-existing `test_reactivity.py` suite (all 17 M45 tests) re-run and confirmed passing unmodified -- the `_Notifiable` refactor is a true behavior-preserving no-op. No Rust changes at all this milestone -- entirely within `python/tre/__init__.py`, so no `cargo`/`maturin` steps were needed; `pytest tests/` (629 passed, up from 623, +6, 1 skipped unchanged), all 82 examples (zero failures, unchanged count -- no new example needed, the existing `test_reactivity.py` suite is the real proof surface here), and the showcase demo all re-run clean as the full regression check.

**Explicitly out of scope for this milestone, named not silent:** eliminating the over-broad dependency attribution itself (the actual *cause*, not just its dangerous consequence) -- named directly above as needing an invasive per-read isolation change this codebase's existing semantics have never called for; a `Computed`/`Effect`-level guard on `_recompute`/`_run` (confirmed unnecessary -- the existing unsubscribe-before-run ordering already prevents that specific self-reentry); a `tesserae`-level change (this milestone touches only `tre`'s own `python/tre/__init__.py`, and `Computed`/`Effect`/`batch`/`untrack` are already re-exported there since M45's own follow-up commit -- nothing new to propagate).

## Milestone 47 — `VirtualList` Scrollbar Thumb (§5, §7, §11.7)

**Status: ✅ Complete — single phase.** User-directed: a full "Known gaps" audit (prompted by "M30, 35, and 36... identify any gaps that need to be looked at") found one real, still-open gap -- `VirtualList` never had a real scrollbar/visual affordance (named in M37's own trailer note, confirmed via direct source read that M38 Phase 6's own scrollbar-thumb paint code was gated to `ScrollView` only, never `VirtualList`/`Carousel`) -- and the user asked to scope and build it.

**Real investigation before designing, not assumed:** read `ScrollView`'s own identical M38 Phase 6 capability directly as the concrete precedent to replicate, not reinvented from scratch -- `ScrollViewState::thumb_geometry(viewport_extent, content_extent) -> (track, thumb, along)`, `Tree::grabs_scroll_view_thumb`/`update_scroll_view_thumb_drag` (grab tolerance via `SCROLLBAR_GRAB_SLOP`, a relative-delta drag anchor, a direct never-eased scroll write), `engine-render::paint_scroll_view_thumb` (paints only when scrollable, after every real child, using the shared `SCROLLBAR_THICKNESS`/`SCROLLBAR_MARGIN`/`SCROLLBAR_MIN_LENGTH`/`SCROLLBAR_THUMB_RADIUS`/`SCROLLBAR_THUMB_OPACITY` tokens already `pub` in `engine-core`, no new tokens needed). **Real, load-bearing difference from a copy-paste, found before writing code:** `VirtualList` has no single measured child to read a "content extent" from (windowed materialization, `Fixed` or `Variable`) -- reuses its own already-real `VirtualListState::total_extent()` (the same primitive `Tree::scroll_virtual_list_by`'s own clamping already uses, so wheel-scroll and thumb-drag can never disagree about it) instead of a caller-supplied parameter; and `VirtualList` is vertical-only today (`scroll_virtual_list_by`'s own signature has no horizontal variant), so the new thumb code is a real, narrower single-axis version, not a second horizontal-capable copy.

### Phase 1 — Real Thumb Geometry, Paint, Grab, and Drag ✅
- Step 1: `VirtualListState::thumb_geometry`/`thumb_drag_anchor`, `Tree::grabs_virtual_list_thumb`/`update_virtual_list_thumb_drag`, `engine-render::paint_virtual_list_thumb` — ✅ (`crates/engine-core/src/node.rs`: `VirtualListState` gained `thumb_drag_anchor: Option<(f64, f64)>` (byte-for-byte the same real anchor shape `ScrollViewState`'s own M38 Phase 6 field already established) and `thumb_geometry(viewport_extent) -> (track, thumb, along)` -- identical math to `ScrollViewState`'s own, reading `self.total_extent()` for content extent and `self.scroll_offset.current` instead of a measured child/`self.scroll`, vertical-only (no `horizontal` parameter at all, unlike `ScrollView`'s). `crates/engine-core/src/tree.rs`: new `virtual_list_viewport_extent`/`grabs_virtual_list_thumb`/`update_virtual_list_thumb_drag`, directly mirroring `scroll_view_extents`/`grabs_scroll_view_thumb`/`update_scroll_view_thumb_drag`'s own real bodies, wired into the identical real dispatch chokepoints: `PointerPressed`'s own ancestor-walk (grab takes priority over the ordinary hit, since the thumb paints *over* real content) gained a second `NodeKind::VirtualList` check right after the existing `ScrollView` one, and `update_drag`'s own `NodeKind` match gained a `VirtualList` arm. `crates/engine-render/src/lib.rs`: `paint_node`'s existing `if let NodeKind::ScrollView(state) = ... { paint_scroll_view_thumb(...) }` gained a sibling `NodeKind::VirtualList` arm calling new `paint_virtual_list_thumb`, sharing the actual geometry-to-pixels fill math with `paint_scroll_view_thumb` via a new small `fill_scrollbar_thumb` helper (factored out once a second real caller needed the identical `RoundedRect` fill -- the same "two real call sites justify factoring out" precedent this codebase already uses throughout) rather than duplicating it. No new Python-facing API -- exactly like `ScrollView`'s own M38 Phase 6, this is pure paint + real dispatch-level interaction, already reachable through the real winit pointer path and `Window.click`. **Verification:** 6 new `engine-core` unit tests (`crates/engine-core/src/tree.rs`) -- exact thumb geometry math (mirroring `ScrollView`'s own equivalent test's real numbers: 200x100 viewport, 400px content -> track 96, thumb 32), `along` tracking real scroll progress, grab tolerance (`SCROLLBAR_GRAB_SLOP`) at a direct hit and a just-within-slop miss, no grab when there's nothing to scroll, real thumb-drag moving `scroll_offset` proportionally for both `Fixed` and `Variable` extent (**a real test-fixture bug caught by actually running the tests, not by inspection:** the first `Variable`-extent drag test used the identical tiny 30px viewport `scroll_virtual_list_by`'s own clamp test already uses, which leaves zero real thumb travel once `SCROLLBAR_MIN_LENGTH` fills the whole track -- a real, correct no-op, not a bug, but invalid for testing *drag specifically*; fixed by using a larger, still genuinely non-uniform fixture with real drag travel). 2 new `engine-render` pixel-level tests (`crates/engine-render/tests/virtual_list_scroll.rs`, mirroring `scroll_view.rs`'s own identical M38 Phase 6 tests): a real point inside the thumb's own computed rect is genuinely painted; a list with nothing to scroll paints no thumb at all (**a second real test-fixture bug caught by running it:** the shared `materialize` fixture paints items spanning the full viewport width, so the "no thumb" check point coincided with real opaque item content rather than background -- fixed with a dedicated narrower-item fixture for just this test, mirroring `scroll_view.rs`'s own identical "use a transparent `Container`, not the shared opaque marker" fixture split for its own "no thumb" test). `examples/scrollable_list.py`'s own doc comment updated to name the new thumb (paints automatically, no script change needed, since its real 1,000-row list already exceeds its own viewport) and to state directly, matching `docking.py`/`resizable_panes.py`'s own established honesty about the identical real limitation: this engine has no synthetic Python-level "press at an arbitrary point, then move" primitive for *any* drag gesture (confirmed via grep before claiming this), so the real thumb-drag itself is proven at the Rust level and left for a human to try interactively, not faked in a script. Full chain green: `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`/`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo test --workspace --release` (`engine-core` 219, up from 213, +6; `engine-render`'s own `virtual_list_scroll` suite 4, up from 2, +2); `maturin develop --release`; `pytest tests/` (629 passed, unchanged -- no Python-facing API added); all 82 examples (zero failures); `demo/showcase.py` (all 5 phases, exit 0).)

**M47 -- `VirtualList` Scrollbar Thumb -- is now fully complete, single phase.** Closes the one real, still-open gap the last "Known gaps" audit found -- see this file's own top-of-file "Fixed gaps" section for where it was moved.

**Explicitly out of scope for this milestone, named not silent:** an actual mouse drag on the real thumb, proven end to end through a Python-level synthetic script -- this engine has no synthetic "press at an arbitrary point, then move" primitive for *any* drag gesture (Splitter, Slider, Carousel, `ScrollView`'s own thumb, and now this one all share the identical real limitation), so the real grab/drag math stays proven at the Rust level, left for a human to try interactively via `examples/scrollable_list.py`; a real scrollbar for `Carousel` (never had one, unrelated -- `Carousel` has its own real drag-to-scroll gesture already, no separate thumb affordance has ever been named as a real gap for it).

## Milestone 48 — General Live Property Exposure: Layout Mutation + Border (§5, §7, §11)

**Status: ✅ Complete — single phase.** User-directed: "I want all properties usable and exposed for a users use. Customization is a key function of any GUI framework. Even changing the MD3 theme should be customizable by a user as the MD3 specification is just the default setting." Preceded by a direct factual question ("Do we have bindable properties for all renderable nodes for yaml and python? Like width, height, border thickness, colors, states like checked, etc…?"), answered with a real, cited audit before any scoping began.

**Real investigation before designing, not assumed:** the audit found `width`/`height`/`padding`/`gap` fixed at construction with no live-mutation path anywhere for Python or YAML -- `Tree::set_layout_style` (`crates/engine-core/src/tree.rs`) is genuinely general (any `NodeId`, arbitrary `taffy::Style`) but had exactly one narrow, real Python-facing caller: `Window.resize_terminal` (`window_factory.rs`), which clones a node's existing `layout_style`, patches only `size`, and calls `set_layout_style` -- the real pattern to generalize, not invent from scratch. `border_color`/`border_width` are real `PaintProperties` fields (M30 Phase 1) but were unreachable from `Node.animate`/`get` (confirmed via direct read of `animate`'s own exhaustive match), unreachable from `{{ }}` YAML bindings, absent from `StyleSpec` (`engine-spec/src/spec.rs`), and not exposed as a constructor kwarg on any widget factory. **Scoping finding that reshaped the follow-on roadmap, not this milestone's own scope:** mid-investigation, a direct follow-up question ("I would like the MD3 theme settings to be a yaml style file loaded as the default...") revealed that roughly half of the user's described cascade already exists -- `crates/engine-spec/src/cascade.rs`'s `Stylesheet`/`StyleRule`/`resolve_style`, a genuine per-field cascade (`baseline < kind < classes < id < inline`), YAML-loadable, fully wired through both initial build and hot-reload, with two real end-to-end usages (`examples/stylesheet_tokens.py`, `demo/showcase.py`). What's missing -- a YAML-authored default/custom theme layered beneath that existing cascade -- was scoped as M49/M50, deliberately deferred; this milestone (M48) stayed the smaller, concrete, immediately-buildable piece both approaches share as a prerequisite (M49's per-kind default styles will want to set `border_width`/`border_color`, unreachable from `StyleSpec` until this milestone).

### Phase 1 — `Node.set_layout`, Border as a First-Class Property ✅
- Step 1: `Node.set_layout(width, height, padding, gap)` — ✅ (`crates/engine-py/src/node.rs`: a new method generalizing `resize_terminal`'s exact real pattern -- clones the node's current `layout_style`, patches only the fields the caller actually passed (`None` by default, so a partial call leaves every other field untouched), pushes the result through `Tree::set_layout_style`. Immediate, not eased -- layout fields aren't `Animated<T>`, so this is a parallel dispatch next to `animate()`, not an extra match arm inside it. `width`/`height`/`padding`/`gap` match `StyleSpec`'s own real scope today (`padding` uniform, matching every side -- per-side padding/margin remains a real, separate, un-added gap, named not silently expanded into).)
- Step 2: `border_color`/`border_width` reachable from `animate()`/`get()` — ✅ (`crates/engine-py/src/node.rs`: two new `animate()` arms mirroring `background`/`corner_radius` exactly -- no new `engine-core` work needed, the fields already exist and are already `Animated`; `border_width` added to `get()`'s own numeric dispatch the same way `corner_radius`/`elevation` already are, `border_color` excluded for the same real reason `background` already is, not a single `f64`.)
- Step 3: `StyleSpec`/`cascade.rs` widened for border — ✅ (`crates/engine-spec/src/spec.rs`: `StyleSpec` gained `border_width: Option<f32>`/`border_color: Option<String>`. `crates/engine-spec/src/build.rs`: `node_kind_and_paint` split into itself (border resolution, applied once after the match -- the same reason `corner_radius`/`opacity` are computed once outside the match rather than duplicated per arm) plus a new `node_kind_and_base_paint` carrying every pre-existing match arm byte-for-byte unchanged, so the real per-`NodeKindSpec` construction logic (Rect/Text/Container/Checkbox/Slider/TextField/Image) needed zero edits. `crates/engine-spec/src/cascade.rs`: `merge()` gained the matching two field-overlay arms, so border participates in the existing real stylesheet cascade (`baseline < kind < classes < id < inline`), not just static leaf values.)
- Step 4: YAML `{{ }}` bindings reach border + the new layout fields — ✅ (`crates/engine-py/src/view.rs`: `apply_binding_value`'s existing `Value::Str`-as-color guard (previously `property == "background"` only) widened to also match `"border_color"`, reusing the identical real hex/CSS-named parser. A new pre-`animate()` branch handles `width`/`height`/`padding`/`gap` directly -- these can never reach `animate()`'s own dispatch at all, since they aren't `Animated<T>` fields, so they're routed to the new `Node.set_layout` instead, the same "special-cased before the generic forwarding" shape `checked`/`text` already establish for their own non-numeric reasons.)
- Step 5: `Window.add_rect` gains construction-time border kwargs — ✅ (`crates/engine-py/src/window_factory.rs`: `add_rect` gained optional `border_color`/`border_width` trailing kwargs, applied to its `PaintProperties` via `Animated::new` before insertion. Confirmed via grep before choosing this factory: no `add_container` exists anywhere in the imperative API -- `add_rect` is the one genuinely generic "colored box" factory, so it's the representative case for this milestone; extending border kwargs to every other widget factory is a natural, separate follow-up, named not silently promised.)

**Verification:** 19 new pytest tests (`tests/test_live_style.py`) — `set_layout` (each field individually, combined, called repeatedly), border via `animate()`/`get()`/construction/`StyleSpec`/bindings, and the non-numeric-binding/invalid-border-color error paths. **Two real test-authoring mistakes caught by actually running the tests, not shipped:** an initial test asserted `node.get("border_width")` immediately after `node.animate(..., duration_ms=0)` on a plain `Window`-created node -- `animate()`'s own documented contract ("only registers, snaps on the next tick") never guarantees this without a running render loop draining it, the identical real reason every sibling `animate()` test in `test_engine_py.py` already asserts only "must not raise," not a readback; fixed to match that convention, with the real readback assertion moved to the `View`-binding-path test instead (which ticks eagerly, per `apply_binding_value`'s own established M44 fix). A second test expected the layout-binding type-mismatch error to be `TypeError`, but the real code (matching its own `checked`/`text` sibling branches) raises `ValueError` -- fixed to match. New live example `examples/live_style.py` + `.yaml`: a real bound border (`border_width`/`border_color`, two `Signal`s) cycling through three states via a dispatched click, combined with a real, live `Node.set_layout` resize on the same node, through a genuine 60-frame `App.run()`. `python/tre/_core.pyi` updated (`Node.set_layout` stub, `add_rect`'s widened signature). Full chain green: `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`/`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo test --workspace --release` (every pre-existing suite unchanged -- zero new Rust-level `#[test]`s needed, since this milestone added no new `engine-core` logic: `Tree::set_layout_style` already has its own direct Rust coverage from M33); `maturin develop --release`; `pytest tests/` (648 passed, up from 629, +19, 1 skipped unchanged); all 83 examples (+1, zero failures); `demo/showcase.py` (all 5 phases, exit 0).

**M48 -- General Live Property Exposure -- is now fully complete, single phase.** The first, concrete piece of the user's larger customization request; M49 (theme as a YAML cascade tier) and M50 (live re-theme) remain sketched-but-unscoped, each needing its own plan-mode pass before implementation.

**Explicitly out of scope for this milestone, named not silent:** per-side padding/margin (`StyleSpec`/`Node.set_layout` both stay uniform-scalar-only, matching this engine's own existing real scope); flex-grow/shrink/basis, align-items/justify-content (real taffy-level gaps, confirmed still unpopulated from any source, not just YAML); border kwargs on every widget factory beyond `add_rect`; anything from the M49/M50 roadmap (theme-as-YAML, role overrides, typography/shape tokens, live re-theme) -- each explicitly deferred to its own future plan-mode pass, not silently folded into this one.

## Milestone 49 — Theme as a YAML File: Color Overrides + Per-Kind Default Styles (§7.1, §16.3)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 4 phases.** User-directed: "Yes scope M49," following M48's own writeup naming this as the next roadmap piece. Scoped via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`).

**Real investigation that materially reshaped the scope, via a dedicated Explore agent, before any design was locked in:** the open question was whether "theme YAML" could reach the *real* MD3 component catalog (`Window.add_button`/`add_fab`/`add_chip`/etc., `crates/engine-py/src/window_factory.rs`, confirmed 7,581 lines, 56 `add_*` factories) or only the narrow declarative `kind:` surface (`engine-spec`, 7 node kinds: `Rect`/`Text`/`Container`/`Checkbox`/`Slider`/`TextField`/`Image`). Corner-radius/elevation turned out to be a real dead end for this milestone: every one of the 56 factories' own values is either a Rust `const` (`CARD_CORNER_RADIUS`, `DIALOG_ELEVATION`, `FAB_REST_ELEVATION_LEVEL`, ~40 more) or derived from a required Python constructor argument (`add_button`'s own `height`) -- never theme-driven, confirmed via direct grep, not assumed. Migrating all 56 factories to a theme-driven shape/elevation scale is real, large, explicitly out-of-scope follow-up (named below). **Color was not a dead end -- it already flowed through one shared choke point, for both paths:** `window_factory.rs`'s `resolve_*_colors` helpers all resolve roles via `ThemeState::role` (`window.rs`), which delegates directly to `ColorScheme::role` (`color.rs`, the same one large 49-role match). The declarative path (`engine_spec::build::resolve_color`) resolves MD3 tokens against a `ColorScheme` too -- a *differently*-constructed one (`View::new`'s own local `DynamicTheme::from_seed` call), but the identical underlying `role()` lookup. A single new override layer, applied once wherever a `ColorScheme` is selected (two real call sites: `Window.set_theme`, `View::new`), reaches the *entire* real component catalog for free, not just the 7 declarative kinds -- a materially better outcome than the plan's own original "theme YAML only affects Rect/Container" framing.

### Phase 1 — Color Role Overrides, Reaching the Whole Catalog ✅
- Step 1: `ColorScheme::set_role`/`apply_overrides` — ✅ (`crates/engine-md3/src/color.rs`: `set_role(&mut self, name: &str, color: Color) -> bool` mirrors the existing `role()` getter's own 49-arm match, assigning instead of returning, `false` for an unrecognized name. `apply_overrides(&mut self, overrides: &HashMap<String, String>) -> Result<(), String>` parses each raw value with the same hex/CSS `peniko::color::parse_color` parser `engine_spec::build::resolve_color`/`engine-py::view::parse_background_color` already use, returning a plain `Result<_, String>` (not `PyResult`) matching `parse_background_color`'s own established GIL-free precedent -- this crate has no `pyo3` dependency at all. Fails loudly on the first unknown role or unparseable color, matching `SpecError`'s own "fail loudly at the boundary" convention. 5 new unit tests: known-role override leaves every other role untouched, unknown role returns `false`/changes nothing, hex-and-CSS-named colors both parse and apply, an unknown-role error names the role, an unparseable-color error names the value.)
- Step 2: wired into `Window.set_theme`/`View::new` — ✅ (`crates/engine-py/src/window.rs`: `set_theme` gained `custom_theme: Option<String>` (a `ThemeSpec` YAML path); after `DynamicTheme::from_seed`, applies `custom_theme`'s `colors:` to *both* `light`/`dark` before storing into `ThemeState` -- every existing `resolve_*_colors` call site across `window_factory.rs` needed zero changes, since they already go through `ThemeState::role`. If `custom_theme` names its own `seed:`, it overrides the (required) `seed` argument -- explained directly in the doc comment as the only way to express "defer to the theme file" when the argument itself can't be omitted the way `View`'s own optional `theme_seed` can. `crates/engine-py/src/view.rs`: `View::new` gained the same `custom_theme` (plus `default_theme`, Phase 4) applied to its own locally-constructed `scheme`, with the *opposite*-direction precedence deliberately: an explicit `theme_seed` argument wins over either theme file's own `seed:`, since `theme_seed` being *optional* there is itself the "defer to the file" signal `Window.set_theme`'s required `seed` can't give.)

### Phase 2 — `StyleSpec` Parity Gap + Shape/Elevation Reference Constants ✅
- Step 1: `StyleSpec.elevation` — ✅ (`crates/engine-spec/src/spec.rs`: gained `elevation: Option<f32>` -- `PaintProperties.elevation` existed since day one but was never exposed to YAML at all, the identical real gap class `border_width`/`border_color` were in before M48. Wired into `node_kind_and_paint` (`build.rs`, the same "universal, applied once after the match" shape M48's own border fix established) and `cascade.rs`'s `merge()`.)
- Step 2: `engine_md3::shape` — ✅ (new module, `crates/engine-md3/src/shape.rs`: real, named `pub const` values for MD3's published corner-radius scale (`SHAPE_NONE`/`_EXTRA_SMALL`/`_SMALL`/`_MEDIUM`/`_LARGE`/`_EXTRA_LARGE` -- `full` deliberately omitted as a constant, since real MD3 "full" shape is relative to an element's own height, not a fixed dp value, and inventing a placeholder number for it would misrepresent what it means) and elevation scale (`ELEVATION_LEVEL_0`..`_5`) -- replacing ~40 scattered, individually-"verified against Material Web's own token source" doc-comment-only mentions of these same real values across `window_factory.rs` with one real, testable source of truth. **Data only, this milestone** -- `window_factory.rs`'s own existing constants are untouched; migrating them to reference these instead is real, separate, explicitly out-of-scope follow-up (below). 2 new unit tests: both scales are strictly increasing (catches a typo'd/reordered value); cross-checked directly against `window_factory.rs`'s own already-shipped real values (`CARD_CORNER_RADIUS`, `DIALOG_CORNER_RADIUS`, `CHIP_CORNER_RADIUS`, `DIALOG_ELEVATION`), not re-derived from memory.)

### Phase 3 — Theme YAML Format + Layered Stylesheet Cascade (Declarative Surface) ✅
- Step 1: `ThemeSpec`/`parse_theme` — ✅ (new module, `crates/engine-spec/src/theme.rs`: `{seed: Option<String>, dark: Option<bool>, colors: HashMap<String, String>, styles: Vec<StyleRule>}`, `deny_unknown_fields`, reusing `StyleRule` (`cascade.rs`) verbatim for `styles:` -- the identical `kind:`-only-recommended shape `Stylesheet` already uses. 3 new parse tests.)
- Step 2: `resolve_style_layered` — ✅ (`crates/engine-spec/src/cascade.rs`: `resolve_style` split into a private `resolve_style_within_sheet` (today's existing baseline/kind/classes/id merge loop, *without* the widget's own inline `style:`) plus itself (now just that helper + one final inline merge) -- byte-for-byte the same external behavior/signature, confirmed by the full pre-existing test suite re-passing unmodified. New `pub fn resolve_style_layered(spec, default_theme, custom_theme, sheet) -> StyleSpec` resolves *each* present layer through its own independent within-sheet cascade, then merges the three layer results in source-priority order, inline merged last -- the real, deliberate design matching the user's own stated mental model exactly ("default theme, then custom theme, then widget wide styles, then in line style," each tier *entirely* superseding the tier below it regardless of any tier's own internal selector specificity), **not** the same thing as concatenating all three sheets' rules into one combined list and re-running a single cascade over it (that would let a default theme's `id:` rule beat an app's own `baseline` stylesheet rule, backwards from the user's own description). 6 new unit tests: a layer entirely supersedes the layer below regardless of selector specificity (the real claim above, proven directly); custom theme supersedes default theme; inline still wins over every layer; a field unset by every higher layer still resolves from the default theme; no layers at all falls back to the widget's own inline style; the full pre-existing 5-test `resolve_style` suite re-run unmodified.)
- Step 3: threaded through `build_tree`/`patch_node`/`load_styled_view`/`Reconciler` — ✅ (`crates/engine-spec/src/build.rs`: `build_tree`/`patch_node`/`load_styled_view` each gained two new leading `Option<&Stylesheet>` params (`default_theme`, `custom_theme`), resolved via `resolve_style_layered` instead of the plain single-sheet `resolve_style` -- real signature changes, `#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` added where the count crossed clippy's default threshold. `crates/engine-spec/src/reconcile.rs`: `Reconciler::load`/`reconcile` and the private `reconcile_node` thread the same two new params through every real call site (mirrors exactly how `sheet`/`scheme` were already threaded). All existing call sites across `build.rs`'s own tests, `reconcile.rs`'s own tests, and `engine-py`'s `component.rs`/`view.rs` updated to the new signatures -- `Component` passes `None, None` for both (it never had stylesheet/scheme support either, matching its existing, unchanged scope).)

### Phase 4 — Python API + Shipped Default Theme + Example ✅
- Step 1: `View.__new__(default_theme=, custom_theme=)` — ✅ (`crates/engine-py/src/view.rs`: two new optional YAML-path params. `default_theme` omitted loads the engine's own shipped default (`SHIPPED_DEFAULT_THEME_YAML`, `include_str!`-embedded at compile time from `crates/engine-py/assets/default_theme.yaml` -- never a runtime filesystem lookup relative to the installed package, so it can never go missing/stale relative to the compiled extension) -- the user's own stated "a yaml style file loaded as the default." A caller's own `default_theme` path *replaces* the shipped one entirely, not merged with it; `custom_theme` is the usual way to override just a few things while keeping the rest of whichever default is active. `View` gained `default_theme`/`custom_theme: Option<Stylesheet>` fields (the already-converted `styles:` half of each parsed theme, mirroring `stylesheet`'s own "remembered for every future reconcile" precedent) so `poll_reload` never re-parses the original theme YAML on every hot-reload.)
- Step 2: shipped `default_theme.yaml` — ✅ (`crates/engine-py/assets/default_theme.yaml`: real per-kind default styles for the 7 declarative node kinds, corner-radius values drawn from Phase 2's own real shape scale, not invented ad hoc -- `Rect`/`Container`: 0 (`SHAPE_NONE`), `Checkbox`: 2, `Slider`: 8 (`SHAPE_SMALL`), `TextField`: 4. `colors:` deliberately empty -- the seed-derived `ColorScheme` already supplies every real role; this file only supplies per-kind *styles*.)
- Step 3: `Window.set_theme(custom_theme=)` — ✅ (Phase 1 Step 2, cross-referenced here since it's this same Python-API phase's own deliverable -- confirmed, per Phase 1's own investigation, that `Window`'s imperative catalog needs no `default_theme` equivalent at all: its corner-radius/elevation never consult any theme regardless, named explicitly rather than added for false symmetry.)
- Step 4: docs/example — ✅ (`python/tre/_core.pyi` updated: `View.__init__`'s widened signature, `Window.set_theme`'s widened signature, both with real doc comments explaining the seed-precedence asymmetry. New `examples/theme_customization.py` + 3 YAML fixtures (`theme_customization_view.yaml`/`_sheet.yaml`/`_custom_theme.yaml`): three checkboxes prove all four real cascade tiers side by side (shipped-default-superseded-by-custom-theme-superseded-by-app-stylesheet-superseded-by-inline), and a `colors:` override is proven to reach both a declarative `background: primary` `Rect` and a real imperative `add_button`, through a genuine 60-frame `App.run()`.)

**Verification:** 15 new pytest tests (`tests/test_theme.py`) across every phase above, including a real regression test that `poll_reload` re-resolves against the *same* theme after a hot-reload rather than silently falling back to the shipped default (the actual reason `Reconciler`/`View` thread the two new layers through explicitly on every reconcile call, not just at construction time). Full chain green: `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`/`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo test --workspace --release` (`engine-md3` 19, up from 14, +5; `engine-spec` 60, up from 52, +8; every other suite unchanged); `maturin develop --release`; `pytest tests/` (663 passed, up from 648, +15, 1 skipped unchanged); all 84 examples (+1, zero failures); `demo/showcase.py` (all 5 phases, exit 0) -- confirmed the shipped default theme's new corner-radius values don't disturb any pre-existing test that already sets `corner_radius` explicitly (every such test's own inline value already wins the cascade, by design).

**M49 -- Theme as a YAML File -- is now fully complete, all 4 phases.** The second concrete piece of the user's larger customization request. *(Note added 2026-09-21: the corner-radius/elevation follow-up named below as out of scope was picked up immediately afterward as M50, per the user's own explicit reprioritization -- see that milestone's own section.)*

**Explicitly out of scope for this milestone, named not silent:** migrating any of `window_factory.rs`'s 56 `add_*` factories' hardcoded corner-radius/elevation constants to consult Phase 2's new shape/elevation scale -- real, separate, large follow-up work, confirmed genuinely necessary by this milestone's own investigation (colors already flow through a shared mechanism; shape/elevation do not, for any component). Typography theming (font family/weight/size stay per-node literals in `TextSpec`, untouched). Token-reference substitution inside `StyleSpec` (e.g. writing `corner_radius: small` and having it resolve against a theme's own shape scale by name) -- `StyleSpec.corner_radius`/`elevation` stay plain literal numbers in this milestone; the shape/elevation scale is real, named reference data an author consults when choosing those literals, not yet substitutable syntax. M50, live re-theme.

## Milestone 50 — Theme-Driven Shape & Elevation for the Imperative MD3 Catalog (§5, §7, §7.1)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 5 phases.** User-directed: "Scope the corner-radius/elevation to use the new theme pattern. I would like the theme backend complete before re-theming everything else" -- explicitly reprioritizing this ahead of M51/live re-theme (M49's own originally-scoped "up next"). Scoped via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`).

**Real investigation before designing, via a dedicated Explore agent, exhaustive rather than sampled:** every one of the 56 `add_*` factories in `window_factory.rs` (7,581 lines) was individually classified. **28 of 56 have a real, themeable corner radius; 14 of those 28 also have a real, themeable elevation; the other 28 have neither** (custom-painted `NodeKind`s with no `Rect` shape at all -- Checkbox/Slider/Switch/Icon/Image/Progress/Terminal/etc. -- or deliberately flat/square MD3 rows -- menu item, list row, divider, status bar). Only `add_fab` (size: small/default/large) and `add_toolbar` (docked/floating) genuinely pick between multiple corner-radius values by variant; every other themeable component uses one fixed value regardless of its own color variant. Two factories (`add_split_button`, `add_button_group`) have a *second*, genuinely distinct corner-radius concept -- an interaction-driven "tightened" shape on hover/press, not the same thing as their own rest-state radius.

### Phase 1 — Mechanism ✅
- Step 1: `ThemeSpec.components` / `ComponentOverride` — ✅ (`crates/engine-spec/src/theme.rs`: new `ComponentOverride { corner_radius: Option<f64>, elevation: Option<f64> }` plus `ThemeSpec.components: HashMap<String, ComponentOverride>` -- a deliberately separate namespace from `styles:` (which only ever reaches the 7 declarative `NodeKindSpec` kinds), keyed by `"<component>"` (applies regardless of variant) or `"<component>.<variant>"` (overrides just one). 3 new parse tests.)
- Step 2: `ThemeState.components` / `shape()` / `elevation()` — ✅ (`crates/engine-py/src/window.rs`: real 2-tier, **per-field** lookup -- variant-specific key checked first, bare key second, `None` if neither has *that specific field* set. **Real bug caught and fixed before any factory used it, by a dedicated unit test, not by inspection:** an early draft checked "does a variant-specific entry exist at all" before falling through to the bare key -- meaning a variant entry setting only `elevation` would incorrectly block the bare key's own `corner_radius` from ever being found. Fixed to look up each field independently. Wired into `Window.set_theme`'s existing `custom_theme` parsing (temporarily `#[allow(dead_code)]` until Phase 2 gave it a real caller, removed once Phase 2 landed). 5 new unit tests, GIL-free (`ThemeState`/`ComponentOverride` are plain Rust): no-override-returns-none, bare-key-read, variant-beats-bare, the shadowing bug above, elevation mirrors shape's own precedence.)

### Phase 2 — Buttons & FAB Family ✅
- Step 1: `add_button`, `add_icon_button`, `add_fab`, `add_extended_fab`, `add_segmented_button`, `add_toolbar` — ✅ (`crates/engine-py/src/window_factory.rs`: `resolve_button_colors` gained a `component: &str` parameter so its own elevation lookup can use a different key per real caller (`"button"` for `add_button`, `"icon_button"` for `add_icon_button`) even though both share the same color-resolution logic; corner radius resolved inline at each call site, since it depends on a caller-supplied dimension the shared resolver has no access to. `add_fab`'s corner radius is keyed by *size* (small/default/large -- a real, fixed per-variant choice, not a continuous formula); its elevation is keyed with no variant at all, since real MD3 anatomy makes FAB elevation flat regardless of size or color.)
- Step 2: `add_split_button`, `add_button_group` — ✅ (**A real, load-bearing bug caught and fixed before it shipped, not by inspection alone:** both factories' own hover/press shape-morph code used to recompute `height / 2.0` as a fresh, independent literal for their `interactive_shape`/`press_interactive_shape` pairs -- completely bypassing whatever `add_button` itself had just resolved for `paint.corner_radius` moments earlier. A themed button's own painted *shape* would have silently disagreed with its own `corner_radius` field the moment a theme overrode `"button"`. Fixed by resolving the theme override *once* (recomputing the identical value `add_button` itself already used internally -- same key, same variant, same height, guaranteed to agree, not read back from the tree, which would reach the same number more roundaboutly) and reusing it for both the rest-state radius and the shape-morph geometry. The "tightened" hover/press radius gets its own, genuinely distinct `"split_button.tightened"`/`"button_group.tightened"` key -- no equivalent concept exists in plain `add_button` to inherit it from.)
- Verification: 12 new pytest tests -- variant-specific override + no-leak-to-other-variants, bare-key-applies-to-every-variant, `icon_button` has its own key distinct from `button` (including the MD3-internal `"text"` variant-name translation for `"standard"`), FAB's size-keyed corner vs. variant-less elevation, `extended_fab`'s own distinct key, `split_button`'s leading button inheriting the `button` key, both `.tightened` keys not raising (no Python-facing readback for `interactive_shape`/`corner_radii_override`), and a full unthemed-defaults-preserved sweep across all 8 factories.

### Phase 3 — Containers & Surfaces ✅
- Step 1: `add_chip`, `add_card`, `add_tooltip`, `add_dialog`, `add_snackbar`, `add_popover`, `add_side_sheet`, `add_navigation_drawer`, `add_search_bar`, `add_search_view` — ✅ (`resolve_card_colors` gained the same `component`-driven elevation-lookup widening `resolve_button_colors` established in Phase 2. `add_side_sheet`/`add_navigation_drawer` apply their override through `corner_radii_override` (per-corner rounding), not the plain `corner_radius` field -- no Python-facing readback for that value, confirmed and tested as a "does not raise" case. Every factory that only *coincidentally* shares a Rust `const` with an unrelated factory today (`add_search_view` reusing `DIALOG_CORNER_RADIUS`, `add_navigation_drawer` reusing `SIDE_SHEET_CORNER_RADIUS`) still gets its own distinct theme key, per this milestone's own established "shares a number today, not a reason to couple overrides" convention (`crates/engine-spec/src/theme.rs`'s own doc comment).)
- Verification: 13 new pytest tests -- variant-specific card elevation + bare-key corner radius, chip's own no-elevation-concept, `dialog`/`popover`/`search_view` each proven to have their own key distinct from whichever const they coincidentally reuse, `side_sheet`/`navigation_drawer` elevation keyed by modal/standard variant, `navigation_drawer`'s own separate `.indicator` sub-key, and a full unthemed-defaults sweep. **A real test-authoring mistake caught by actually running the tests, not shipped:** an initial test asserted `add_dialog`'s own return value carried the themed `corner_radius`/`elevation` directly -- but `add_dialog` returns the *scrim* node (always a literal `0.0`, a full-window backdrop), not the themed panel, which is one of its children and never returned to Python at all; fixed to a "does not raise" test, matching the same real limit `add_segmented_button`/`add_side_sheet` already established.

### Phase 4 — Navigation, Data Entry & Misc ✅
- Step 1: `add_badge`, `add_navigation_rail`, `add_top_app_bar`, `add_tabs`, `add_date_picker_day`, `add_time_input_field`, `add_period_selector`, `add_spin_box`, `add_pagination`, `add_graph_node` — ✅ (the final 10 of the 28 themeable factories. `add_badge`'s own dot/labeled variants each get their own sub-key (both are real, fixed, height-based constants, not width-dependent, confirmed by direct read of `add_badge`'s own real formula before writing this). `add_navigation_rail`/`add_tabs`' own outer frame/row stay deliberately un-themed -- real MD3 anatomy uses "corner-none" there on purpose, the same "a deliberate design choice, not a missing value" reasoning already established for these two; only their own real per-item indicator constants get a key. `add_top_app_bar`/`add_spin_box`'s own icon-button-shaped sub-elements deliberately *reuse* the `"icon_button"` key from Phase 2 rather than inventing a third, redundant one for the identical real shape.)
- Verification: 11 new pytest tests, plus a full unthemed-defaults sweep across all 10.

### Phase 5 — Shipped Defaults, Docs, Example ✅
- Step 1: shipped `default_theme.yaml`'s `components:` section — ✅ (`crates/engine-py/assets/default_theme.yaml`: every safe, fixed default value copied byte-for-byte from `window_factory.rs`'s own real `const`s, verified by direct read immediately before writing each one, not recalled from memory or the earlier investigation's own prose summary. **A real correctness constraint identified and honored, not glossed over:** components whose real default is a *formula over a caller-supplied dimension* -- `add_button`/`add_icon_button`/`add_segmented_button`/`add_toolbar`'s own corner radius, `add_button_group`'s own tightened shape -- were deliberately given **no** entry in the shipped file at all. Baking in one fixed number for these would have silently overridden their real "scales with the caller's own height/size" behavior for every call that doesn't happen to match whatever number was chosen -- a real, silent behavior change, not a neutral default. Omitting them is what correctly preserves the existing formula (`ThemeState::shape` returns `None`, so each factory's own real fallback computation still runs, exactly as before this milestone).)
- Step 2: `Window.set_theme(default_theme=)` — ✅ (**a real completeness gap found and closed while populating the shipped file, not originally scoped for this phase:** `Window.set_theme` had no way to auto-load *any* default theme at all -- only `custom_theme` -- which meant the `components:` section Step 1 had just populated would have been entirely dead data for the whole imperative catalog, unreachable by any real code path (confirmed directly: `View` already auto-loads the shipped default for its own `styles:` cascade, but nothing analogous existed for `Window`). Fixed by giving `Window.set_theme` its own `default_theme` parameter, mirroring `View.__new__`'s identical convention (omit for the engine's own shipped default; a real path replaces it entirely, not merged with it) -- deliberately scoped to `components:` only, never `colors:`/`seed:`, since `Window`'s color/seed story is already fully served by the required `seed` argument plus `custom_theme`'s own override, and letting a second theme file quietly compete with a required argument would be a real, confusing ambiguity, stated directly in the doc comment rather than left implicit.)
- Step 3: docs/example — ✅ (`python/tre/_core.pyi` updated: `Window.set_theme`'s widened signature and a real docstring explaining the live-vs-construction-time distinction for colors vs. shape/elevation; `View.__init__`'s docstring noting `components:` is silently unused there, since `View` has no imperative factories. Extended `examples/theme_customization.py` + its own custom-theme YAML fixture with a real `components: {button.filled: {corner_radius: 4, elevation: 2}}` entry, asserted directly on a real `add_button` node -- no new Python API needed for the override itself, reusing the identical `custom_theme` parameter M49 already built.)

**Verification:** full chain green at every phase boundary, not just at the end: `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`/`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo test --workspace --release` (`engine-py` 20, up from 11, +9 -- 5 new `ThemeState` lookup tests this milestone, `engine-spec` 63, up from 60, +3; every `engine-core`/`engine-render` suite unchanged, confirming no rendering/layout logic was touched at all); `maturin develop --release`; `pytest tests/` (702 passed, up from 663, +39, 1 skipped unchanged); all 84 examples (one extended, zero new files, zero failures); `demo/showcase.py` (all 5 phases, exit 0) -- re-run in full after every phase, and again after Phase 5 populated the shipped defaults specifically, to confirm the real, load-bearing claim the whole milestone's safety rested on: zero behavior change for any pre-existing example or test that never sets a `components:` override.

**M50 -- Theme-Driven Shape & Elevation for the Imperative MD3 Catalog -- is now fully complete, all 5 phases.** Completes the theme backend across the entire real MD3 catalog, not just the 7 declarative node kinds -- both color (M49) and now shape/elevation (M50) reach every themeable component through the same real `custom_theme`/`default_theme` parameters. M51 (renumbered from the prior "M50" slot -- live re-theme) is next, per the user's own stated ordering, and is now more valuable to build since there's real theme-tier shape/elevation data to re-resolve, not just colors.

**Explicitly out of scope for this milestone, named not silent:** the 28 factories with no real shape/elevation concept at all (unchanged from the investigation's own classification). Typography theming (unchanged since M49). Token-reference substitution inside `StyleSpec`/`components:` (unchanged since M49 -- values stay plain literal numbers, `engine_md3::shape`'s own constants are reference data an author consults, not yet substitutable syntax). M51, live re-theme.

## Milestone 51 — Live Re-Theme for the Declarative Surface (`View.set_theme`) (§7.1, §16.2, §16.3)

**Status: ✅ Complete — single phase.** User-directed: "Scope M51," following M50's own writeup renumbering this as the next roadmap item once the theme backend (color + shape/elevation) was complete across the whole MD3 catalog. Scoped via a formal plan (`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`).

**Real investigation before designing confirmed this milestone is a genuinely small, well-bounded addition, not a new subsystem:** `crates/engine-spec/src/reconcile.rs`'s `Reconciler` already retains the full parsed `WidgetSpec` (`self.spec`) and every widget `id`'s `NodeId` (`self.ids`) for the *lifetime* of a `View`, purely to support hot-reload. `crates/engine-spec/src/build.rs`'s `patch_node` already does exactly "recompute this node's `PaintProperties`/`layout_style` from its spec + the active theme layers, overwrite in place, leave `id`/`parent`/`children`/`access`/`interaction` untouched" -- the *entire* mechanism a live re-theme needs for one node, already built, already tested, already the real engine behind every existing hot-reload. `reconcile_node` only *skips* `patch_node` when the spec is unchanged (a real fast path for the common hot-reload case, where most nodes usually didn't change) -- live re-theme is the opposite case: the spec is always unchanged, but the *theme* changed, so `patch_node` needs to run unconditionally for every node instead. **Explicitly investigated and deliberately not attempted, named not silent:** live re-theme for `Window`'s own imperative catalog -- `Window.add_button`/etc. retain no spec at all once built, so there is nothing analogous to unconditionally re-run; a real fix would need a new per-node "how was this constructed" theme-derivation tracking mechanism, a genuinely separate, large piece of work -- the same "live token-linkage" concept flagged as the highest-risk/highest-value piece of the whole customization request since the very first investigation in this session, long before M49 existed. `Window.set_theme`'s own existing narrow 4-field color re-tint (`Tree::set_all_interaction_tints`/`set_all_component_tints`) is unchanged by this milestone.

### Phase 1 — `resolve_theme_layers`, `Reconciler::retheme`, `View.set_theme` ✅
- Step 1: `resolve_theme_layers` — ✅ (`crates/engine-py/src/view.rs`: `View::new`'s own inline ~35-line theme-resolution block -- parses `default_theme`/`custom_theme` YAML or the shipped default, converts `styles:` to a `Stylesheet`, resolves seed precedence, applies `colors:` overrides -- factored into a new private `fn resolve_theme_layers(default_theme, custom_theme, theme_seed, dark) -> PyResult<(Stylesheet, Option<Stylesheet>, Option<ColorScheme>)>` once a genuine second real call site (`View.set_theme`, Step 3) needed the identical logic -- the same "two real call sites justify factoring out" precedent `resolve_button_colors`/`fill_scrollbar_thumb` already established in earlier milestones. `View::new` itself is behavior-identical, confirmed by the full pre-existing `test_theme.py` suite re-passing unmodified.)
- Step 2: `Reconciler::retheme` — ✅ (`crates/engine-spec/src/reconcile.rs`: `pub fn retheme(&self, tree: &mut Tree, default_theme: Option<&Stylesheet>, custom_theme: Option<&Stylesheet>, sheet: Option<&Stylesheet>, scheme: Option<&ColorScheme>, base_dir: Option<&Path>) -> Result<(), SpecError>`, walking `self.spec` via a new private `retheme_node` recursive helper (mirroring `record_ids`'s own recursive-walk shape) and calling `patch_node` *unconditionally* for every node via `self.ids[spec.id]` -- no `node_props_equal` check at all, since the spec is guaranteed unchanged (a theme change, not a content change). Zero changes needed to `patch_node`/`build_tree` themselves. `&self`, not `&mut self` -- reads `self.spec`/`self.ids`, only mutates the passed-in `Tree`. 2 new unit tests, GIL-free: a `corner_radius` change from a new theme layer is applied to every matching node; a node with its own inline style is untouched (inline still wins over the new theme, exactly like at construction time).)
- Step 3: `View.set_theme` — ✅ (`crates/engine-py/src/view.rs`: `#[pyo3(signature = (default_theme=None, custom_theme=None, theme_seed=None, dark=false))] fn set_theme(...)`, calling `resolve_theme_layers` then `self.reconciler.retheme(...)`, then updating `self.default_theme`/`self.custom_theme`/`self.scheme` so a *later* `poll_reload()` continues resolving against the newly-installed theme rather than silently reverting to whatever `View::new` originally used. **Real, deliberate convention, matching `Window.set_theme`'s own already-shipped precedent, not invented fresh here:** each call is a complete, fresh theme selection -- omitting `default_theme`/`custom_theme` resets to the shipped default / no custom override, not "keep whatever the previous call used." A caller who only wants to change the seed re-passes the same theme path(s) it already has in hand.)
- Step 4: docs/example — ✅ (`python/tre/_core.pyi` updated: new `View.set_theme` stub, matching `Window.set_theme`'s own doc-comment conventions, naming the real "only the static cascade is recomputed, a bound field reverts to its spec's own static value" limit directly. Extended `examples/theme_customization.py` + a new `theme_customization_retheme.yaml` fixture: after the window is already showing, `view.set_theme(custom_theme=...)` swaps in a second custom theme live, and `theme_checkbox` (no stylesheet rule or inline override of its own) picks up the new `corner_radius: 18` on the exact same, already-built node, while `stylesheet_checkbox`/`inline_checkbox` are unaffected -- the app's own stylesheet and each widget's own inline style still win, exactly like at construction time.)

**Verification:** 7 new pytest tests (`tests/test_theme.py`): `set_theme(custom_theme=...)` changes an already-built node's `corner_radius` live, read back via the exact same `Node` object the view returned before the call (proving in-place patching, not a rebuild); a widget's own inline style still wins over the new theme; omitting `default_theme`/`custom_theme` on a later call resets to the shipped default rather than being a silent no-op; `set_theme(theme_seed=...)` changes a declarative `background: primary` token without raising (the same honest "no Python-facing color getter" limit every other color test in this suite already states); node ids survive a retheme (both a pre-held `Node` and a fresh lookup read the same live value); a `poll_reload()` called *after* `set_theme()` continues resolving against the newly-installed theme, not the original one; and a **real, verified-not-assumed finding, caught by actually running the test rather than by inspection:** a `{{ }}`-bound property does *not* survive a `retheme()` call -- `patch_node` only recomputes the static style cascade (the identical, pre-existing behavior any content-only `poll_reload` already has today, not a new limitation this milestone introduces), so a bound field reverts to its spec's own static value, proven here as "reverts sanely to a real value," not "crashes" or "goes stale." Full chain green: `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`/`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo test --workspace --release` (`engine-spec` 65, up from 63, +2; every other suite unchanged -- no `engine-core`/`engine-render`/`engine-md3` logic touched at all); `maturin develop --release`; `pytest tests/` (709 passed, up from 702, +7, 1 skipped unchanged); all 84 examples (zero new files, one extended, zero failures); `demo/showcase.py` (all 5 phases, exit 0).

**M51 -- Live Re-Theme for the Declarative Surface -- is now fully complete, single phase.** Completes the "theme as a YAML file" roadmap for the declarative `View` surface end to end: construction-time resolution (M49/M50) plus genuine live re-resolution (M51), all through the same `default_theme`/`custom_theme`/`theme_seed` parameters. Live re-theme for `Window`'s own imperative catalog remains explicitly out of scope, named below -- the "live token-linkage" mechanism it would need is real, separate, large follow-up work, not yet scoped.

**Explicitly out of scope for this milestone, named not silent:** live re-theme for `Window`'s imperative MD3 catalog (`add_button`/etc.) -- would need a new per-node "how was this constructed" theme-derivation tracking mechanism, confirmed genuinely necessary and genuinely large by this milestone's own investigation; `Window.set_theme`'s own existing narrow 4-field color re-tint is unchanged. Re-applying `{{ }}` bindings after a `retheme()` call -- `patch_node` recomputes only the static cascade, identical to any content-only `poll_reload` today; verified, not silently assumed safe, by a dedicated test.
